# DEFINITIONS

## SECTION A

| TERM | DEFINITION |
|---|---|
| **Balancing Market** | As defined in the Electricity Market Rules. |
| **Real-Time Balancing Market** | As defined in the Electricity Market Rules. |
| **Electricity Market** | The set of arrangements between Contracting Parties within the Contractual Framework of the Electricity Market Rules for the wholesale purchase and sale of energy products (electricity and capacity). The Electricity Market is governed by the Electricity Market Rules. |
| **License** | The license issued in accordance with the Law. |
| **Distribution License** | The License granted to the Distribution System Owner in accordance with Article 26 of the Law. |
| **Transmission License** | The License granted to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) in accordance with Article 26 of the Law. |
| **Licensed Very Small Scale Generating Station** | A Generating Station, other than Renewable Energy Source Stations, with a Generating Capacity greater than or equal to 1 MW, but less than 5 MW. |
| **Reactive Energy** | The electrical reactive energy generated, flowing, or supplied by an electrical circuit during a time interval which is the integral over time of the instantaneous Reactive Power, and is measured in VArh units or standard multiples thereof. |
| **Reactive Power** | The product of the Voltage, the current, and the sine of the phase angle between them, expressed in VAr units and standard multiples thereof.<br>In the applications of the definition in the Transmission Rules, Reactive Power shall mean lagging (Lagging VAr) when referring to injection and leading (Leading VAr) when referring to absorption. |
| **Wind Farm** | An Electricity Generating Station comprising wind turbine system installations, for the purpose of converting kinetic wind energy into electrical energy. |
| **Requesting Safety Coordinator** | The Safety Coordinator who requests the Safety Precautions. |
| **Extreme Electrical System Conditions** | A condition resulting either after the declaration of an Emergency State by the TSOC, or pursuant to the provisions of the Electricity Market Rules regarding the term of the same name. Under Extreme Electrical System Conditions, the Contingency Reserve is activated. |
| **Renewable Energy Source (RES)** | As defined in Article 2 of the Law. |
| **Independent Aggregator** | As defined in the Electricity Market Rules. |
| **De-energisation** | Disconnection of Facilities and/or Equipment from the Transmission System using circuit breakers. |
| **Direct Line** | As defined in Article 2 of the Law. |
| **Direct Connection to the Transmission System** | The direct connection of Users to the Transmission System via KYEA, KYEK or KYEP. |

---
*Version 1.1.0 | Index of Terms and Definitions | Page 8*

<!-- PAGE 9 -->

**Version 1.1.0 Index of Terms and Definitions**  
**Page 9**

| Term / Defined Term | Definition |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Electricity Storage** | The deferral of the final use of electrical energy to a moment in time later than when it was generated, or the conversion of electrical energy into a form of energy that can be stored, the storing of such energy, and the subsequent reconversion of such energy into electrical energy or its use in a different energy carrier.<br>The operation of Electricity Storage includes the distinct phases of: (a) Energy Injection, and (b) Energy Absorption. |
| **Power System Restoration** | The restoration of the Power System or part of the Power System to a normal operating state following a condition of Partial Shutdown or Total Shutdown. |
| **Demand Response** | As defined in the Electricity Market Rules. |
| **Remote Transmission Facilities** | Any Facilities and Apparatus or Meters belonging to the Transmission System Owner, which:<br>(a) are embedded in the User's System and are not directly connected to the substation belonging to the Transmission System Owner via Facilities and/or Apparatus belonging to the Transmission System Owner;<br>(b) operate under the direction and control of the User following a relevant agreement between the Transmission System Owner and the respective User. |
| **Isolation** | The disconnection of Facilities and/or Apparatus from the remainder of the Transmission System, the Distribution System, or the User System, using circuit switches and/or disconnectors, in accordance with T12.1.4.2.2. |
| **Desynchronisation** | The process by which a Synchronised Generating Unit or another Synchronised System is disconnected from the Transmission System, the Distribution System, or the User System. |
| **CERA Decision or CERA Regulatory Decision** | As defined in Article 2 of the Law. |
| **Contingency / Unexpected Incident** | The unexpected failure or loss of an element of the Power System, such as a generator, transmission line, circuit breaker, switch, or other electrical element. |
| **Electricity Authority of Cyprus (EAC)** | The Electricity Authority of Cyprus. |
| **Safety from the System** | The condition which safeguards persons when performing specific work on the Transmission System, the Distribution System, or a User's System from the inherent hazards of the Transmission System, the Distribution System, or a User's System. |
| **Automatic Under-Frequency Load Shedding (UFLS)** | The automatic disconnection of Consumer circuits when the Frequency value falls below a specified threshold. |
| **Automatic Over-Voltage Demand Disconnection** | The automatic disconnection of circuits when the Voltage value increases or the rate of Voltage rise exceeds acceptable limits, as specified by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC). |
| **Automatic Under-Voltage Demand Disconnection** | The automatic disconnection of circuits when the Voltage value decreases or the rate of Voltage drop exceeds acceptable limits, as specified by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC). |

<!-- PAGE 10 -->

Version 1.1.0 | Index of Terms and Definitions  
Page 10

| TERM | DEFINITION |
|---|---|
| **Automatic Demand Reconnection After Frequency Restoration** | A system for reconnecting Consumers automatically following a Disturbance Event (as defined in T6) and after the restoration of the Target Frequency. |
| **Automatic Frequency Restoration Reserve (aFRR)** | The portion of the FRR that is generally activated automatically (without prior Dispatch Instruction from TSOC), as defined in the Electricity Market Rules. |
| **Automatic Generation Control (AGC)** | As defined in the Electricity Market Rules. |
| **Automatic Voltage Regulator (AVR)** | The system that automatically regulates the input / output Voltage of a User, maintaining it constant at a target value. The relevant User obligations are specified in T1.7. |

### B

| TERM | DEFINITION |
|---|---|
| **Meter Database** | The system that uniquely identifies the Meter and the Users associated with the Meter, and contains data related to the Meter, as determined by the Electricity Market Rules. |
| **Generating Unit Auxiliaries** | Any element of a Producer's Facilities and/or Machinery that is not a direct part of the boiler installation or the Generating Unit, but is required for the operation of the boiler facilities or the Generating Unit. The term "Auxiliary" shall be construed accordingly. |
| **Power Station Auxiliary Facilities** | Any element of a Power Station's Facilities and/or Machinery that is not a direct part of the boiler installation or the Generating Units, but is required for the operation of the boiler facilities or the Generating Units. |
| **Auxiliary Load** | The electricity Demand of all Generating Unit Auxiliaries or Power Station Auxiliary Facilities required for the operation of the Generating Units. |

### C

| TERM | DEFINITION |
|---|---|
| **Earthing** | Method of establishing a connection between conductors and earth via an Earthing Device. |

### D

| TERM | DEFINITION |
|---|---|
| **Operational Data** | Data required pursuant to the Transmission Rules and the Electricity Market Rules, relating to scheduled or planned actions concerning the operation of the Transmission System or the User System. |
| **Metering Data** | As defined in the Electricity Market Rules. That is, the quantities of Energy measured and recorded by a Meter. |
| **System Planning Data** | Data that must be submitted by all Users at regular intervals, or other relevant data or information as specified by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), in accordance with the Planning Rules. |

<!-- PAGE 11 -->

| Version 1.1.0 | Index of Terms and Definitions | Page 11 |
|:---|:---|:---|

| Term | Definition |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Capacity Adequacy Index** | An indicator issued by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) for each weekly peak of the year based on Availability and Demand forecasts regarding whether or not sufficient resources exist to meet Demand. |
| **Ten-Year Transmission System Development Plan** | The Transmission System development plan prepared by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) in accordance with the provisions of Article 63 of the Law and T2. This plan covers a period of ten calendar years. |
| **System Operations Log** | The chronologically logged messages related to safety coordination that have been sent and received by each Safety Coordinator. |
| **Binding Project Design Data** | Data related to the User Development Project when the offer for a Connection or System Use Agreement and/or supplementary agreements are accepted. |
| **Declared Maximum Expected Power Off-take** | The Maximum Expected Power Off-take notified to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) in accordance with the provisions of the Transmission Rules, and specifically according to T8.2. |
| **Meter Verification Statement** | The statement of comparison between the cumulative energy recorded in the Meter Register and the energy sum calculated from the Metering Data. |
| **Declaration of Change of Operational Characteristics** | A notification prepared by the User and submitted to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) in accordance with the provisions of T8.3 that updates the Registered Operational Characteristics values of a Generation / Demand Entity when these change. A relevant Unavailability Declaration releases the User from the requirement to submit this notification for a temporary change in the Registered Operational Characteristics of the corresponding Generation / Demand Entity. |
| **Unavailability Declaration** | A notification prepared by a User and submitted to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) concerning the Availability of a Generation/Demand Entity for energy generation/injection or off-take. It is submitted by Producers with Conventional Generation Units, Storage Facility Operators or Storage Representatives as applicable, as well as RES and CHP Producers with a Generation Capacity exceeding 1 MW operating outside National Support Schemes.<br><br>The Unavailability Declaration is submitted in accordance with the relevant provisions of the Trading and Settlement Rules (TSR) and includes:<br>a) Its start time<br>b) Its estimated duration<br>c) The estimated change in the Availability of the Generation/Demand Entity for energy generation/injection or off-take<br>d) The description of the reasons for unavailability |
| **Operating Diagrams** | Diagrams constituting a schematic representation of High or Medium Voltage Equipment as applicable and the connections of all external circuits at a Connection Site, including numbering, nomenclature, and labeling. |

<!-- PAGE 12 -->

**Index of Terms and Definitions**  
*Page 12 | Version 1.1.0*

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Dispute Resolution Procedure** | The procedures described in T11, in the Connection Agreement, in the Use of System Agreement, and in agreements for the provision of Ancillary Services, which relate to the resolution of disputes. |
| **Integrated Scheduling Process** | The generation scheduling process defined in the Electricity Market Rules. |
| **Transmission System Connection Procedure** | A document prepared by the TSOC and approved by CERA based on Regulatory Decision 02/2022, describing the procedure for connecting Users to the Transmission System. The approved document is published on the TSOC website. |
| **Available Energy (Storage System)** | The Active Energy (in MWh) stored in a Storage System at any given time. |
| **Available Input Power** | The Active Power that can be generated at any given time by a RES Unit based on the available primary energy source (e.g., solar irradiance, wind speed). |
| **Availability** | The capability of a Generation / Demand Entity, transmission line, or other facility to supply or absorb Energy or to provide Ancillary Services to the Electrical System in accordance with its Registered Operating Characteristics and technical parameters. The terms "Available" and "Availabilities" shall be construed accordingly. |
| **Voltage Disturbance** | The condition where the Transmission System Voltage falls outside the normal operating range, as defined in T1.8.3.1. |
| **Maintenance Ban Period** | The time period specified by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus during which a User is not permitted to schedule maintenance of their Facilities. |
| **Transmission System Use Charges** | Charges established by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus and subject to approval by CERA for the use of the Transmission System. |
| **Storage Facility Operator** | As defined in the Electricity Market Rules. |
| **Distribution System Operator (DSO)** | The Electricity Authority of Cyprus (EAC), acting as the Distribution System Owner and operating as the Distribution System Operator, responsible, inter alia, for the planning, development, operation, and maintenance of the Distribution System. |
| **Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC)** | The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus, as defined in Article 2 of the Law. |
| **Distributed Generation or Distributed Generation Units** | As defined in the Distribution Code. |
| **Trial Operation** | The status assigned to a Generation / Demand Entity during Testing or Operational Inspections. |
| **Operational Tests of Equipment Connected to the Transmission System** | A test mutually agreed upon under a contract that must not be unreasonably delayed, conducted in accordance with the Transmission Code. |
| **System Tests** | Tests involving the simulation of conditions or the controlled application of abnormal, unusual, or extreme conditions of the Transmission System, the Distribution System, or the User System, or a specific part thereof, but excluding Control or Re-testing and Equipment Acceptance Tests or any other smaller-scale test. |

<!-- PAGE 13 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **Index of Terms and Definitions** | **Page 13**

| TERM | DEFINITION |
|---|---|
| **Test** | The Test carried out by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and/or the User. |
| **Equipment Testing and Commissioning Test** | The Test carried out on equipment connected to the Transmission System or the Distribution System for the first time, or following a fault or modification, or for the purpose of testing or verifying the equipment's characteristics or settings. |
| **Black Start Test** | A Test carried out by a User with a Black Start Unit, in accordance with the instructions of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), to demonstrate that the Black Start Unit has the capability to provide Black Start Service. |

### E

| TERM | DEFINITION |
|---|---|
| **Facilities** | Fixed and mobile devices used for the generation and/or injection and/or transmission and/or absorption and/or consumption of electricity, excluding Machinery. |
| **Electricity Storage Facility** | As defined in the Electricity Market Rules or the Transitional Electricity Market Regulations, whichever is in force. |
| **Electricity Storage Facility with Integrated RES** | As defined in the Electricity Market Rules or the Transitional Electricity Market Regulations, whichever is in force. |
| **Electricity Storage Facility without Integrated RES** | As defined in the Electricity Market Rules or the Transitional Electricity Market Regulations, whichever is in force. |
| **Generation Facilities** | The Power Station comprising any Generating Units. |
| **Switching / Operation Facilities** | The premises, buildings, and equipment used for the purposes of monitoring, testing, and operating the User's Facilities and Machinery. |
| **Approved Generating Unit Maintenance Schedule (AGUMS)** | The effective Maintenance Schedule for Generating Units pursuant to T4. |
| **Approved Transmission System Maintenance Schedule (ATSMS)** | The effective Maintenance Schedule for the Transmission System pursuant to T4. |
| **Meters and Metering Manual** | As defined in the Electricity Market Rules. |
| **National Energy Control Center (NECC)** | The National Energy Control Center of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), for the operation of the Electrical System. |
| **Special Generation Schedule** | The Generation scheduling of a Unit, an Electricity Storage Facility, or a portfolio of Electricity Storage Facilities operating under Trial Operation status. |
| **Forecast Report** | The forecast report as defined in Article 2 of the Law. |
| **Start-up** | The procedure bringing a Generating Unit from Shutdown State to the Start-up Completion Point, where it becomes fully available for Power Injection Instructions from TSOC. |
| **Storage Representative** | As defined in the Electricity Market Rules. |

<!-- PAGE 14 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **Glossary of Terms and Definitions** | **Page 14**

| Term | Definition |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Demand Response Representative** | As defined in the Electricity Market Rules. |
| **Load Representative** | As defined in the Electricity Market Rules. |
| **Estimated Reading** | As defined in the Electricity Market Rules. |
| **Out-of-Scope Contingency Event** | The simultaneous occurrence of multiple Contingency Events without a common cause or the loss of Generating Units with a total loss of generating capacity exceeding the Reference Incident. |
| **Minimum Energy Availability of an Electricity Storage Facility** | The minimum quantity of Energy that can be stored in an Electricity Storage Facility or aggregated across a portfolio of Electricity Storage Facilities, measured in MWh. |
| **Minimum Generation** | The minimum Active Power Output that a Generating Unit can produce continuously, as registered by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) pursuant to T2.A4. |
| **Minimum STEP Load** | The lower limit of the STEP Control Range. |
| **Minimum Down Time** | The minimum time that must elapse from the moment a Generating Unit is disconnected from the Power System – following an Instruction to be placed in an Out-of-Service State – until the next execution of a Startup Instruction. |
| **Minimum Run Time After Startup** | The minimum time that must elapse from the completion of the Generating Unit Startup procedure until the reduction of generation to a level below Minimum Generation – following an Instruction to be placed in an Out-of-Service State. |
| **Equipment Testing and Commissioning** | Activities comprising the execution of the Equipment Testing and Commissioning Test or the implementation of Equipment Testing and Commissioning Instructions pursuant to the terms of the Connection Agreement or, as required by the applicable framework, the testing of any User equipment components required under the Transmission Code prior to connection or reconnection to ensure compliance with all requirements and specifications for connection to the Transmission System or the Distribution System. It also includes activities establishing new values for User equipment parameters resulting from hardware changes or modifications, and additionally, those activities comprising the execution of Equipment Testing and Commissioning Tests or the implementation of Equipment Testing and Commissioning Instructions, as required by the applicable framework. |
| **Frequency Control** | The procedure by which operation within acceptable Frequency limits is achieved, both during normal operation and during disturbances, at any given moment, in accordance with the provisions of T5.3. Frequency Control is achieved through the management of the Operational Margin by the TSOC. |
| **Indirect Connection to the Transmission System** | The connection of Users to the Transmission System, with a requested installed capacity above the "Capacity Limit for Connection to the Transmission System", via Distribution System infrastructure, i.e., via Distribution System electrical lines and a Transmission Substation. |

<!-- PAGE 15 -->

| Version 1.1.0 | Index of Terms and Definitions | Page 15 |
|---|---|---|

| Term | Definition |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Good Electricity Utility Practice** | The standards, practices, methods, and procedures that comply with safety requirements and legal requirements, and which are achieved by exercising that degree of skill, diligence, prudence, and foresight that would reasonably and ordinarily be expected from skilled and experienced technicians who are capable of performing such types of work under the same or similar conditions. |
| **Indicative Generating Unit Maintenance Schedule (IGUMS)** | The Indicative Maintenance Schedule of Generating Units in accordance with T4. |
| **Indicative Transmission System Maintenance Schedule (ITSMS)** | The Indicative Maintenance Schedule of the Transmission System in accordance with T4. |
| **Energy** | Active and/or Reactive Energy, as specified by the text in each instance. |
| **Active Energy** | Electrical energy produced, flowing, or supplied by an electrical circuit over a period of time, calculated as the integral of instantaneous Active Power with respect to time, and measured in Watt-hours (Wh) or standard multiples thereof. |
| **Active Power** | The product of voltage and the in-phase component of alternating current, measured in units of Watts (W) or standard multiples thereof. |
| **Generating Unit Active Power Output** | The Active Power produced by a Generating Unit, excluding the power supplied to the Generating Unit Auxiliary Load. |
| **Unified Action Plan for Power System Restoration after Blackout** | The plan prepared and updated by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) that sets out the instructions for all parties involved in Power System Restoration following a Total or Partial Blackout, in order to achieve Power System Restoration with the maximum possible safety and in the shortest practically achievable time. |
| **Operational Instructions** | Operational instructions and management procedures supporting the Safety Regulations and local and remote operation of Facilities and Machinery, issued in connection with the actual operation of Facilities and/or Machinery at or from the Connection Site. |
| **Instruction** | Any Instruction issued by the TSOC to a User. Instructions are issued in accordance with T15. |
| **Secured Connection** | A Secured Connection ensures that the N-1 Criterion is satisfied. |
| **Equipment** | A Machine or set of devices or Machines, or the set of main devices of an Installation, or all devices required to perform a specific function relating to the Generation, injection, transformation, storage, Transmission, Distribution, or use of electrical energy. |
| **Switching Equipment** | Equipment featuring a mechanical switching device capable of making and breaking the rated current, as well as making and breaking overcurrents based on specified limits and endurance conditions. |

<!-- PAGE 16 -->

| **Version 1.1.0** | **Glossary of Terms and Definitions** | **Page 16** |
|:---|:---|:---|

| Term | Definition |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Authorized Representative** | The representative who has been duly authorized by the User or by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) to handle matters related to the Transmission Rules on behalf of the User or the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus, as applicable. |
| **Authorized Operator** | The person designated by the User as responsible for the control of the Facilities and Plant associated with the User System. |
| **Black Start / Restart after Total Blackout** | The procedure for restarting the Power System following a Total Blackout or Partial Blackout, using the Black Start Service. |
| **Power Adequacy** | The condition where sufficient resources exist in the Power System to meet Demand requirements and the Operating Margin. |
| **Operationally Affected Users** | Users who are affected by a specific Operational Control in terms of Availability, continuity, and quality of supply. |
| **Target Frequency** | The Frequency determined by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) pursuant to T1.8, as the target operating Frequency of the Transmission System. |
| **Update of Operational Characteristics by TSOC** | An update of a User's Registered Operational Characteristics performed with due justification by TSOC. This update is carried out in cases of non-compliance or whenever otherwise deemed necessary, and is notified to the relevant User. The new values of Registered Operational Characteristics remain valid until the submission of a Declaration of Change of Operational Characteristics by the relevant User. |
| **Ancillary Service** | As defined in the Law. For the purposes of the Transmission Rules, Ancillary Service is any service additional to the provision of electrical capacity, the supply of electrical energy, and the transmission and distribution of such energy. Ancillary Services are necessary for the proper operation of a stable and reliable Power System. Ancillary Services ensure the response capability of the Power System both under normal operating conditions and during disturbances. |
| **Business Day** | The days from Monday to Friday, excluding national public holidays and holidays specified by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC). |
| **User Development Project** | Means the User Facilities and/or Plant that are to be connected to the Transmission or Distribution System, or a specific Modification related to User Facilities and/or Plant already connected to the Transmission or Distribution System, or a proposed new connection or Modification of the connection within the User System. |
| **Investigation** | An investigation conducted by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) in accordance with T11. |
| **STEP Control Range** | The range of loads to which the Special Automatic Load Shedding Scheme / Tap Control System (STEP) can be applied. |

<!-- PAGE 17 -->

**Version 1.1.0**  
**Index of Terms and Definitions**  
**Page 17**

| TERM | DEFINITION |
|---|---|
| **European Network of Transmission System Operators for Electricity (ENTSO-E)** | The European Network of Transmission System Operators for Electricity (ENTSO-E), which represents all Transmission System Operators for Electricity in the European Union and others connected to their networks, for all regions, and for all technical and market issues that concern them. |
| **Implementing Security Coordinator** | The Security Coordinator who applies Preventive Security Measures within a designated operational limit. |
| **Replacement Reserve (RR)** | The total Active Power reserves (spinning or non-spinning) available to restore any Demand shedding and to restore or support the required level of Operating Reserve, in accordance with the provisions of T5.3. |
| **Frequency Restoration Reserve (FRR)** | The total Active Power reserves available for regulating the Power System Frequency under normal operating conditions and for restoring the Power System Frequency to the Target Frequency following a Disturbance Event, in accordance with the provisions of T5.3. Frequency Restoration Reserve can be upward or downward and is distinguished into automatic (aFRR) and manual (mFRR), according to its mode of activation. |
| **Contingency Reserve (CR)** | The additional available Generation capacity ensuring that Demand and Ancillary Services requirements are met under Extreme Power System Conditions, in accordance with the provisions of T5.3. Contingency Reserve is provided by Contracted Units pursuant to the provisions of the Electricity Market Rules, particularly Chapter 5 and Annex 5.3 thereof. |
| **Frequency Containment Reserve (FCR)** | The total Active Power reserves available to contain the Frequency following the occurrence of a power balance imbalance, in accordance with the provisions of T5.3. As Frequency may vary in an upward or downward direction, upward and downward Frequency Containment Reserve is defined in each case. Frequency Containment Reserve is provided both during normal operation and during a Disturbance Event. |
| **Fast Frequency Response (FFR)** | The total Active Power reserves available in accordance with the provisions of T5.3. |
| **Backup Protection** | The Protection system that actuates Switching Equipment when the main Protection of another Protection system fails to operate for the same or other elements of the Power System. |

### Z

| TERM | DEFINITION |
|---|---|
| **Demand** | Electricity Demand in Active Power, unless stated otherwise. |

### H

| TERM | DEFINITION |
|---|---|
| **Power System** | The Transmission System, the Distribution System, and all User Systems within the Republic of Cyprus. |

<!-- PAGE 18 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **Index of Terms and Definitions** | **Page 18**

---

| TERM | DEFINITION |
|---|---|
| **Electronic Alarm Transmission System** | The primary means by which an Alarm is transmitted by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) to Users (or to certain Users only). |
| **Solar Thermal Station** | A Power Generating Station that utilizes direct solar radiation, converting it into thermal energy and subsequently into electrical energy. |
| **Trading Day** | As defined in the Electricity Market Rules. |
| **Operational Date** | When the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) is satisfied that all Operational Tests of the Equipment Connected to the Transmission System have been properly performed and satisfactorily completed, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall notify the User accordingly, specifying the time and date of such completion. |
| **Completion Date of Works** | The date on which the User is expected to connect or begin using the Transmission System. |
| **Connection Date** | The date on which the Equipment Control and Acceptance Instructions have been duly implemented, to the satisfaction of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), regarding each part of the User's equipment. Subsequently, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall notify the User as soon as possible, specifying the date of completion of this implementation. |
| **Modification Date** | When, following a Modification, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) is satisfied that all Tests under the Transmission Rules have been properly executed and satisfactorily completed, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall notify the User as soon as possible, specifying the time and date of such completion. |

---

### Θ

| TERM | DEFINITION |
|---|---|
| **Summer Time** | The time as derived during the time period between the last Sunday of March until the last Sunday of October. |
| **Thermal State of a Power Generating Unit (Hot, Warm, Cold)** | Is the thermal state of a Power Generating Unit (hot, warm, or cold).<br><br>A Power Generating Unit that was in the warm or cold Thermal State of a Power Generating Unit is considered to transition to the hot Thermal State of a Power Generating Unit upon completion of the Start-up process.<br><br>From the moment of desynchronization of the Power Generating Unit and provided that the unit remains in an out-of-service state, upon the passage of the Transition Time to Warm / Cold Thermal State, the unit transitions to the warm and subsequently to the cold Thermal State of a Power Generating Unit. |

### Ι

| TERM | DEFINITION |
|---|---|
| **Distribution System Owner** | As defined in Article 2 of the Law. |
| **Transmission System Owner** | The owner of the assets of the Transmission System in accordance with Article 2 of the Law. |

<!-- PAGE 19 -->

**Edition 1.1.0** | **Glossary of Terms and Definitions** | **Page 19**

| TERM | DEFINITION |
|---|---|
| **Generation Capacity** | The nominal maximum Active Power flow from a Generation Unit that can be continuously provided to the Transmission System or to the Distribution System, as applicable, at the Connection Point. Generation Capacity does not change due to any temporary changes such as faults or seasonal variations and, consequently, does not change with Unavailability Declarations. |
| **Loading Capacity** | The continuous loading capacity of electrical equipment expressed in MW or MVA, as applicable. |
| **K** | |
| **TERM** | **DEFINITION** |
| **Designated Metering Point** | The actual location at which the general accuracy requirements, as referred to in T13, are met. The locations of the Metering points are fully specified in the relevant Connection Agreements. |
| **Good Utility Practice** | Any of the practices, methods, and actions that should be adopted at the appropriate time by a given person, exercising the degree of knowledge, skill, diligence, prudence, and foresight reasonably and ordinarily expected from an experienced and expert person who is required to perform the same or similar work under the same or similar conditions. Good Utility Practice is not limited to the optimal practice method or action to the exclusion of all others, but constitutes the spectrum of all acceptable practical methods and actions applied under various circumstances. |
| **Electricity Market Rules** | As defined in Article 2 of the Law. |
| **Distribution Rules** | As defined in Article 2 of the Law. |
| **Transmission Rules** | As defined in Article 2 of the Law. |
| **Safety Regulations** | The regulations of EAC or a User, compliance with which ensures that persons working on the Facilities and/or Plant to which the rules apply are protected from hazards arising from the Transmission System, the Distribution System, or a User's System. |
| **Transitional Electricity Market Arrangement Regulations** | Is the operating code of the wholesale market during the transitional arrangement, issued pursuant to CERA Regulatory Decision 04/2017. |
| **Site Operating Regulations** | Refers to the site operating regulations in T12. |
| **Contingency List** | The list of Contingencies that must be simulated to verify compliance with the Operational Security Limits.<br>TSOC is responsible for drafting and revising the Contingency List. |
| **Consumer** | Is the "final customer" as defined in the Law. |
| **Autogenerating Consumer** | A Consumer who generates electricity for their own use. |
| **Priority Supply Consumers** | Consumers who are generally exempt from load shedding procedures and have priority for restoration in the event of load shedding. |
| **Dispatchable Generation Unit (DGU)** | A Generation Unit capable of receiving and executing all Instructions of TSOC at any time and for any Generation level from Minimum Generation to Maximum Continuous Load. |

<!-- PAGE 20 -->

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Dispatch** | The issuance of Dispatch Instructions by TSOC to Users regarding the operation of the Generation/Demand Entities they represent, within the framework of the Balancing Market, the provision of other Ancillary Services, and the safe operation of the Electrical System. The term "Dispatchable" and other similar terms shall be interpreted accordingly. |
| **Emergency State** | An Emergency State is declared by TSOC or the Transmission System Owner when deemed necessary. Additionally, a system state may be characterized as an Emergency State ex-post by TSOC. Depending on the case, CERA, the Transmission System Owner, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus, the Distribution System Operator, and the Distribution System Owner are immediately informed.<br><br>An Emergency State may constitute any abnormal system condition requiring automatic or immediate manual action to prevent or limit the loss of transmission facilities or generation supply that would adversely affect the operational reliability of the Electrical System. It includes, without being limited to, conditions where:<br>(i) there is a severe malfunction of the Transmission System or the Distribution System, or<br>(ii) there is a Partial or Total Shutdown of the Electrical System, or<br>(iii) a deficit in Power Adequacy is forecasted, or<br>(iv) the safety of the Electrical System, persons, devices, or Facilities is threatened. |
| **Out-of-Service State** | The state of a Generation/Demand Entity during which it is Desynchronized and/or not operating. |
| **Frequency Sensitive Mode (FSM)** | The operating mode of a Generation/Demand Entity during which the generated/injected/absorbed Active Power varies due to a change in the Frequency of the Electrical System, so as to contribute to the restoration of the Target Frequency. |
| **Limited Frequency Sensitive Mode — Overfrequency (LFSM-O)** | The operating mode of a Generation/Demand Entity during which a variation in the generated/injected/absorbed Active Power is triggered due to a change in the Frequency of the Electrical System above a specified value. |
| **Limited Frequency Sensitive Mode — Underfrequency (LFSM-U)** | The operating mode of a Generation/Demand Entity during which a variation in the generated/injected/absorbed Active Power is triggered due to a change in the Frequency of the Electrical System below a specified value. |
| **N State** | The state of the Electrical System in which no Transmission Element is Unavailable due to the occurrence of a Contingency (Unforeseen Event). |
| **N-1 State** | The state of the Electrical System where one or more Transmission Elements are Unavailable due to the occurrence of one (1) Contingency (Unforeseen Event). |
| **Registered Data** | The items of the Preliminary Project Design Data and the Binding Project Design Data that are finalized upon connection (which may be subject to any relevant future changes). |

---
Version 1.1.0 | Index of Terms and Definitions | Page 20

<!-- PAGE 21 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **Index of Terms and Definitions** | **Page 21**

| Defined Term | Definition |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Registered Operating Characteristics** | The total Operating Characteristics of a Generation / Demand Entity included in the Registered Data and notified to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) in accordance with the provisions of the Transmission Rules. Registered Operating Characteristics are updated by Users in accordance with the relevant clauses of the Transmission Rules. |
| **Registered Information** | For Generation / Demand Entities, includes the following information:<br>(a) Registered Data of the Generation / Demand Entity.<br>(b) Identity of the representative of the Generation / Demand Entity.<br>(c) Identity of the Meter or Meters recording the input / output of the Generation / Demand Entity.<br>(d) Node where each such Meter is electrically located (or the corresponding nearest Node).<br><br>For the Distribution System Operator (DSO), Registered Information includes the following:<br>(a) Identification of the Meter and Node of each Connection Point between the Distribution System and the Transmission System.<br>(b) Identification of the Meters of Distributed Generation connected to the Distribution System.<br>(c) Identification of the Meters of Consumers connected to the Distribution System but not supplied by the Distribution System Operator. |
| **Registered Fuels** | The fuels of a Generation Unit that the unit is capable of using, which are registered in accordance with the Planning Code of the Transmission Rules and the provisions of the Market Operating Rules (Electricity Market Rules). |
| **Meter Register** | The register located inside each Meter which records cumulative energy for each measured quantity. |
| **Central Electricity Storage Facility Substation (CSESF)** | The private HV/MV substation of the Electricity Storage Facility Operator, located on its Facility Site, which contains the Connection Point to the Transmission System or the Distribution System and includes only the necessary Facilities and Equipment of the Electricity Storage Facility Operator's system. The CSESF does not constitute and cannot constitute part of the Transmission System or the Distribution System, and its Isolation must under no circumstances affect the operation of the Transmission System or the Distribution System. The Isolation of the CSESF must be possible both from the side of the Transmission System or Distribution System and from the side of the Facility Site. Expansion/reinforcement of the Transmission System or Distribution System via the CSESF is not applicable. |

<!-- PAGE 22 -->

| Version 1.1.0 | Index of Terms and Definitions | Page 22 |
| :--- | :--- |

| Term | Definition |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Central Consumer Premises Substation (CCPS)** | The private HV/MV substation of the Consumer, located at their Premises, which contains the Connection Point to the Transmission System or the Distribution System and includes only the necessary Facilities and Equipment of the Consumer System. The CCPS does not constitute nor may it constitute part of the Transmission System or the Distribution System, and its Isolation must under no circumstances affect the operation of the Transmission System or the Distribution System. Its Isolation must be possible both from the side of the Transmission System or Distribution System and from the side of the Premises. The expansion/reinforcement of the Transmission System or Distribution System through the CCPS is not applicable. |
| **Central Producer Premises Substation (CPPS)** | The private HV/MV substation of the Producer, located at their Production Site, which contains the Connection Point to the Transmission System or the Distribution System and includes only the necessary Facilities and Equipment of the Producer System. The CPPS does not constitute nor may it constitute part of the Transmission System or the Distribution System, and its Isolation must under no circumstances affect the operation of the Transmission System or the Distribution System. Its Isolation must be possible both from the side of the Transmission System or Distribution System and from the side of the Production Site. The expansion/reinforcement of the Transmission System or Distribution System through the CPPS is not applicable. |
| **Distribution System Control Center** | The Energy Control Center of the Distribution System Operator, central or local, as notified by the Distribution System Operator to Users at regular intervals. |
| **Key Box** | A box for the secure keeping of keys, which features two separate locks. |
| **Safety Key** | The unique key at a Location capable of controlling a lock, which will cause the Locking of an Isolation Device and/or an Earthing Device. |
| **Key Box Key** | The unique key at a Location capable of operating only one of the two locks of the Key Box. |
| **Locking** | The application of a locking device which ensures the Locking of a Machine/Equipment item. |
| **Locked** | The state of a Machine/Equipment item which cannot be altered without the operation of a locking device. |
| **Common Site Location Plans** | The drawings prepared for each Connection Site incorporating the spatial plans of the Connection Site, the preliminary electrical diagrams, the common protection/control plans, and the common service plans. |
| **Red Alert** | The Alert issued by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) to Users under the circumstances specified in T10. |
| **Node** | The busbar at the terminal end of a branch of the Transmission System. |
| **Derating / Restriction** | A total or partial reduction in the Availability of a User. |
| **Transmission System Planning Criteria** | System Planning practices and considerations followed by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC). |

<!-- PAGE 23 -->

| TERM | DEFINITION |
|---|---|
| **N-1 Criterion** | The rule according to which Transmission Elements remaining in operation within the control area of TSOC following the occurrence of a Contingency can serve the new operating state without violating the Operational Security Limits. |
| **Main Meter** | The Meter which, based on the settlement period, constitutes the primary choice for the relevant data. By default, this is the Main Meter. |

### L

| TERM | DEFINITION |
|---|---|
| **Operation** | A scheduled or planned action related to the operation of the Transmission System, the Distribution System, or a User System under normal conditions or Emergency States (including Distributed Generation in the Distribution System). |
| **Operating Characteristics** | The technical capabilities, adaptability, and limitations for the operation of a Generation / Demand Entity, as registered or declared, in accordance with the terms of the Transmission Rules and the Electricity Market Rules. |
| **Operating Procedures** | Management orders and procedures supporting the Safety Regulations and the local and remote operation of Facilities and Plant/Apparatus, issued in relation to the actual operation of Facilities and/or Plant/Apparatus at or from a Connection Site. |
| **Operational Effect** | Any effect from the operation of any other relevant system that causes the operation of the Transmission System, the Distribution System, or the User System (or operation under increased risk) in a manner different from that in which it could have operated or would normally operate had that event not occurred. The term “Operationally Affected” shall be interpreted accordingly. |
| **Operating Reserve** | The portion of the Operating Margin that includes the Fast Frequency Response Reserve (FFR), the Frequency Containment Reserve (FCR), and the Frequency Restoration Reserve (FRR). |
| **Operating Margin** | The available amount of Active Power for reserve after covering the expected Demand of the Electricity System, pursuant to T5.3. It includes the Operating Reserve, the Replacement Reserve (RR), and the Contingency Reserve. |
| **Operational Tests** | Tests performed by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) to maintain and develop operating procedures, train personnel, and obtain information regarding the Transmission System under abnormal operating conditions, or tests performed by other Users for similar purposes regarding their Facilities. |
| **Distribution System Control Engineers** | As defined in the Distribution Rules. |
| **Market Operator** | As defined in the Electricity Market Rules. |
| **Control Engineer / Safety Coordinator** | The Authorised Person under the provisions of the Transmission Rules acting on behalf of a User and responsible for the safety of electrical installations within the User's Area of Responsibility. |

| **Gate Closure Time for the Day-Ahead Market** | As defined in the Electricity Market Rules. |

# M

| TERM | DEFINITION |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Maximum Expected Power Consumption** | The maximum value of average power per Trading Period expected to be absorbed by a Consumer connected to the Transmission System. |
| **Maximum Energy Availability of Electricity Storage Facility (capacity)** | It is the maximum quantity of Energy that can be stored in an Electricity Storage Facility or cumulatively in a portfolio of Electricity Storage Facilities, measured in MWh. |
| **Maximum Absorption Capacity** | The maximum power that can be absorbed by a Consumer or a Demand Response Load portfolio.<br><br>For a Consumer, it is the maximum power that it can absorb from the grid at the Connection Point, as defined in the relevant Connection Agreement.<br><br>For a Demand Response Load portfolio, it is the sum of the Maximum Absorption Capacity of the Demand Response Loads of which the portfolio consists. |
| **Maximum Discharge Capacity of Electricity Storage Facility** | The maximum Active Power that can be continuously injected from an Electricity Storage Facility or a portfolio of Electricity Storage Facilities into the Transmission System or the Distribution System. |
| **Maximum Charge Capacity of Electricity Storage Facility** | The maximum Active Power that can be continuously absorbed by an Electricity Storage Facility or a portfolio of Electricity Storage Facilities from the Transmission System or from the Distribution System. |
| **Maximum Continuous Loading** | The maximum Active Power generation capability, modified according to any constraints (environmental, faults, etc.), that can be produced continuously by the Generation Unit without causing a reduction in equipment lifetime, subtracting the power capacity used to supply the Auxiliary Load. |
| **Maximum AGC Load** | The upper limit of the AGC Control Range. |
| **Partial Blackout** | The condition resulting when all generation has stopped in a part of the Power System and there is no electricity supply from any other point. |
| **Medium Voltage (MV)** | The Voltage used for electricity supply whose root mean square value (nominal value) exceeds 1000 V but does not exceed 36000 V. |
| **Unit Auxiliary Transformer** | A transformer connected directly to the terminals of a Generation Unit and which supplies power to the Generation Unit Auxiliaries. |
| **Generation Unit Transformer** | The transformer whose primary function is to provide interconnection between the Generation Unit and the Power System and to transform the Voltage of the Generation Unit to the Voltage of the Power System. |
| **Energy Import Measurement** | Measurement of Energy flow from the Transmission System or Distribution System towards a User. |

<!-- PAGE 25 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **Index of Terms and Definitions** | **Page 25**

| Term | Definition |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Energy Outflow Measurement** | Measurement of Energy flow from a User to the Transmission or Distribution System. |
| **Meter** | A device of appropriate specifications for measuring and recording electricity units. |
| **Verification Meter** | The Meter which, based on the settlement period, constitutes the second choice for data collection. |
| **Profile Meter** | As defined in the Electricity Market Rules. |
| **Metering Arrangements** | Meters, time switches, measurement transformers (including arrangements for summated combination), equipment for protection and isolation of measurements, circuit components and associated equipment and cabling for data storage and data communication, which form part of the Active Power and Reactive Power metering equipment associated with an Installation Site. |
| **Plant / Apparatus** | An item of equipment in which electrical conductors are used or supported or part of which are electrical conductors and includes meters, lines, and appliances used or intended to be used for the transmission of electricity for the purpose of supplying or using electricity. |
| **RES Unit** | A Generation Unit that generates Energy from a Renewable Energy Source. |
| **RES Unit with Integrated Electricity Storage** | As defined in the Electricity Market Rules or the Regulations for the Transitional Regulation of the Electricity Market, whichever of the two is in force. |
| **Black Start Unit** | The Generation Unit, Electricity Storage Facility, or portfolio of Electricity Storage Facilities that provides Black Start Services, in accordance with the provisions specified in the Electricity Market Rules and the Transmission Code. |
| **Generation Unit** | Unless otherwise specified in the Transmission Code, refers to any Plant / Apparatus that generates electricity. It is clarified that these Units are not identical to Generation Units as defined in the Electricity Market Rules. |
| **Power Park Module (PPM)** | A Generation Unit or set of Generation Units connected to the Power System via power electronics and, additionally, has a single Connection Point to the Power System, including a closed distribution system. |
| **Cogeneration Unit (CHP Unit)** | As defined in the "Promotion of Energy Efficiency in Heating and Cooling and Cogeneration of Electricity and Heat Law of 2006 (174(I)/2006)" and its amendments. |
| **High-Efficiency Cogeneration Unit (HE-CHP Unit)** | A CHP Unit responding to the criteria of Annex III of the "Promotion of Energy Efficiency in Heating and Cooling and Cogeneration of Electricity and Heat Law of 2006 (174(I)/2006)" and its amendments. |
| **Blue Alert** | An Alert issued by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) under the circumstances specified in T10. |

### N

| TERM | DEFINITION |
| :--- | :--- |

<!-- PAGE 26 -->

| **Law** | **The Regulation of the Electricity Market Law of 2021, as amended from time to time.** |
|---|---|

# O

| TERM | DEFINITION |
|---|---|
| **Equipment Testing and Commissioning Instructions** | A step-by-step testing procedure for the Testing and Acceptance of Equipment. |
| **Short-Term Forecast** | The forecast made by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) for each Trading Period of the next three Trading Days, which may refer either to the RES Injection Forecast or to the Demand Forecast. |
| **Total Blackout** | The condition that occurs when the Generating Units and Electricity Storage Facilities and/or their portfolios are in an Out-of-Service State and there is no supply of electricity to the Power System from any source. |
| **Generation / Demand Entity** | A Generating Unit, or Power Park Module, or an Electricity Storage Facility, or a portfolio of Electricity Storage Facilities, or a portfolio of RES Units and/or CHP Units, or a Consumer in the Transmission System, or an AZ Load portfolio. |
| **Maximum Allowable Rate of Change of Frequency** | The maximum acceptable limit of the Rate of Change of Frequency (RoCoF) in the Transmission System under disturbance conditions as defined in T1.8.2. |
| **Operational Security Limits** | The acceptable operating limits for the safe operation of the Transmission System, such as thermal limits, Voltage limits, short-circuit current limits, Frequency limits, and dynamic stability limits, which are specified in T1. |
| **Ownership Boundary** | The boundary between the Distribution System and/or the Transmission System and/or equipment belonging to the User, as applicable. |
| **Capacity Limit for Connection to the Transmission System** | The limit specified following a proposal by the TSOC and DSO and approved by CERA in accordance with the provisions of Regulatory Decision 02/2022, functioning as a separation threshold for initial differentiation of connections between the Transmission System and the Distribution System. |

# P

| TERM | DEFINITION |
|---|---|
| **Generation** | The process of generating electricity. |
| **Small-Scale Generators** | Generators operating Licensed or License-Exempted Very Small Power Generating Stations and Generators operating Small Power Generating Stations, for which the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus considers that the Generator is located in a position that does not make its operation particularly critical for the operation of the Transmission System. |
| **Generator** | The person who produces electricity pursuant to a License or is exempted from it in accordance with the Law, operating according to their capacity as a generator within the Republic of Cyprus. |

**Version 1.1.0** | **Index of Terms and Definitions** | **Page 26**

<!-- PAGE 27 -->

| Version 1.1.0 | Index of Terms and Definitions | Page 27 |
|---|---|

| Term | Definition |
| :--- | :--- |
| **RES and CHP Producer** | A Producer operating RES Units and/or CHP Units. It is clarified that these Producers are not fully identical to RES and CHP Producers as defined in the Electricity Market Rules, as they also include Producers participating in National Support Schemes. |
| **Monitoring** | Monitoring performed by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) in accordance with T11. The term "Monitor" shall be interpreted accordingly. |
| **Balancing Service Provider (BSP)** | As defined in the Electricity Market Rules. |
| **End of Start-Up Period** | The time period after which the Rate of Change of Output Power of the Generating Unit does not depend on the initial operating state of the Generating Unit. |
| **Settlement Period** | As defined in the Electricity Market Rules. |
| **Trading Period** | Half-hour period as defined in the Electricity Market Rules. |
| **Transmission System Constraints** | Physical and operational constraints that exist during the transmission of electrical energy through the Transmission System. |
| **Area of Responsibility** | The area in which the User's Facilities and Equipment are located, as delineated in the relevant Directive of the TSOC / DSO and/or in the Connection Agreement. |
| **Incident** | An unscheduled or unplanned event associated with the Transmission System, the Distribution System, or a specific User System, including faults and interruptions. |
| **Regional Control Centres** | Switching/Operational Facilities used by the Distribution System Operator (DSO) for monitoring and controlling a part (specific area of responsibility) of the Distribution System, as communicated by the Distribution System Operator to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and Users at regular intervals. |
| **Probability of Unplanned Outages** | The probability, expressed as a percentage, of a Facility being unavailable to provide Energy or Ancillary Services. |
| **Warning Notices** | A notice/sign warning against any intervention (e.g., CAUTION WORK IN PROGRESS). |
| **Certified Metering Data** | As defined in the Electricity Market Rules. |
| **Meter Certificate** | The certificate, having a limited period of validity, for Metering Devices issued following the performance of a certification test in accordance with the specifications of the International Electrotechnical Commission (IEC) and European Union Directives on Measuring Instruments, where applicable. |
| **Power Quality** | The desired states of supply power quality and variations in power quality expected at Connection Points. |
| **Policy for Determining the Limiting Rate of Change of Frequency** | The set of methodologies used by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), as well as the relevant procedures for implementing these methodologies, in order to determine the Limiting Rate of Change of Frequency (RoCoF). The Limiting Rate of Change of Frequency is directly related to the total inertia requirements of the Power System. |

<!-- PAGE 28 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **Index of Terms and Definitions** | **Page 28**

| Term | Definition |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Operating Margin Policy** | The set of technical requirements and methodologies used by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), as well as the relevant procedures for implementing these methodologies, in order to ensure the existence of an adequate Operating Margin in the Power System. The Operating Margin Policy is published on the TSOC website. |
| **Charging Policy for Connection to the Transmission System** | A document prepared by TSOC and approved by CERA based on Regulatory Decision 01/2022, which establishes the general principles and guidelines for connection charges to the Transmission System. The approved document is published on the TSOC website. |
| **Orange Alert** | An alert issued by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus to Users under the circumstances specified in T10. |
| **Actual Metering Point** | The actual or physical point at which the Measurement of an electrical quantity is performed. |
| **RES Injection Forecast** | The forecast of injections from RES Units connected to the Transmission System or the Distribution System. The Forecast is prepared in accordance with T3, separately for each RES technology. It is emphasized that it also includes a forecast of injections from RES Units in Trial Operation. |
| **Demand Forecast** | The total energy expected to be consumed by Consumers. This forecast is prepared in accordance with T3. |
| **CHP Generation Schedule** | The schedule of injections from CHP Units connected to the Transmission System or the Distribution System. Submission is made in accordance with T3 and also includes the injection schedule from CHP Units in Trial Operation. |
| **Maintenance Schedule** | The schedule that defines the type, duration, and urgency of all Maintenances of User Facilities or the Transmission System required during a specific timeframe. |
| **Generating Unit Maintenance Schedule** | Maintenance Schedule pertaining to Generating Units. |
| **Transmission System Maintenance Schedule** | Maintenance Schedule pertaining to the Transmission System. |
| **Scheduled Rotational Demand Shedding** | The planned Deactivation of Consumer Facilities on a rotational basis when there is a significant deficit in the required Generation to meet total Demand for an extended period of time. |
| **Scheduled Operation Date** | Has the meaning defined in the Connection Agreement. |
| **Scheduled Maintenance** | The Maintenance of User Facilities or part of the Transmission System or part of the Distribution System carried out according to a defined schedule. |
| **Power Adequacy Shortage Warning** | A warning issued by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus when it estimates, based on Availability and Demand forecasts, that there are insufficient resources to meet Peak Demand. |
| **Non-Compliance Warning** | A warning issued by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus to a User regarding non-compliance with an Instruction. |
| **Day-Ahead Market** | As defined in the Electricity Market Rules. |

<!-- PAGE 29 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **Index of Terms and Definitions** | **Page 29**

| **TERM** | **DEFINITION** |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Preliminary Project Design Data** | Data related to the proposed User Development Project at the time the User submits an application for a Connection and System Use Agreement and/or an additional Agreement, but prior to the acceptance of the offer. |
| **Preventive Safety Measures** | Measures taken preventively for the safe execution of works in accordance with T12. |
| **Supplier** | As defined in the Law. |
| **Protection** | The provisions for detecting abnormal operating conditions of the Transmission System, the Distribution System, or the User System, which cause fault clearing or activation of signals or indications. |
| **Connection Offer or Connection Offer and Terms** | The document constituting the offer of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and/or the Distribution System Operator (where required) for connecting all User Facilities to the Transmission System and/or the Distribution System, containing the terms and connection charges. |
| **Provisional Maintenance Program of Generating Units (PMPGU)** | The Provisional Maintenance Program of Generating Units of the Producer prepared by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) in accordance with T4. |
| **Provisional Maintenance Program of Transmission System (PMPTS)** | The Provisional Maintenance Program of the Transmission System. |
| **User Proposing the Test** | The User who submits a proposal for testing in accordance with T9. |
| **Protocol Between Transmission and Distribution System Operators** | Protocol concluded between the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and the Distribution System Operator pursuant to Article 47 of the Law, dealing with operational coordination matters between the Transmission System and the Distribution System. |
| **Transmission System Protocol** | As defined in Article 2 of the Law. |

### R

| **TERM** | **DEFINITION** |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Consumer Demand Control** | The reduction of electricity supply to a Consumer or the disconnection of a Consumer in a manner agreed for commercial purposes between the Supplier and the Consumer. |
| **Emergency Demand Control** | All or any of the methods by which an increase in Demand or a decrease in Demand is achieved, for reasons of reliability and safety of the Electrical System, as specified in T6. |
| **Voltage Regulation** | Maintaining the Transmission System Voltage or User System Voltage within permissible limits by automatic or manual means. |
| **Speed Governor** | The system that results in the variation of Active Power of a Generating Unit in response to a variation in Frequency, in the direction that assists in restoring the Desired Frequency. |
| **Cyprus Energy Regulatory Authority (CERA)** | As defined in Article 2 of the Law. |

<!-- PAGE 30 -->

**Edition 1.1.0** | **Index of Terms and Definitions** | **Page 30**

---

| **Term** | **Definition** |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Rates of Change / Ramp Rates** | For Generation Units, it is the Power Generation Ramp-Up Rate or Ramp-Down Rate, whereas for Demand Units (Consumers), it refers to the rate of change of power absorption, as applicable and specified. For Energy Storage Facilities, portfolios of Energy Storage Facilities, or Energy Storage Systems, it refers to the transition time from one active power injection/absorption level (in MW) to another, or from standby mode to a specific active power injection/absorption level (and vice versa). |
| **Power Generation Ramp-Up Rate** | The rate of increase of Active Power Output of the Generation Unit following the end of the Start-Up Period. This rate of increase continues until the Generation Unit reaches the power output level specified by the system operator or the Maximum Continuous Rating (MCR) of the Generation Unit. Following the end of the Start-Up Period, the Rate of Change does not depend on the initial operational state of the facilities, but may depend on the Active Power Output of the Generation Unit (MW). |
| **Power Generation Ramp-Down Rate** | The rate of decrease of Active Power Output of the Generation Unit following the end of the Start-Up Period. Ramp-Down Rates pertain to the range of power outputs from the Maximum Continuous Rating (MCR) of the Generation Unit down to Minimum Generation. The Rate of Change does not depend on the initial operational state of the facilities, but may depend on the Active Power Output of the Generation Unit (MW). |
| **De-synchronisation Ramp Rate / Rate of Reduction for Disconnection** | The rate at which a Generation Unit reduces its Active Power Output (MW) from Minimum Generation to zero when an Instruction is issued to cease its generation. |
| **Rate of Change of Frequency (RoCoF)** | The rate of increase or rate of decrease of Frequency, as measured over a specified rolling time period defined in each relevant article of the Transmission Code. |
| **Loading Rate / Run-Up Rate** | Following the pickup of Block Load at Synchronisation and after the elapsed Soak Time (Balancing Time), a Generation Unit will continue to increase its output up to Minimum Generation or the End Point of the Start-Up Period. The rate of increase of Active Power Output of the Generation Unit is not constant but varies during Start-Up. The average rate at which its Active Power Output increases during Start-Up is the Loading Rate. The rate applied to the Generation Unit depends on the operational thermal state (hot, warm, or cold) of the Facility when instructed to connect to the Transmission System. |

<br/>

| **Term** | **Definition** |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Significant System Incidents** | Incidents which had, or possibly had, or are likely to have Operational Effects on the Transmission System, the Distribution System, or the User System. |
| **Metering Point** | Actual or Defined Metering Point. |

| **Version 1.1.0** | **Index of Terms and Definitions** | **Page 31** |
|---|---|---|

| Term | Definition |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Start-up Period End Point** | The level of Active Power Output of a Generating Unit (MW) at which Loading Rates cease to apply. Changes in Active Power Output (MW) of Generating Units, after reaching the Active Power Output level of the Start-up Period End Point, are within the relevant Rates of Change. |
| **Connection Point** | The actual point where Facilities, Equipment, or the User's System connect to the Transmission System or the Distribution System. |
| **Distributed Generation Connection Point in the User System** | The point at which a Generating Unit considered as Distributed Generation is connected to the User System. |
| **Supply Point from the Transmission System** | A supply point from the Transmission System to the Distribution System or to Consumers or to Electricity Storage Facilities connected to the Transmission System. |
| **Severe Disturbance Event** | A Disturbance Event which causes a Frequency deviation exceeding ±0.50 Hz from the Target Frequency. |
| **Short-Term Flicker Severity ($P_{st}$)** | An index of visual flicker severity, which is evaluated over a 10-minute time period. |
| **Long-Term Flicker Severity ($P_{lt}$)** | An index of visual flicker severity, which is evaluated over a 2-hour time period. |
| **Rare Unforeseen Event** | The simultaneous occurrence of multiple Unforeseen Events with a common cause. |
| **Power System Stabilizer** | A device that injects a supplementary signal into the Automatic Voltage Regulator so as to improve the damping of Power System oscillations. |
| **Renewable Energy Sources Station** | Generating Stations operating Generating Units that produce Energy from Renewable Energy Sources. |
| **Power Station** | A facility consisting of one or more Generating Units. |
| **Large Scale Power Station** | A Generating Station with a Generating Capacity greater than or equal to 100 MW. |
| **Medium Scale Power Station** | A Generating Station with a Generating Capacity equal to or greater than 50 MW, but less than 100 MW. |
| **Small Scale Power Station** | A Generating Station (Distributed or not) with a Generating Capacity equal to or greater than 5 MW, but less than 50 MW. |
| **Very Small Scale Power Station** | A Very Small Scale Power Station Exempted from License or a Licensed Very Small Scale Power Station. |
| **Very Small Scale Power Station Exempted from License** | A Generating Station with a Capacity less than or equal to 1 MW or, in the case of Renewable Energy Sources Stations, with a Capacity less than or equal to 5 MW, which secures a Construction/Operation License Exemption from CERA. |
| **Governor Droop** | The percentage drop in Frequency that causes the change in power output from zero to the rated power of a Generating Unit, due to the response of its Speed Governor. (Droop) |
| **Transmission Element** | An electrical device of the Transmission System with terminal ends connected to any other electrical device, such as a transformer, disconnector, power circuit breaker, or busbars. |

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Synchronous Power Generating Module** | A Power Generating Module or an indivisible set of Power generating facilities capable of producing electrical energy such that the frequency of the generated Voltage, the rotational speed of the generator, and the Voltage frequency of the network maintain a constant ratio, and, as a result, are synchronized. |
| **Synchronisation** | The condition under which a Power Generating Module or a system is connected to the Transmission System or to the Distribution System or to a User's System such that the frequency and phase relationships of the Power Generating Module or system, as the case may be, and of the Transmission System or Distribution System or User's System to which it is connected are identical. The term “Synchronised” shall be construed accordingly. |
| **Reference Incident** | The Incident taken into account when determining reserve requirements, and particularly FCR requirements. |
| **Disturbance Incident** | An Incident that causes a Frequency deviation outside the normal operating range as defined in T1.8.2. |
| **Conventional Power Generating Module** | A Power Generating Module that produces energy using conventional energy sources (e.g. fossil fuels). The term Conventional Power Generating Station shall be deemed to have a corresponding meaning. |
| **Operation and Maintenance Agreement** | The bilateral operation and maintenance agreement entered into by mutual consent between a User and EAC or another organization possessing the necessary experience and expertise to carry out maintenance on the User's Facilities and Apparatus, and regularly employs personnel holding the necessary Certificates to perform duties of Control Engineer and Safety Coordinator within the User's Area of Responsibility. |
| **Connection Agreement** | The document to be executed following the acceptance of the Connection Offer, between TSOC, the User, and the DSO where required, containing the terms and Connection charge. |
| **Interface Agreement** | An agreement between a User and the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus, containing the terms for addressing the impacts on Facilities or Apparatus owned or operated by a specified User and located on a site owned by another User and/or for shared facilities and/or the provision of services at or near the Connection Site. |
| **Use of System Agreement** | An agreement between the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus or the Distribution System Operator and a User, as applicable and in accordance with the Law, defining the terms for the transmission of electricity from Producers (Distributed or non-Distributed) and Electricity Storage Facilities through the Transmission System and/or the Distribution System to Consumers. |
| **Contracted Unit** | A Generation/Demand Entity providing Contingency Reserve in accordance with the Electricity Market Rules. |
| **Contingency Reserve Contract** | As defined in the Electricity Market Rules. |

| **High-Efficiency Combined Heat and Power (HE-CHP)** | High-Efficiency Cogeneration as defined in the "Promotion of Energy Efficiency in Heating and Cooling and Combined Heat and Power Law of 2006 (174(I)/2006)" and its amendments. |

| **Load Factor** | The ratio of average load, expressed as a percentage of the peak load generated by the specific Generating Unit over a defined period of time. |
| **Maintenance** | A scheduled or planned action carried out for technical reasons that causes a total or partial reduction in the Availability of a User, such that the Registered Data of that User are altered. Maintenances are scheduled in accordance with section T4. |
| **Safety Coordinator** | The person or persons designated and authorized in writing to be responsible for Safety Precautions at each Connection Point when specific work (which includes testing) is to be performed. This necessitates the provision of Safety Precautions on High, Medium, and/or Low Voltage Apparatus in accordance with the Safety Rules and Distribution System Operating Rules, as applicable. |
| **Isolation Device** | A device used to achieve Isolation. |

Following its construction, the Entry Substation forms part of the Transmission System or Distribution System and may constitute a Connection Point for other Users as well. |

| **Low Voltage or LV** | The Voltage used for electricity supply, the upper root mean square (nominal) value of which does not exceed 1,000 V. |
| **Synchronization Operations** | The coupling (closing the circuit breaker manually or automatically) of two asynchronous systems using a synchronoscope or other equivalent means. |
| **Switching Operation** | An operation, such as changing the state of switching devices / circuit breakers. |

| **Balancing Time (Soaking Time)** | The time period during which the Active Power Output of a Generating Unit must remain constant after its Synchronization with the Power System during the Start-up process, before it begins to increase again. The duration of the balancing time depends on the initial operating state of the Generating Unit upon Synchronization. |
| **Transition Time from Hot to Warm State** | The time (HH:mm) that must elapse from the desynchronization of a Generating Unit (at which point it is in the hot Thermal State) until it transitions to the warm Thermal State of the Generating Unit. |
| **Transition Time from Warm to Cold State** | The time (HH:mm) that must elapse from the moment a Generating Unit enters the warm Thermal State until it transitions to the cold Thermal State of the Generating Unit. |
| **Installation Site** | The premises of the Transmission System Owner, the Distribution System Owner, or the User's premises, as applicable. |
| **Generation Site** | The premises owned by (or used under lease, license, or other contract by) the Generator and containing the Connection Point. |
| **Connection Site** | The area in which the User's Facilities and Equipment, located on the User's side of the Connection Point, are to be installed, including the plot of land and access roads. |

### Manner of Interpretation of References in the Transmission Rules:

i. References to the terms "In Writing" or "Written" include typing, printing, and other modes of reproducing words in a legible and non-transitory form. This includes the reproduction of words or other signals by electronic means, such as email and/or mutually agreed information systems through which information and/or Instructions are exchanged.

ii. When a word is mentioned in the Index of Terms and Definitions that is defined more specifically in a specific chapter of the Transmission Rules, the definition in that specific chapter of the Transmission Rules shall prevail over the definition found in the Index of Terms and Definitions in the event of any conflict.

<!-- PAGE 38 -->

iii. References to another document or part of the Transmission Rules must not impose additional or concurrent obligations or confer additional or concurrent rights at the point in the text containing such a reference.

iv. Nothing contained in the Transmission Rules shall aim to diminish the statutory or licensed obligations of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) or the Distribution System Operator (DSO).

v. Time references pertain to the time and date in Cyprus.
