### T1.A2 INDICATIVE LIST OF EQUIPMENT INCLUDED IN OPERATIONAL DIAGRAMS

#### T1.A2.1 Basic Principles:
*(a)* Where applicable, all High Voltage Equipment at each Connection Site must be included in an Operational Diagram. Provided that the clarity of the diagram is not compromised, its layout should represent as faithfully as possible the geographical layout of the Connection Site.

*(b)* When the creation of more than one Operational Diagram is unavoidable, the repetition of identical information across multiple Operational Diagrams must be avoided.

*(c)* The Operational Diagram must accurately present the current state of the Equipment, for example whether it has been commissioned for operation or not. If not commissioned for operation, the relevant parts shall be designated as "spare".

*(d)* Operational Diagrams shall make provision for the notification of approvals, together with the conditions regarding revision details and dates.

*(e)* Operational Diagrams shall be prepared on A3 size paper or on a paper format agreed upon with the Transmission System Operator.

*(f)* The Operational Diagram shall normally be single-line. Nevertheless, details appearing across all phases must be presented.

---

#### T1.A2.2 EQUIPMENT SHOWN ON OPERATIONAL DIAGRAMS

1. Busbars
2. Circuit Breakers
3. Disconnector and Load-Break Switches
4. Disconnectors (Any automation features)
5. Bypass Facilities
6. Earthing Switches
7. Maintenance Earthing Switches
8. Overhead Line Entrances
9. Overhead Line Terminations
10. Cables and Cable Sealing Ends
11. Generation Units
12. Transformers of Generation Units and Electricity Storage Facilities
13. Auxiliary Transformers of Generation Units, Station Transformers, including low voltage automatic circuit breakers
14. Electricity Storage Facilities
15. Synchronous Compensators
16. Reactive Power Compensators
17. Capacitors (Including Filters or current-limiting reactors)
18. General equipment for Compensation Capacitors
19. Series or Shunt Reactors
20. Transmission System Transformers (step-up, step-down, bus-tie transformers)
21. Tertiary Windings

<!-- PAGE 83 -->

22. Earthing and Auxiliary Transformers
23. Three-Phase Voltage Transformers
24. Single-Phase Voltage Transformers with Phase Identification
25. High Precision Voltage Transformers and Phase Identification
26. Surge Arrester (Lightning Arrester)
27. Neutral Earthing Arrangements in High Voltage Installations
28. Ripple Control Injection Signal and Related Equipment
29. Arc Suppression Coils
30. Neutral and Phase Connections of Single-Phase Transformers
31. Current Transformers (where separate installation elements exist)
32. Bushings
33. Combined Voltage / Current Transformer Units
34. Short-Circuiting and Discharging Switches
35. Thyristors
36. Resistor with Integrated Non-Linear Voltage-Dependent Variability
37. Gas-Insulated Substation (GIS) / Hermetically Sealed Gas Installation Zone
38. Inverters.

### T1.A3 GRAPHICAL SYMBOLS ON OPERATIONAL DIAGRAMS AND COMMON INSTALLATION AREA DRAWINGS

**T1.A3.1** Operational Diagrams and Common Installation Area Drawings shall be prepared using the graphical symbols described in the latest applicable relevant IEC Specifications.

**T1.A3.2** The following reference standards are relevant:
* (a) Specification EN 60617 DB Graphical symbols for diagrams
* (b) Specification EN 61082 Preparation of documents used in electrotechnology
* (c) Specification EN 60848 GRAFSET specification language for sequential function charts
* (d) Specification EN 61175 Industrial systems, installations and equipment and industrial products - Designation of signals.

<!-- PAGE 84 -->

# T1.B - ANNEX OF CONNECTION CONDITIONS TO THE TRANSMISSION SYSTEM - BOUNDARY DEMARCATION BETWEEN TRANSMISSION SYSTEM, DISTRIBUTION SYSTEM AND INDEPENDENT AREAS OF RESPONSIBILITY

---

> [Technical Diagram Architecture Note: Single Line Diagram of Operational Boundary Demarcation - Annex T1-B1]
> 
> **Diagram Description & Structural Layout:**
> The schematic defines the functional and operational boundary demarcation between the Transmission System, Distribution System, and Independent Areas of Responsibility for Consumers and Producers:
> 
> 1. **Transmission System (TSOC):** High Voltage (HV) substation switchgear incorporating main and transfer / double busbar configurations (Busbar A / Busbar B), circuit breakers, isolators, and instrument transformers.
> 2. **Distribution System (DSO / EAC):** Medium Voltage (MV) distribution switchgear (11 kV / 22 kV) fed via step-down power transformers from the transmission grid.
> 3. **Independent Consumer Area of Responsibility:** Consumer HV/MV substation interface comprising dedicated boundary disconnect switches, circuit breakers, and metering points.
> 4. **Independent Producer Area of Responsibility:** Generating unit interface bays displaying connection nodes to Busbar A and Busbar B, point of common coupling (PCC), and demarcation disconnectors.
> 
> **Diagram Title Block Details:**
> * **Framework:** Transmission and Distribution Rules
> * **Standard:** Transmission System Rules T1 – Connection Conditions to the Transmission System (Annex "B1")
> * **Title:** Boundary Demarcation Between Transmission System, Distribution System, and Independent Areas of Responsibility for Operational Purposes
> * **Drawing Identification:** Annex « T1 - B1 »
> * **Legend:** Graphic indicators denoting color-coded/patterned boundary lines for Transmission System, Distribution System, and Independent Areas of Responsibility.

> [Technical Diagram Architecture Note: Demarcation Diagram Between Transmission System, Distribution System, and Independent Areas of Responsibility for Ownership and Maintenance Purposes (Annex T1-B2)]

### ANNEX T1 - B2

#### Title Block Information
* **Rules Reference:** TRANSMISSION AND DISTRIBUTION RULES — TRANSMISSION SYSTEM RULES T1: TERMS OF CONNECTION TO THE TRANSMISSION SYSTEM
* **Title:** DEMARCATION BETWEEN THE TRANSMISSION SYSTEM, DISTRIBUTION SYSTEM AND INDEPENDENT AREAS OF RESPONSIBILITY FOR OWNERSHIP AND MAINTENANCE PURPOSES
* **Date:** February 2022
* **Annex Code:** ANNEX « T1 - B2 »

#### System Regions and Demarcation Boundaries
* **Transmission System (Σύστημα Μεταφοράς):** Central high-voltage grid infrastructure including HV busbars, circuit breakers, disconnect switches, and current/voltage transformers defining primary grid control.
* **Distribution System (Σύστημα Διανομής):** Medium/low-voltage networks (e.g., 22 kV / 11 kV busbars and transformers) interfacing with the Transmission System boundary.
* **Independent Consumer Area of Responsibility (Ανεξάρτητη Περιοχή Ευθύνης Καταναλωτή):** Switchgear, step-down transformers, and MV busbars owned and maintained by independent high-voltage consumers downstream of the primary interface point.
* **Independent Producer Area of Responsibility (Ανεξάρτητη Περιοχή Ευθύνης Παραγωγού):** Generating unit step-up transformers, plant switchgear, and dedicated connection lines owned and maintained by independent power producers up to the transmission system boundary point.

#### Legend
* **Boundaries:** Color-coded demarcation lines indicating operational, ownership, and maintenance responsibility limits between the Transmission System, Distribution System, and Independent Consumer/Producer Regions.

# T2 - PLANNING CODE

## T2.1 INTRODUCTION

The technical and design criteria and procedures applied by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) during the planning and development of the Transmission System, in accordance with the provisions of the Law, are defined in the Planning Code (T2).

The same criteria and procedures are taken into account by Users during the planning and development of User Systems. The Planning Code provides detailed information provided by Users to the TSOC and information provided by the TSOC to Users.

The development of the Transmission System provided for under Article 73 of the Law, which includes its reinforcement and extension, is carried out for a series of reasons, including but not limited to the following:

(a) Increase in Demand (including normal growth of existing loads and new load quantities)  
(b) need for improvement of the operational reliability of the Transmission System  
(c) development in a User System that is already connected to the Transmission System  
(d) creation of a new Connection Site or modification of an existing Connection Site between the User System and the Transmission System  
(e) change in requirements for electricity transmission facilities resulting from changes in parameters such as Generators, Electricity Storage Facilities, technological requirements, and/or environmental requirements  
(f) cumulative impacts of a series of development procedures referred to in (a), (b), (c), (d), and (e) by one or more Users  

Correspondingly, the reinforcement or extension of the Transmission System may include works:

(a) at a new or existing Connection Site where the User's Plant and/or Apparatus are connected to the Transmission System  
(b) on new or existing transmission lines or other equipment connecting the Connection Site with the remainder of the Transmission System  
(c) on new or existing transmission lines or other equipment located at remote locations or between remote locations from the Connection Site  

The required time for the planning and development of the Transmission System depends on:

(a) the type and extent of the necessary reinforcement and/or extension works  
(b) the time required for presentations, applications, governmental approvals, wayleaves/rights-of-way, easement strips, building permits, as well as for any necessary legal/judicial settlements thereof

<!-- PAGE 88 -->

(c) the degree of complexity for undertaking the new project, combined with provision for the simultaneous maintenance of satisfactory security and quality of supply in the existing Transmission System.

On the basis of all the above, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) prepares and/or revises at least once a year the Ten-Year Transmission System Development Plan (TYNDP), in accordance with the provisions of Article 63 of the Law.

---

### T2.2 PURPOSE

The purpose of the Planning Code is to provide:

(a) cooperation and communication between the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and the User regarding any proposed development of the User System that may affect the operation of the Transmission System or its direct connection to the Transmission System;

(b) the provision of information required by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) from Users so that the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) is able to undertake the planning and development of the Transmission System in accordance with the Transmission System Design Criteria and the relevant specifications;

(c) the provision of information required by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) for the purpose of preparing the Forecast Report;

(d) the provision of information required by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) for the smooth operation of the Transmission System within the framework of the Electricity Market operation.

---

### T2.3 SCOPE OF APPLICATION

The Planning Code applies to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and the following Users:

(a) all Producers, Storage Facility Operators, and Consumers for Generation/Demand Entities that:
   i. are connected to the Transmission System;
   ii. are connected to the Distribution System for which a Connection Agreement has been concluded with TSOC;
   iii. submit an application for the issuance of a Connection Offer to TSOC;

(b) the Distribution System Operator (DSO);

(c) the Transmission System Owner;

(d) the Distribution System Owner.

The above categories of Users are bound by the Planning Code prior to undertaking generation, distribution, injection/withdrawal, or consumption of electrical energy, as applicable; therefore, references to the various categories (or to the general category) of User shall be understood as references to prospective Users as well as to Users who are actually connected.

---

### T2.4 CONNECTION PLANNING PROCEDURES

#### T2.4.1 Information

---
*Version 1.1.0* | *T2 – Planning Code* | *Page 88*

<!-- PAGE 89 -->

**Edition 1.1.0 T2 – Design Rules**  
**Page 89**

Users and prospective Users of the Transmission System can evaluate connection and usage capabilities of the Transmission System through:
* **(a)** The Ten-Year Network Development Plan (TYNDP), which allows prospective Users to evaluate the available connection and usage capabilities of the Transmission System and to evaluate the sections of the Transmission System that provide greater capabilities for new connections and transfer of additional quantities of electrical energy;
* **(b)** The Connection Offer proposed by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) for the purpose of concluding or modifying a Connection Agreement;
* **(c)** The published Transmission System Use Tariffs.

---

### T2.4.2 Connection Application

* **T2.4.2.1** Users who propose a new Connection Site or Modification of an existing Connection Site must submit a Written Connection Application to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus or to the Distribution System Operator in accordance with the provisions of T2.4.3.

* **T2.4.2.2** The Written Connection Application submitted by the User to TSOC for the issuance of a Connection Offer must include:
  * **(a)** A description of the Facilities and Apparatus to be connected to the Transmission System or, as the case may be, of the Modification relating to the User's Facilities and Apparatus already connected to the Transmission System, each of which is defined as a "User Development Project" in the Design Rules;
  * **(b)** The relevant data specified in the Transmission System Connection Procedure and indicatively described in the Annex to the Design Rules;
  * **(c)** The desired Connection Date and Operational Date of the proposed User Development Project.

* **T2.4.2.3** The Written Connection Application must be sent to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus as specified in detail in the CERA-approved TSOC document "Procedure for Connection to the Transmission System".

* **T2.4.2.4** The data provided with or submitted in support of the Written Connection Application are directly related to the application and are submitted to support it. These data constitute the Preliminary Project Design Data until the moment the Connection Offer is fully accepted.

---

### T2.4.3 Allocation of Responsibilities between the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus and the Distribution System Operator regarding the Processing of Connection Applications to the Transmission System or the Distribution System

The criterion for processing/forwarding a connection application of a Producer, Storage Facility Operator, or Consumer for connection to the Transmission System or the Distribution System is based on its requested installed capacity, expressed in MW, as follows:

* **(a)** For Users with a requested installed capacity equal to or less than the Power Limit for connection to the Transmission System, the connection application shall be addressed to and examined by the Distribution System Operator. 

<!-- PAGE 90 -->

**Edition 1.1.0 | T2 – Design Rules**  
**Page 90**

The DSO is responsible for evaluating these applications and for the final drafting of the Connection Offer and the Connection Agreement. The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus shall not be involved in these cases except in the event that the Distribution System Operator deems that it concerns the TSOC. It is understood that, where required, consultation shall take place between the DSO and the TSOC.

(b) For Users with a requested installed capacity exceeding the Power Limit for connection to the Transmission System, the connection application shall be addressed to and examined by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus. The TSOC is responsible for evaluating these applications and for the final drafting of the Connection Offer and the Connection Agreement. The Distribution System Operator shall not be involved in these cases except in the event that the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus deems that it concerns the DSO. It is understood that, where required, consultation shall take place between the TSOC and the DSO.

---

### T2.4.4 General Provisions for Network Connection
In general, for the connection of Users to the Transmission System or the Distribution System, the following apply:

(a) Users with a requested installed capacity equal to or lower than the Power Limit for connection to the Transmission System are entitled to a connection to the Distribution System, and the DSO is obliged to provide it in a manner that serves the optimal overall development of the network. These specific Users are also entitled to a Direct connection to the Transmission System, provided they request it.

(b) For Users with a requested installed capacity exceeding the Power Limit for connection to the Transmission System, the techno-economically optimal connection method shall be selected from the following options (following consultation between the TSOC and the DSO, if required):
* i. Direct connection to the Transmission System
* ii. Indirect connection to the Transmission System
* iii. Connection to the Distribution System.

For cases of requested installed capacity exceeding 50 MW, the network shall be constructed so as to ensure secured supply (Firm Connection) in accordance with T2.7.5(a). For all other cases, a Firm Connection shall not be granted unless requested by the Producer or the Storage Facility Operator and approved by the TSOC.

---

### T2.4.5 Connection Offer

The Connection Offer is issued based on the CERA-approved TSOC document *"Connection Procedure to the Transmission System"*. The terms and requirements, as described in this document, constitute a necessary supplement to the provisions related to the Connection Offer.

#### T2.4.5.1 
The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus examines the application for connection to the Transmission System based on the CERA-approved document *"Connection Procedure to the Transmission System"*. If the...

<!-- PAGE 91 -->

**Document Context:** Issue 1.1.0 | Code T2 – Design Code | Page 91

***

...[if the] Transmission System Operator of Cyprus considers that the connection is complex, the provisions of T2.4.6 shall apply. If the applicant User considers that the application processing time is unreasonably prolonged, the User is entitled to appeal to CERA. CERA, after evaluating the information provided to it by the User and the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus, and considering that the application is reasonable, may propose to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus a new deadline for examining the application to issue a Connection Offer.

The Connection Offer shall include, but is not limited to, the following:
(a) detailed information regarding the method of connection implementation, including details of the Facilities and Apparatus required to establish the connection;
(b) a description of any Modifications attributable to the applicant User;
(c) an indicative indication of the Connection Date and the Operational Date;
(d) an estimate of the costs entailed by the connection;
(e) the period of validity of the Connection Offer.

**T2.4.5.2** The Connection Offer must be accepted by the applicant User within the timeframe specified in the Connection Procedure to the Transmission System. Upon expiry of this timeframe, the Connection Offer shall automatically cease to be valid. Following acceptance of the Connection Offer, a Connection Agreement shall be signed by both parties, which correlates the works of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus with the User Development Project and binds the contracting parties in accordance with its terms. Within a timeframe specified by the CERA-approved TSOC document "Connection Procedure to the Transmission System" (or for a longer timeframe if agreed by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus for the specific case) prior to acceptance of the Connection Offer, the User shall provide the data relating to the User Development Project, as specified in the Appendix to the Design Code.

**T2.4.5.3** If the Connection Offer is accepted, then all data provided in the Written Application and the data submitted together with it shall constitute the Committed Project Planning Data.

### T2.4.6 Complexity Conditions

**T2.4.6.1** The scale and complexity of any extension or reinforcement of the Transmission System vary according to the nature, location, and timing of the proposed User Development Project that forms the subject of the application. In several cases, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus may perform additional or more extensive system studies for a fuller evaluation of the impact of the proposed User Development Project on the Transmission System. If the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus deems that additional or more extensive studies are necessary, the Connection Offer may indicate the areas requiring more detailed analysis. Prior to the execution of additional studies, the User shall declare whether it wishes the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus to undertake their execution and accept the potential submission of a revised Connection Offer within the allowed timeframe or within a longer timeframe.

<!-- PAGE 92 -->

...which the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) reasonably deems necessary. The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus conducts the above studies either itself or assigns them to another service provider (consulting firms, etc.) and in any case is entitled to require from the User the payment of the expenses incurred by these studies.

The TSOC may, in any case and for every Connection Application submitted to the TSOC, request the User to perform a technical study simulating the behavior of the proposed User Development under normal operating conditions and under fault conditions. The study must include static and dynamic analysis, in accordance with the criteria, preconditions, and specific details specified by the TSOC. Regardless of who performs the study (the TSOC or the User), the User must satisfy clause T2.8, so that the TSOC maintains an up-to-date and valid model of the System at all times and is able to conduct any studies deemed necessary (related, for example, to the Forecast Report or to the impact on the network of the User Development).

**T2.4.6.2** For the preparation of the above detailed system studies by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus, the User shall, upon request by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus, submit some or all of the data specified in the Appendix of T2 as Committed Project Planning Data prior to the expiry of the specified time limit, provided that the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus considers them relevant and necessary. In the event that these data are provided, they shall be deemed as Preliminary Project Planning Data and are submitted to support the written application as described in T2.4.2.

**T2.4.7** When a Producer or a Storage Facility Operator intends to close, decommission, or cease maintaining, in accordance with Good Industry Practice, any Generating Unit or Generating Units with a total Generating Capacity greater than 5 MW, or Electricity Storage Facilities of any capacity, it shall notify the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus at least six (6) months prior to the target date for the suspension of normal operation of the Unit. Attention is drawn in these cases to the additional obligations of the Producer or the Storage Facility Operator pursuant to the provisions of Chapter 6 of the Electricity Market Rules.

---

### T2.5 SYSTEM PLANNING

**T2.5.1** For the planning and development of the Transmission System, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus, in accordance with the relevant specifications provided in T2.7, may require Users to submit data and information to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus on a regular basis. Information received for this purpose constitutes System Planning Data.

**T2.5.2** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus may also require additional data and information from a User. When the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus reasonably considers that these...

<!-- PAGE 93 -->

**Edition 1.1.0 | T2 – Planning Code | Page 93**

---

...information is required, then the User must submit the information to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) without delay. The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may require this information in order to:
(a) plan and develop the Transmission System in accordance with the relevant specifications;
(b) monitor the adequacy and performance of the Power System and project the future adequacy and performance of the Power System;
(c) satisfy its legal or regulatory obligations.

**T2.5.3** During the design of the Transmission System development, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may require an individual User, or a group of Users, to modify or install new Plant or Apparatus, where the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) can reasonably demonstrate that such action is prudent and necessary. The User may reasonably contest the modification or installation of new Plant or Apparatus, as required under Good Industry Practice, if it considers it to be technically infeasible. This case may include, but is not limited to, for example, the installation of Power System Stabilizers (PSS).

---

### T2.6 DATA

**T2.6.1** With regard to T2, the following relevant types of data exist: **Preliminary Project Planning Data**, **Committed Project Planning Data**, and **Transmission System Planning Data**.

**T2.6.1.1** These types, which relate to levels of confidentiality, commitment, and validation, are described below.

**T2.6.1.2** **Preliminary Project Planning Data** and **Committed Project Planning Data** relate to the data required from the User at various stages during the process of establishing a new Connection Site or Modification of an existing Connection Site, as specified in T2.4, and more specifically in the Connection or Modification application form. **Transmission System Planning Data** relates to data that must be submitted by all Users at regular intervals, or to other data or information as required by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) in accordance with T2.6.

**T2.6.2** An existing User proposing a new Connection Site shall provide the data included in the application for a Connection Offer, in compliance with the Planning Code rules relevant to the new Connection Site. This information constitutes Preliminary Project Planning Data or Committed Project Planning Data (as applicable). Conversely, data provided by an existing User in accordance with the Planning Code rules relevant to an existing Connection Site constitutes Transmission System Planning Data.

**T2.6.3 Preliminary Project Planning Data**

<!-- PAGE 94 -->

**Version 1.1.0 T2 – Design Rules**  
**Page 94**

***

**T2.6.3.1** At the point in time when a User submits an application for a Connection Offer and prior to the point in time when the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) makes such an offer, the data relating to the User Development constitute the **Preliminary Project Design Data**. These data are considered confidential, subject to the terms relating to the policy of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) on confidentiality matters.

**T2.6.3.2** **Preliminary Project Design Data** include data that may reasonably be required by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) for the evaluation of the connection application, as specified in T2.4.2 and, if applicable, other data directly related and submitted in support of the application.

### T2.6.4 Committed Project Design Data

When the Connection Offer is formally accepted by the prospective User, the data relating to the User Development, which have already been submitted as Preliminary Project Design Data, together with any additional data required by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) in accordance with the Design Rules, constitute the **Committed Project Design Data**. These data, together with other data held by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) pertaining to the Transmission System, form the basis for evaluating new applications from any User and for implementing Transmission System design and power system analysis. Consequently, Committed Project Design Data are non-confidential to the extent that the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) is committed:

(a) to use them for drafting the Forecast Report and for providing information regarding the Forecast Report;  
(b) to use them for considering and/or advising on applications (or prospective applications) of other Users (including using them to provide data therefrom, orally and in writing, to other Users who are applying or intending to submit an application, which according to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) are relevant to other applications or future applications);  
(c) to use them for Transmission System design purposes.

**T2.6.5** To illustrate the different types of data, Preliminary Project Design Data and Committed Project Design Data are categorized into data which:

(a) relate to forecasts;  
(b) are finalized after the connection is implemented (and are subject to any subsequent modifications) and are known as **Registered Data**;  
(c) relate to Plant and/or Apparatus which, upon implementation of the connection, constitute Registered Data and, prior to implementation of the connection, constitute an estimate of expected data.

### T2.6.6 Transmission System Design Data

<!-- PAGE 95 -->

**Version 1.1.0 | T2 – Planning Code | Page 95**

***

**T2.6.6.1** The Planning Code requires the User to submit to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), as soon as reasonably practicable and no later than the Operational Date, the requirements described in the Appendix to the Planning Code for data that have not been previously requested by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and have not been provided by the User. These data include the confirmation of estimated values assumed for planning purposes or, where practicable, their replacement with verified actual values, together with revised future estimates for items relating to forecasts.

**T2.6.6.2** The Planning Code requires Users to submit to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) each year the Transmission System Planning Data, as set out in the Appendix to T2. If, between the date of submission and the next submission date, there is no change in the data submitted for a specific year, instead of resubmitting these data, the User may submit a Written Confirmation stating that there has been no change to the data submitted previously for that specified year.

**T2.6.6.3** Transmission System Planning Data, together with other data held by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) relating to the Transmission System, form the basis for considering new applications from any User and for implementing the planning of the Transmission System. Therefore, Transmission System Planning Data are not confidential to the extent that the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) is bound:

(a) to use them for the preparation of the Forecast Report and for providing information relating to the Forecast Report;

(b) to use them for considering and/or advising upon applications (or prospective applications) of other Users (including using them to provide data derived therefrom, orally or in writing, to other Users applying or intending to submit an application, which according to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) relate to other applications or future applications);

(c) to use them for System planning purposes.

To illustrate the different types of data referred to above, Transmission System Planning Data are categorized into data items which:

(a) relate to forecasts;

(b) are finalized after connection has been effected (and are subject to any subsequent changes) and are known as Registered Data;

(c) relate to Plant and/or Apparatus and which, as regards matters concerning Plant and/or Apparatus such as the submission date, constitute Registered Data, but for the subsequent ten years will represent an estimate of expected data.

**T2.6.7 Data Concerning Consumers Constituting Demand Response Loads**

<!-- PAGE 96 -->

**Edition 1.1.0** | **T2 – Design Rules** | **Page 96**

---

**T2.6.7.1** For design purposes and the proper operation of the Transmission System, the data described in Appendix T2.A3 for Consumers representing AZ Loads shall be submitted to the TSOC. These data must be updated appropriately whenever they change.

---

### T2.7 DESIGN SPECIFICATIONS

**T2.7.1** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall apply the Transmission System Design Criteria and relevant specifications for the design and development of the Transmission System.

**T2.7.2** For the determination of the technical requirements of a User connection, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus shall not discriminate between Users of the same category, location, and size, even though it may not be technically and economically feasible to achieve uniform connection methods in all cases.

**T2.7.3** The connection method used may exceed the relevant specifications following a request by the User and provided that the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus agrees.

**T2.7.4** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus must take into account all data provided by Users and perform detailed system analyses. The methodology must be based on clearly defined criteria so as to achieve an impartial development of Transmission System plans. Transmission System development plans, including its reinforcement and expansion, shall consider overall geographical Demand trends so as to allow the rational development of the Distribution System through the appropriate and logical location of the Transmission System. In these cases, there must be close cooperation between the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus and the Distribution System Operator (DSO).

**T2.7.5** For new connections to the Transmission System, the following Transmission System Design Criteria apply, among others:

* **(a)** If the Generating Capacity or the Maximum Discharge Capacity (in MW) of the Power Generating Station or the Electricity Storage Facility is less than 50 MW, the connection is Non-Firm. If the Producer or the Electricity Storage Facility Operator so desires and technical or other System conditions permit, the connection may be Firm, at their own financial expense. In the event that the Generating Capacity or Maximum Discharge Capacity (in MW) is equal to or greater than 50 MW, the connection is always Firm.

* **(b)** The main trunk lines of the Transmission System, as defined by the TSOC, shall not be interrupted for the insertion of a Substation, unless, in the judgment of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus, there are reasons for improved interconnection within the Transmission System and increased security of the Electrical System.

---

### T2.8 REQUIREMENTS FOR MODELING

<!-- PAGE 97 -->

**Version 1.1.0 | T2 – Design Rules | Page 97**

---

**T2.8.1**  
Section T2.8 applies to all Power Producers and Storage Facility Operators, except for those connected to the Low Voltage Distribution System. It supplements clause T2.4.6 without replacing it.

**T2.8.2**  
Each User described in clause T2.8.1 shall provide to TSOC without delay the necessary data, parameters, information, and software models of generators, storage systems, control systems, reactive power compensation systems, and/or any other main, individual, supplementary, or auxiliary systems that will allow the modeling of the entire Power Generating Station or Electricity Storage Facility for the purpose of conducting detailed system studies (including static and dynamic analysis), as well as any other technical data or information requested that pertain to the connection and operation of their Power Generating Station or Electricity Storage Facility and are necessary for the elaboration of system studies. Relevant clause is T2.4.6.2.

**T2.8.3**  
Each User described in clause T2.8.1 shall provide to TSOC without delay, and in any case prior to the issuance of a Connection Offer, static and dynamic models simulating the behavior of their Power Generating Station or Electricity Storage Facility under normal and non-normal operating conditions. These models must be made available to TSOC electronically in DigSILENT PowerFactory® format and comply with the criteria and requirements specified by TSOC. The electronic file submitted must be compatible with the latest version of the DigSILENT PowerFactory® software.

The complete static and dynamic model to be submitted by Power Producers must include, but is not limited to, the following:

* **(a)** Generator modeling for each Power Generating Unit;
* **(b)** Modeling of all relevant control systems, such as the turbine-governor system, the automatic voltage regulator (AVR), and the power system stabilizer (PSS), for each conventional Power Generating Unit;
* **(c)** Modeling of other limits and limiters affecting generator operation, such as PF/MVAr control, V/Hz limiters, minimum/maximum exciter field current limiters, stator current limiters, PQ capability limits, generator stability limits, etc.;
* **(d)** Modeling of Fault Ride Through (FRT) capability;
* **(e)** Modeling of the step-up transformer(s) for each Power Generating Unit;
* **(f)** Modeling of the topology within the Power Generating Station and at the Connection Point with the grid (e.g., connection cable, etc.);
* **(g)** Modeling of control mechanisms/systems implemented or installed at the Power Generating Station, including the following control mechanisms/systems:
  * **i.** Active Power Control
  * **ii.** Reactive Power Control
  * **iii.** Rate of Change Control
  * **iv.** Voltage Control at the Connection Point

<!-- PAGE 98 -->

**Edition 1.1.0 T2 – Design Rules**
**Page 98**

v. other  
(h) Modeling of other characteristics of equipment, mechanisms, or control systems of the Power Generating Station.

Correspondingly, the integrated static and dynamic model to be submitted by the Electricity Storage Facility Operators must include the following, without being limited to these:

(a) Modeling of Storage Systems (including inverters) and the RES generating unit generator in the case of an Electricity Storage Facility with Integrated RES  
(b) Modeling of all relevant control systems, such as the automatic voltage regulator (AVR)  
(c) Modeling of other limits and limiters affecting the operation of the Electricity Storage Facility  
(d) Modeling of Fault Ride Through (FRT) / Uninterrupted Operation Capability following a fault  
(e) Modeling of step-up transformer(s)  
(f) Modeling of the topology within the Electricity Storage Facility and at the Connection Point with the grid (e.g. connection cable, etc.)  
(g) Modeling of control mechanisms/systems implemented or installed in the Electricity Storage Facility, including the following control mechanisms/systems:  
i. Active Power Control  
ii. Reactive Power Control  
iii. Ramp Rate Control  
iv. Voltage Control at the Connection Point  
v. other  
(h) Modeling of other characteristics of the equipment, mechanisms, or control systems of the Electricity Storage Facility.

Regarding the modeling of the protection systems of each Generating Unit and Power Generating Station or Electricity Storage Facility (e.g. under/over-frequency, under/over-voltage, Rate of Change of Frequency (ROCOF), etc.), the User must submit to the TSOC the final settings of each protection system or different type of protection that has been installed at the Power Generating Station or Electricity Storage Facility, as in force on the Operational Date. In the event that, after the Operational Date, these settings are modified according to the procedures provided for in the Transmission Rules, the User must immediately inform the TSOC and cooperate for the updating of the model.

The requirement to provide models to the TSOC based on the provisions of T2.8 applies both to Power Generating Stations with conventional Generating Units and to RES Stations and Electricity Storage Facilities. Depending on the case, the User must clarify whether any of the above items do not apply to their Power Generating Station or Electricity Storage Facility.

Additionally, these models must necessarily be accompanied by an explanatory document regarding their import into the DigSILENT PowerFactory® platform operated by the TSOC, as well as an appropriate manual (documentation) describing in detail the operation...

<!-- PAGE 99 -->

...and the use of each structural element of the modeled equipment, e.g., generator, wind turbine generator, storage systems, control systems, MW-MVAr operating diagram, protection systems, etc.

**T2.8.4** All information and models provided by the User to TSOC must be accurate, correct, appropriately updated, verified, and validated, so as to:
i. allow TSOC to use them to perform technical studies analyzing the behavior of the Power Plant or Electricity Storage Facility, and
ii. convince TSOC that the behavior of the integrated model of the Power Plant or Electricity Storage Facility and the results obtained from the simulation are representative of the behavior that actual equipment would exhibit if operated under actual operating conditions, similar to those of the simulation.

**T2.8.5** If the model of the Power Plant or Electricity Storage Facility is not appropriately verified and validated prior to the energization of the Power Plant or Electricity Storage Facility, then TSOC retains the right to request the User to conduct appropriate tests and measurements at the Power Plant in order to evaluate and/or verify the validity and correctness of the model. The tests and measurements conducted, as well as the entire data verification and validation process, shall be implemented in accordance with the provisions of T2.9.

### T2.9 DATA VERIFICATION AND VALIDATION

**T2.9.1** If a User submits data which, in the opinion of TSOC, is incorrect, then TSOC may require the User to submit additional information that TSOC deems necessary to verify the accuracy of the data.

**T2.9.2** If, following the evaluation of the information submitted in accordance with T2.9.1, TSOC reasonably considers that the additional information is not sufficient to verify the original data, then TSOC may require the User to conduct Tests to verify the data. When a Test or Tests are required, these shall be conducted in accordance with the terms of the relevant operating rules.

**T2.9.3** If the data submitted by the User is verified as correct by a Test or Tests, then the entire cost of the agreed Test or Tests shall be borne fully by TSOC.

**T2.9.4** If the data submitted is not verified or is deemed inaccurate, then the cost of the Tests shall be borne fully by the User, and the data values resulting from the Tests shall be the values used for the data. If, due to changes in data resulting from a Test or Tests, TSOC needs to perform additional system studies, the User shall also bear the cost of these additional studies.

# T2.A - PLANNING CODE APPENDIX - REQUIRED DATA

## T2.A1 INTRODUCTION

This Appendix specifies, by way of indication, the data that must be submitted to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) by Users or prospective Users of the Transmission System. The requirement to provide data is defined by the Planning Code. Any material changes to the data, as specified in T2.A3, T2.A4, or T2.A5, must be notified to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) as soon as possible.

The exact data to be submitted are specified in the Connection Procedure to the Transmission System.

---

## T2.A1.1 GENERAL INFORMATION

The data required to be provided by all Users are:

1. **User Details:**
   - (a) Full name or legal entity name
   - (b) Address
   - (c) Telephone number
   - (d) Fax number
   - (e) E-mail address

2. **Contact Person Details:**
   - (a) Full name or legal entity name
   - (b) Address
   - (c) Telephone number
   - (d) Fax number
   - (e) E-mail address

---

## T2.A2 NEW CONNECTIONS

### T2.A2.1 General Information for New Connections

The data required to be provided by all Users for new connections are:

- **(a)** Projected or expected Operational Date.
- **(b)** Projected or expected Connection Date.
- **(c)** Required operational reliability of the connection (number of connected circuits, for example one, two, or more) in accordance with technical standards and the operational security and reliability standards of the system.
- **(d)** Map and Diagrams:
  A Topographical Plan from the Department of Lands and Surveys of a scale of at least 1:5,000 is provided, stating the Sheet, Plan, Block, and Parcel, with the location of the installation clearly marked with an "X". Additionally, the coordinates of the electrical connection point to the Transmission System are specified, considering that it is at the point...

<!-- PAGE 102 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T2A – Design Rules - Annexes** | **Page 102**

---

...of the High Voltage insulators of the Transformers connected to the Transmission System.

(e) Provision of a site plan of the proposed installation (scale 1:200 or 1:250), indicating the proposed position of the transmission substation fencing, the location of the connection point, the generators, the storage systems, the transformers, the facility buildings, etc. The plan must be submitted on paper. A digital format of the data may also be required, which must be provided if available.

(f) Provision of an electrical single-line diagram of the proposed installation showing in detail the major equipment of the installation. The diagram must be submitted on paper. A digital format of the data may also be required, which must be provided if available.

### T2.A2.2 Information Regarding Licensing and Authorization of Generation and Storage (Only for Generation and Storage Applications)

#### T2.A2.2.1 Generation/Storage License:
Details regarding the Generation or Storage License obtained by the applicants or regarding the application for the granting of a License.

#### T2.A2.2.2 Authorization:
Details regarding the authorization or Building Permit for the construction or reconstruction of the power generation station or the Electricity Storage Facility with which connection is sought.

---

### T2.A3 DEMAND DATA REQUIREMENTS FROM CONSUMERS AND DSO

#### T2.A3.1 Use of Demand Data

At the point in time when the User submits an application for a Connection Offer and prior to the point in time when the offer is made by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and accepted by the applicant User, the above data are considered as **Preliminary Project Design Data** in accordance with T2.6.3.

When the Connection Offer is formally accepted by the prospective User, all data must be provided by the User and constitute the **Binding Project Design Data** in accordance with T2.6.4.

By the projected Operational Date or Modification Date, all data requirements recorded in this Annex must be submitted by the User to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and constitute the **Transmission System Design Data** in accordance with T2.6.6. These data include the confirmation of estimated values considered for design purposes and their replacement with verified actual values and with revised estimates for future Demand.

#### T2.A3.2 Maximum Absorption Capacity

<!-- PAGE 103 -->

Maximum Absorption Capacity (in MVA along with the power factor $\cos\phi$ at maximum Demand) is required to be provided.

### T2.A3.3 Measured Data and Forecast Data

**T2.A3.3.1** For each off-take/injection point from the Transmission System to a network of lower voltage level (Distribution System or a Consumer connected to the Transmission System), the required information is as follows:

(a) Measurement data for each calendar year. The information must be provided no later than the 28th of February of the following year.  
(b) A ten-year Demand forecast as specified for each case by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) (see Table 2A.1).  
(c) Description of the basic principles of the forecasting process.  
(d) Date and time of peak Demand.

### T2.A3.5 Required User System Data

**T2.A3.5.1** The required data for the User System are the following:

* **(a)** Single-line diagram of the User System Facilities at the level of detail agreed with the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC).
* **(b)** Electrical characteristics of all circuits at 11 kV, 22 kV, 66 kV, 132 kV, 220 kV and of equipment ($R, X, B, R_0, X_0, B_0$), continuous operation and overload limits.
* **(c)** Contribution from the User's network to a three-phase short circuit at the connection point.
* **(d)** Connection details of all transformers, capacitors, reactors, and other elements connected at 11 kV, 22 kV, 66 kV, 132 kV, 220 kV (in star, delta, zig-zag, etc.).
* **(e)** Electrical characteristics of all circuits at 11 kV, 22 kV, 66 kV, 132 kV, 220 kV and equipment at voltage levels below 11 kV that can form a closed loop between two connection points on the Transmission System.

---

### T2.A3.6 Standby Supply Data

**T2.A3.6.1** For each load that can be supplied from more than one supply point, in cases where this is deemed necessary by the TSOC, the following information is required:

* **(a)** Source of standby supply (alternative supply points).
* **(b)** Required supply power in standby state (in MVA together with the power factor $\cos\phi$).

---

### T2.A3.7 Load Fluctuations

**T2.A3.7.1** For each Consumer connected to the Transmission System, the following information, among others, is required if requested by the TSOC:

* **(a)** Rate of Change of Active Power and Reactive Power, for both increase and decrease (in kW/s and kVAr/s respectively).
* **(b)** The minimum repeating time interval between fluctuations of Active Power and Reactive Power Demand (in seconds).
* **(c)** The magnitude of the largest step change in Active Power and Reactive Power Demand (in kW and kVAr respectively).
* **(d)** Description of any Disturbing Loads intended to be connected to the Transmission System.

---

### T2.A3.8 Demand Response Load Data

**T2.A3.8.1** For Consumers connected to the Transmission System that constitute Demand Response Loads, in addition to the remaining provisions of T2.A3, data specified by the Transmission System Operator must be declared, and at a minimum the following:

* **(a)** Maximum Absorption Capacity.
* **(b)** Capability to provide Ancillary Services as follows:
  * **i.** Maximum technical capability to provide Frequency Containment Reserve (FCR) (upward and downward) and Frequency Restoration Reserve (FRR) (upward and downward).

<!-- PAGE 106 -->

ii. The capability to operate under Automatic Generation Control (AGC), and where such capability exists, the control range and the minimum upward and downward Ramp Rate of absorbed Active Power under AGC.

iii. Any limitations regarding the provision of Ancillary Services.

(c) The start-up time of the load, defined as the time from the moment the load absorbs no energy until the load reaches normal operation status.

(d) A document serving as evidence of compliance of Load AZ with the requirements of the TSR. Through this document, the Registered Information concerning Load AZ must also be confirmed.

(e) If automatic control is used, setting details.

### T2.A4 GENERATOR DATA REQUIREMENTS

#### T2.A4.1 General Details

Every Producer must submit to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) the following detailed information required for the design, construction, and operation of the Transmission System:

(a) Station name  
(b) Number of generating units  
(c) Generating unit technology  
(d) Primary fuel type / Prime mover (e.g., natural gas)  
(e) Secondary fuel type (e.g., fuel oil)  
(f) Required Equipment Loading Capacity for the connection of Generation (in MW)  
(g) Required Equipment Loading Capacity for the connection of Reactive Power generation (in MVArs) to the Transmission System (for synchronous generators)  

#### T2.A4.2 Processing of Generator Data

The following symbols are used for the data:

\* Data that must be provided by applicants and constitute the Preliminary Project Design Data as described in T2.6.3.

§ Data which, if not provided by the applicant as Preliminary Project Design Data, are estimated by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) under the sole responsibility of the applicant. The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) informs the applicant that these data estimates constitute Preliminary Project Design Data as described in T2.6.3.

When the Connection Offer is formally accepted by the prospective User, the User shall provide all data, which constitute the Committed Project Design Data as described in T2.6.4.

In accordance with the Operational Date or the Modification Date, all data requirements, as recorded in this Appendix, must be submitted by the User and constitute the System Design Data as described in T2.6.6. These include the confirmation of estimated values considered for design purposes and their replacement with verified actual values and revised estimates for forecast items.

#### T2.A4.3 Registered Generating Unit Data

**T2.A4.3.1** The minimum requirements for the operating conditions of the Generating Units are specified in the Transmission System Connection Terms.

**T2.A4.3.2** For thermal unit installations, a functional block diagram of the main plant components is required, showing boilers, heat exchangers, heat or steam supplies to other processes, etc., indicating whether it is single-shaft or split-shaft.

<!-- PAGE 110 -->

* (d) Maximum Energy Generation Period (in hh:mm) – If applicable

**T2.A4.3.4** It is noted that the declared Ramp Up Rate and Ramp Down Rate must be equal to the corresponding maximum values specified by the manufacturer of the Generation Unit. In the event that technical reasons render the use of these rates undesirable, the Producer is entitled to declare lower rates, provided that this declaration is justified and approved by TSOC. TSOC may review the relevant decision temporarily or permanently, at its discretion, informing the Producer accordingly.

  The declared capability for providing upward and downward Frequency Containment Reserve (FCR) from a Generation / Demand Entity.

### T2.A4.5 § Automatic Voltage Regulator:

Provision of the control block diagram in the frequency domain (Laplace) in accordance with IEEE standards for excitation models (or as otherwise agreed with the Transmission System Operator), such that all time constants and gains defining the transfer function from the compensator or generator terminal Voltage and field current up to the generator field excitation Voltage are fully specified.

---

### T2.A4.6 § Governor Control System:

A control block diagram in the frequency domain (Laplace domain) shall be provided in accordance with standardized IEEE diagrams for prime movers of thermal and hydroelectric units (or as otherwise agreed with the Transmission System Operator), such that all time constants and gains are fully defined and the transfer function of the governor in relation to frequency variations and operating conditions is fully explained.

---

### T2.A4.7 § Control Devices (including Power System Stabilizers) and Protection Relays

An additional control block diagram in the frequency domain (Laplace domain) is required to be provided for distinct control devices or special protection relays of the generating unit, which act automatically on operating characteristics within 30 sec following a system disturbance and have a minimum time constant of at least 0.02 sec.

---

### T2.A4.8 § Environmental Impact

The data of Table 2A.7 shall be declared.

All submitted data must be in SI units.<br>• In case the emissions curve is not available, emissions must be submitted as value pairs of $\text{CO}_2$ emissions ($\text{kg}$) and active power ($\text{MW}$), per $1\text{ MW}$. |

All submitted data must be in SI units.<br>• In case the emissions curve is not available, emissions must be submitted as value pairs of $\text{SO}_2$ emissions ($\text{mg/Nm}^3$) and active power ($\text{MW}$), per $1\text{ MW}$. |
| $\text{NO}_x$ | • Capability to calculate the $\text{NO}_x$ emissions curve vs. Unit Active Power Output, as well as all relevant data for its application. All submitted data must be in SI units.<br>• In case the emissions curve is not available, emissions must be submitted as value pairs of $\text{NO}_x$ emissions ($\text{mg/Nm}^3$) and active power ($\text{MW}$), per $1\text{ MW}$. |

# T3 - DEMAND FORECASTS AND RES INJECTION FORECASTS

## T3.1 INTRODUCTION

**T3.1.1** Demand Forecasts and RES Injection Forecasts (T3) deal with the forecasting of Demand, as well as the forecasting of Generation from RES, for the purposes of operational planning, maintenance, and development of the Transmission System, as well as for the purposes of more effective fulfillment by TSOC of its obligations within the framework of the Balancing Market, as these are specified in the Electricity Market Rules.

**T3.1.2** Demand Forecasts and RES Injection Forecasts are conducted by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus on a multi-day, weekly, monthly, and annual basis, as applicable, taking into account the data and information provided by Users in accordance with T3. The data provided in accordance with the requirements of T2 are also taken into account.

**T3.1.3** It is explicitly clarified that T3 does not apply to the preparation of the Daily Demand Forecast and RES Injection Forecast conducted by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus within the framework of the Balancing Market in accordance with the Electricity Market Rules. The factors and data taken into account by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus when preparing this type of Forecasts are specified in the relevant Chapters of the Transmission Rules as well as the Electricity Market Rules.

**T3.1.4** Furthermore, it is clarified that the fulfillment of the obligation to provide information by Users within the framework of T3 does not in any case exempt them from their obligations within the framework of the operation of the Electricity Market, such as, for example, obligations for the submission of offers and declarations in accordance with the provisions of the Electricity Market Rules.

**T3.1.5** In T3, the Demand Forecast concerns:
* The total Demand on the Transmission System, when prepared by TSOC
* The total Demand on the Distribution System, when prepared by DSO

**T3.1.6** In T3, the RES Injection Forecast concerns:
* Generation from RES on the Distribution System (excluding RES Units connected to the Distribution System for which, however, the Connection Agreement has been concluded with TSOC), when compiled by the DSO
* Generation from RES at the Designated Metering Point, when compiled by a RES Producer
* The RES Injection Forecast in accordance with the provisions of the Electricity Market Rules, when compiled by TSOC.

**T3.1.7** T3 also deals with the provision of data regarding planned and unplanned interventions of the Distribution System Operator, and/or other Users, which may cause changes

<!-- PAGE 123 -->

**Version 1.1.0 | T3 – Demand Forecasts and RES Injection Forecasts**  
**Page 123**

---

...over 5 MW in Demand during operational, maintenance, and development planning of the Transmission System.

**T3.1.8** T3 also includes specific additional obligations for RES and CHP Producers regarding the provision of data to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus.

### T3.2 PURPOSE

The purpose of T3 is to:

**T3.2.1** Facilitate the provision of data to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus by Users within the framework of operational, maintenance, and development planning of the Transmission System.

**T3.2.2** Provide methods for evaluating errors in Demand Forecasts and RES Injection Forecasts submitted to TSOC by Users via the relevant Directive provided for in T3.4.6.4.

**T3.2.3** Provide the necessary information and data taken into account by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus when forecasting Demand and RES Injection.

### T3.3 SCOPE OF APPLICATION

T3 applies to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus and to Users, who in T3 include the following:

* **(a)** RES and CHP Producers, for RES Units and CHP Units connected to the Transmission System as well as for RES Units connected to the Distribution System for which a Connection Agreement has been concluded with TSOC;
* **(b)** Distribution System Operator;
* **(c)** Consumers connected to the Transmission System.

### T3.4 PROVISION OF INFORMATION AND REQUIREMENTS FROM THE TRANSMISSION SYSTEM OPERATOR OF CYPRUS

**T3.4.1** Each User shall provide to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus the data required in the relevant provisions of T3.4 referenced below (excluding Demand solely related to Power Station Auxiliary Installations).

**T3.4.2** In this T3.4:

* **(a)** Annual Demand Forecasts refer to Demand per week of the year.
* **(b)** Monthly Demand Forecasts refer to Demand per week.
* **(c)** Weekly Demand Forecasts refer to Demand per day.
* **(d)** Daily or Short-term Demand Forecasts and Short-term RES Injection Forecasts refer to Demand or Generation for each Settlement Period.
* **(e)** Year 0 refers to the current Year at any point in time, Year 1 refers to the next Year at any point in time, Year 2 refers to the year following Year 1, etc.

<!-- PAGE 124 -->

**Version 1.1.0**  
**T3 – Demand Forecasts and RES Injection Forecasts**  
**Page 124**

---

### T3.4.3 Information provided on an annual basis

For each week of Year 1, Demand Forecasts for each week of the following year must be provided in writing to TSOC, no later than the 15th calendar day of the last month of Year 0, including the maximum and minimum Demand values for each week, per Transmission System Supply Point. These forecasts shall be provided by the Distribution System Operator (DSO) to TSOC.

In cases where there is a busbar splitting at a Transmission System Supply Point resulting in the operation of distinct sections, separate forecast data sets for each busbar section must be provided in accordance with the specific requirements of TSOC.

---

### T3.4.4 Information provided on a monthly basis

No later than one week prior to the start of the following month, the following must be provided in writing to TSOC:

(a) **Forecast of total Demand** for each week of the following month, including the maximum and minimum Demand value for each week. These forecasts shall be provided by the Distribution System Operator (DSO) to TSOC.

(b) **Any Consumer Demand Control** scheduled by any Supplier that may result in a change in Demand of 5 MW or more as an average value for each Settlement Period and for any Transmission System Supply Point. This includes controlled Demand, e.g., water heating, thermal storage heating, street lighting, water pumping, etc. These data shall be provided by the Distribution System Operator (DSO) to TSOC.

---

### T3.4.5 Information provided on a weekly basis

No later than every Friday, a Forecast of total Demand for each day of the following week must be provided in writing to TSOC, including the maximum and minimum Demand value for each day. These forecasts shall be communicated to TSOC by the Distribution System Operator (DSO).

---

### T3.4.6 Information provided on a daily basis

**T3.4.6.1** The Distribution System Operator (DSO) is obligated to submit to TSOC Demand Forecasts regarding the total energy expected to be consumed in the Distribution System. These forecasts shall be submitted for each Settlement Period on a daily basis.

**T3.4.6.2** The Distribution System Operator (DSO) is obligated to submit to TSOC Short-Term RES Injection Forecasts for the Distribution System, as well as the Schedule...

<!-- PAGE 125 -->

**Version 1.1.0 | T3 – Demand Forecasts and RES Infeed Forecasts | Page 125**

---

... total CHP Generation for all CHP Generation Units connected to the Distribution System. The forecasts and relevant schedules shall be submitted for each Trading Period on a daily basis and revised at regular intervals defined by the TSOC.

**T3.4.6.3** RES and CHP Producers shall be obligated to submit to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus RES Infeed Forecasts for each Trading Period, on a multi-day and daily basis, as well as revised RES Infeed Forecasts at regular intervals defined by the TSOC.

**T3.4.6.4** The RES Infeed Forecasts submitted to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus must lie within the error limits defined in a relevant Directive issued by the TSOC.

**T3.4.6.5** In cases of violations and where deemed necessary, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus shall prepare a supplementary document titled "Non-Compliance Report" in accordance with the provisions of T11.9.

**T3.4.6.6** For the purpose of evaluating the accuracy of RES Infeed Forecasts, any generation curtailments executed to ensure the safe operation of the Electricity System shall be taken into account.

**T3.4.6.7** RES and CHP Producers shall be obligated to submit to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus the corresponding CHP Generation Schedule for each Trading Period, on a daily basis.

**T3.4.6.8** The submission of information provided to the TSOC pursuant to the provisions of T3.4.6 on a daily basis must be submitted at least 13 hours prior to the start of the Trading Day.

### T3.4.7 Information provided as soon as it becomes available

The Distribution System Operator, Producers, Load Representatives, and Consumers connected to the Transmission System must inform the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus of conditions that may cause a change in load as specified in the Electricity Market Rules, Chapter 8, Paragraph 5.4, events that may cause a change in available generation as specified in the Electricity Market Rules, Chapter 6, Paragraph 3, as well as any changes to the information provided pursuant to T3.4.4, T3.4.5, and T3.4.6 as soon as such information becomes available. This information must be provided in writing, or as otherwise agreed between the Users and the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus.

---
< End of T3 >
