Version 1.1.0 | T4 – Maintenance Scheduling Rules | Page 127

# T4 - MAINTENANCE SCHEDULING RULES

## T4.1 INTRODUCTION

The safe operation of the Power System requires that maintenance of User Facilities and of the Transmission System is performed in a timely and regular manner. This is a fundamental prerequisite for the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) to fulfill its obligations regarding the operation of the Transmission System and to enable Users to regularly schedule their maintenance, taking into account Facility requirements and resource constraints. The Maintenance Scheduling Rules under T4 standardize the procedures through which such maintenance activities are executed.

## T4.2 OBJECTIVE

The primary objective of T4 is to promote the development and implementation of the Generation Unit Maintenance Schedule (GUMS) and to ensure the development and implementation of the Transmission System Maintenance Schedule (TSMS), in such a manner that maintenance activities are performed in a coordinated way, thereby minimizing the number and impact of constraints imposed on the safe and economic operation of the Transmission System.

To achieve this objective, T4 defines the following:

(a) The formal procedure for submitting maintenance proposals by Generators to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC).

(b) The formal procedure for submitting maintenance proposals by the Transmission System Owner to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC).

(c) The procedures for reviewing the Indicative, Provisional, and Approved Maintenance Schedule by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), in consultation with Generators. T4 applies to all proposed maintenance activities that may affect the capability of a Generating Unit to achieve its full Maximum Continuous Rating (MCR) on each Registered Fuel in accordance with its Registered Operating Characteristics. Furthermore, in T4.9, Generators are required to inform the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) of any proposed maintenance on a Generating Unit or other relevant Facilities or Equipment of the Power Station that affects the availability of Ancillary Services provided by that specific Generating Unit.

(d) The procedures for reviewing the Transmission System Maintenance Schedule by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), in consultation with the Transmission System Owner.

(e) The formal procedure for submitting maintenance proposals by Transmission-connected Consumers to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC).

(f) The procedures and timeline sequence for the issuance of the GUMS and TSMS by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC).

<!-- PAGE 128 -->

Edition 1.1.0 | T4 – Maintenance Scheduling Rules | Page 128

T4 applies to all proposed Maintenances that may affect the Loading Capacity of any element of the Transmission System.

### T4.3 SCOPE OF APPLICATION

**T4.3.1** Maintenance Scheduling applies to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and to the following entities, each of which is a User under T4:
* (a) Producers operating Medium Power and Large Power Generating Stations,
* (b) Producers operating Small Power Generating Stations, at the reasonable discretion of the TSOC,
* (c) Transmission System Owner,
* (d) Distribution System Operator,
* (e) Consumers connected to the Transmission System.

**T4.3.2** For Electricity Storage Facilities connected to the Transmission System, the Storage Facility Operator or the Storage Representative shall provide notification at least 30 calendar days in advance regarding the maintenance scheduling of the facilities they represent and its duration. The TSOC may, at its discretion, request the rescheduling of maintenance in cases where the outage of these facilities may affect the safe operation of the Transmission System.

**T4.3.3** Provisions corresponding to those set out in T4.4 and T4.7 may also be applied to specific Electricity Storage Facilities or portfolios thereof, at the discretion of the TSOC.

---

### T4.4 GENERATING UNIT MAINTENANCE SCHEDULING

**T4.4.1** Throughout T4, the current year is designated as Year 0, the following year as Year 1, and so on. The maintenance scheduling process for a Generating Unit commences at the latest two years prior to the Scheduled Operational Date or the date specified in relevant contracts, whichever occurs later.

**T4.4.2** For the rolling forward of the Generating Unit Maintenance Schedule from one year to the next, for each year except the initial year in the scheduling process, the following apply:
* (a) The proposals submitted by each Producer for Year 2 reflect its current Indicative Maintenance Schedule for Year 3.
* (b) The proposals submitted by each Producer for Year 1 reflect its current Provisional Maintenance Schedule for Year 2.

In the above process, exceptional changes may be made when the Producer adjusts schedules according to prevailing conditions. In cases where changes are made, the Producer is not obliged to provide explanations unless requested by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC). The overall Generating Unit Maintenance Schedule is derived from the Maintenance Schedules of the Producers and comprises the Approved Generating Unit Maintenance Schedule (AGUMS), the Provisional

<!-- PAGE 129 -->

**Edition 1.1.0** | **T4 – Maintenance Planning Rules** | **Page 129**

### Generation Unit Maintenance Program (PGUMP) and Indicative Generation Unit Maintenance Program (IGUMP)

**T4.4.3** By September 1st of Year 0, with the possibility of extension if deemed feasible by TSOC, Generators shall draft for each of their Generation Units detailed Maintenance schedules and estimated values for the Probability of Unplanned Outages, which shall be included in the following:

* **(a)** The **Proposed Generation Unit Maintenance Program** for Year 1, which is submitted to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) by September 1st of Year 0 and, if approved, is deemed the **Approved Generation Unit Maintenance Program (AGUMP)**. This program, apart from the initial year in the planning process, is based on the Provisional Generation Unit Maintenance Program of the previous year for Year 2, provided that over time these two programs refer to the same time period, as well as on any modifications by the Generator to adapt to prevailing conditions.

* **(b)** The **Provisional Generation Unit Maintenance Program (PGUMP)** for Year 2, which is submitted to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus immediately upon request.

* **(c)** The **Indicative Generation Unit Maintenance Program (IGUMP)** for Years 3 through 5, which is submitted to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus immediately upon request.

Generators shall specify for each Generation Unit the start date and time, as well as the duration of each Maintenance. In the AGUMP and PGUMP submitted by Generators, Maintenance is not permitted to be scheduled during time periods declared by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus in accordance with T4.6 as Maintenance Embargo Periods for Power Adequacy Reasons of the Electrical System. For the same reasons, Generators shall, to the extent possible, avoid concurrent maintenance of their Generation Units, as this may lead to power adequacy issues and uneconomic operation of the Transmission System, even during periods of lower overall Demand.

**T4.4.4** For the planning of Maintenances, as well as for any other matter covered under T4, the Generator shall act with prudence and in good faith. In this context, the Generator is obligated to act in accordance with Good Industry Practice for the planning of its Maintenances. Specifically, situations must be avoided where a Generator is forced to perform Maintenance within a short period of time because it was not properly planned in accordance with Good Industry Practice, such as cases where reasonable foresight was not exercised for Maintenance that ought to have been performed based on the number of operations or operating hours since the last maintenance of its machinery.

**T4.4.5** With the exception of cases where it is documented that a specific Maintenance cannot be rescheduled, Generators shall also submit the following details for Maintenances proposed for inclusion in the AGUMP, PGUMP, and IGUMP:

* **(a)** preferred alternative periods for each Maintenance within the respective year.

<!-- PAGE 130 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T4 – Maintenance Scheduling Rules** | **Page 130**

---

* (b) minimum acceptable Maintenance duration, if this is less than the proposed Maintenance duration;
* (c) cases in which the simultaneous Maintenance of two or more Generating Units of the same Producer is necessary, desirable, impossible, or undesirable;
* (d) priority order of Maintenances proposed by the Producer;
* (e) Maintenances for which specific reasons exist that make their execution necessary within the year for which they are proposed;
* (f) Maintenances whose timing of execution depends on the operating hours of the Generating Unit, equivalent operating hours, number of starts, or other data dependent on the dispatch of the Generating Unit.

---

**T4.4.6** For years 3 to 5, it is necessary to provide the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) with indicative data regarding forecasted long-duration Maintenances, so that significant Maintenances that could impact Power Adequacy or the maintenance and development programs of the Transmission System can be identified in advance. For the rolling forward of Generating Unit Maintenance Scheduling from one year to the next, Producers are not restricted in submitting their proposals by previous Indicative Maintenance Schedules. However, in all cases, effort must be made to compile Schedules that are as accurate as possible.

**T4.4.7** During the time period between September 1st and October 10th of Year 0, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) prepares a safe operation study for the period of the following year and for years 2–5 if deemed necessary, based on the proposed Maintenances and other relevant factors such as Outages or potential faults of other Generating Units, maintenance or potential faults of the Transmission System, system load growth, and security of fuel supply. If the proposed Maintenances of the Producers are likely to have negative impacts on Power Adequacy, economic operation, or operational security of the Power System, then the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) must highlight these impacts to all Producers.

**T4.4.8** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) must inform all Producers by October 20th of Year 0 of any reservations it has regarding the Generating Unit Maintenance Scheduling.

**T4.4.9** During the time period between October 20th and November 1st of Year 0, all reservations of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) must be announced to the Producers. The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) must consult with the Producers to address the identified issues. If a mutually acceptable solution has not been found by December 1st of Year 0 and, in the assessment of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), a capacity deficit will exist that affects the smooth economic or technical operation of the Power System in Year 1, then the Transmission System Operator...

<!-- PAGE 131 -->

**Revision 1.1.0** | **T4 – Maintenance Scheduling Rules** | **Page 131**

---

... Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) issues a Power Adequacy Shortage Warning and informs CERA.

**T4.4.10** By the 20th of December of Year 0, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall issue for each Producer the Generating Unit Maintenance Schedule for Year 1 and, if deemed necessary, for Year 2, which includes the Approved Generation Maintenance Schedule (AGMS) for Year 1. The schedule issued by the TSOC is final, and Producers are bound to adhere to it unless safety reasons concerning Facilities or personnel arise.

---

### T4.5 TRANSMISSION SYSTEM MAINTENANCE SCHEDULING

**T4.5.1** The maintenance scheduling process for a Transmission Asset shall commence at the latest one year prior to the Scheduled Operational Date or the date specified in relevant contracts, whichever occurs later.

**T4.5.2.1** For the roll-forward of the Transmission System Maintenance Schedule from one year to the next, for every year except the initial year in the scheduling process, the proposals submitted by the Transmission System Owner for Year 2 shall reflect its current Indicative Transmission System Maintenance Schedule (ITSMS) for Year 3.

**T4.5.2.2** For the roll-forward of the Transmission System Maintenance Schedule from one year to the next, for every year except the initial year in the scheduling process, the proposals submitted by the Transmission System Owner for Year 1 shall reflect its current Provisional Transmission System Maintenance Schedule (PTSMS) for Year 2.

In the above two cases, exceptional changes may be made when the Transmission System Owner adjusts the schedules according to prevailing conditions. When changes are made, the Transmission System Owner is not obligated to provide explanations unless requested by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC).

**T4.5.3** The Transmission System Maintenance Schedule consists of the Approved Transmission System Maintenance Schedule (ATSMS), the Provisional Transmission System Maintenance Schedule (PTSMS), and the Indicative Transmission System Maintenance Schedule (ITSMS) of the Transmission System.

**T4.5.4** By the 1st of September of Year 0, the Transmission System Owner shall submit to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) for each Transmission Asset the maintenance schedules together with its estimates for Failure Probabilities, which include the following:

* **(a)** Proposed Transmission System Maintenance Schedule for Year 1, which, upon approval, shall be considered the Approved Transmission System Maintenance Schedule (ATSMS). Except for the initial year in the scheduling process, this schedule shall be based on the PTSMS of the previous year for Year 2, given that over time these two schedules refer to the same...

<!-- PAGE 132 -->

**Document Version:** 1.1.0 | **Section:** T4 – Maintenance Scheduling Rules | **Page:** 132

***

...time period, as well as to any changes made by the Transmission System Owner to adapt to prevailing conditions.

(b) **Provisional Transmission System Maintenance Program (PTSMP)** for Year 2, which shall be submitted to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) immediately upon request.

(c) **Indicative Transmission System Maintenance Program (ITSMP)** for Years 3 to 5, which shall be submitted to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) immediately upon request.

The Transmission System Owner shall define the start date, start time, and duration of each Maintenance for each Transmission System Element.

**T4.5.5**  
For the scheduling of Maintenances, as well as for any other matter dealt with in Section T4.5, the Transmission System Owner shall act prudently and in good faith. In this sense, the Transmission System Owner is obligated to act in accordance with the rules of Good Industry Practice for the scheduling of its Maintenances. In particular, situations must be avoided where the Transmission System Owner is compelled to execute a Maintenance within a short period of time because it was not properly scheduled in accordance with the rules of Good Industry Practice, such as in cases where prior foresight was not exercised for a Maintenance that should have been performed based on the number of equipment operations or the operating time elapsed since its last maintenance.

**T4.5.6**  
With the exception of cases where it is documented that a specific Maintenance cannot be deferred, the Transmission System Owner shall also submit the following data for Maintenances proposed for inclusion in the Annual Transmission System Maintenance Program (ATSMP), the PTSMP, and the ITSMP:

(a) preferred alternative periods for each Maintenance within the corresponding year;  
(b) minimum acceptable Maintenance duration, if shorter than the proposed Maintenance duration;  
(c) cases in which:  
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;(i) the simultaneous Maintenance of two or more Transmission Elements is necessary or desirable;  
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;(ii) the simultaneous Maintenance of two or more Transmission Elements would reasonably be expected, but for specific reasons is impossible or undesirable;  
(d) order of priority of the Maintenances proposed by the Transmission System Owner;  
(e) Maintenances for which special reasons exist that render their execution necessary within the year for which they are proposed;  
(f) Maintenances whose time of execution depends on the operating hours of the Transmission Element, the number of its operations, or other data dependent on the utilization of the Transmission Element.

**T4.5.7**  
For Years 3 to 5, it is necessary to disclose indicative data to the TSOC regarding foreseen long-duration Maintenances, so that major Maintenances that could impact Power Adequacy or the safe and economic operation of the Transmission System can be identified in advance. For the roll-over of the Transmission System Maintenance Scheduling from one year...

<!-- PAGE 133 -->

**Issue 1.1.0** | **T4 – Maintenance Scheduling Rules**  
**Page 133**

---

...in the following year, the Transmission System Owner is not restricted, when submitting its proposals, by the previous Draft New Five-Year Transmission System Maintenance Programs (ENPSSM).

**T4.5.8** In the period between September 1st and October 10th of Year 0, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) conducts an operational security study for the period of the following year (Year 1) and also for Years 2 to 5 if deemed necessary, based on the proposed Maintenances and other relevant factors such as Maintenances or potential outages/faults of Generation Units and system load growth. If the proposed Maintenances of the Transmission System Owner are likely to have adverse impacts on operational security or the economic operation of the system, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall highlight these impacts to the Transmission System Owner.

**T4.5.9** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall inform the Transmission System Owner by October 20th of Year 0 regarding any reservations it holds concerning the Transmission System Maintenance Schedule.

**T4.5.10** In the period between October 20th and November 1st of Year 0, all reservations of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) must be communicated to the Transmission System Owner. The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall consult with the Transmission System Owner to resolve the identified problems.

**T4.5.11** By December 20th of Year 0, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall issue to the Transmission System Owner the Transmission System Maintenance Schedule for Year 1, and for Years 2 to 5 if deemed necessary. The Transmission System Maintenance Schedule includes the Approved Annual Transmission System Maintenance Program (EGPSSM) for Year 1, the Preliminary Multi-Year Transmission System Maintenance Program (PPSSM) for Year 2, and the Draft New Five-Year Transmission System Maintenance Program (ENPSSM) for Years 3 to 5.

**T4.5.12 Consumer Maintenance Scheduling**

**T4.5.12.1** By September 1st of Year 0, Consumers connected to the Transmission System shall submit to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) their proposals for the following:

(a) Maintenances for Year 1. Except for the initial year in the scheduling procedure, these are based on the previous year's proposals for Year 2, provided that over time these two proposals refer to the same time period, and on any modifications by Consumers connected to the Transmission System to adapt to prevailing conditions.  
(b) Maintenances for Year 2, if deemed necessary.  
(c) Maintenances for Years 3 to 5, if deemed necessary.

**T4.5.12.2** Consumers connected to the Transmission System shall specify for each of their Maintenances the start date and time as well as its duration.

**T4.5.12.3** When Consumers connected to the Transmission System propose their Maintenances, they shall also submit the following data, except in cases where it is documented that a specific Maintenance cannot be rescheduled:

<!-- PAGE 134 -->

**Version 1.1.0 | T4 – Maintenance Planning Rules**
**Page 134**

(a) Preferred alternative periods for each Maintenance within the corresponding year.

(b) The minimum acceptable duration of Maintenance, if this is shorter than the proposed Maintenance duration.

(c) Maintenances for which special reasons exist making their execution necessary within the year for which they are proposed.

---

### T4.5.12.4
The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus shall inform each Consumer connected to the Transmission System by the 20th of October of Year 0 regarding any reservations it holds concerning the proposals submitted for Maintenance. This process may be used by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus for coordinating different requested maintenances.

### T4.5.12.5
During the period between the 20th of October and the 1st of November of Year 0, all reservations of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus must be communicated to each Consumer connected to the Transmission System. The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus shall consult with each Consumer connected to the Transmission System to address the identified issues.

### T4.5.12.6
By the 20th of December of Year 0, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus issues to each Consumer connected to the Transmission System its Maintenance schedule for Year 1 and for Years 2 to 5, if deemed necessary.

---

## T4.6 POWER ADEQUACY ASSESSMENT

In order for the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus to assess Power Adequacy, it determines the variation in Demand by applying appropriate Demand forecasting methods and takes into account the submitted and estimated User Maintenance Schedules and the Probabilities of Unplanned Outages. The precise methodology followed is based on the provisions of the relevant European Regulations and the corresponding methodologies applied by ENTSO-E, and is described in a relevant Technical Instruction of TSOC, which is submitted to CERA for approval.

### T4.6.1
For the period of 3 to 5 years, if deemed necessary by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus, based on the proposals for Maintenance and the Probabilities of Unplanned Outages potentially submitted by Users, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus estimates for Maintenance Schedules, as well as the available Demand forecasts, it prepares an Availability forecast, a Demand forecast, and a Power Adequacy Index for each weekly peak of each year.

The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus may designate as a Maintenance Prohibition Period any time period within Years 3 to 5, if, in its judgment, this is required to ensure the secure operation of the system during that time period.

<!-- PAGE 135 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T4 – Maintenance Scheduling Rules** | **Page 135**

---

### T4.6.2
For Year 2, if deemed necessary, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), based on the proposals for Maintenances and the Probabilities of Unplanned Outages submitted by Users, as well as the available Demand forecasts, prepares forecasts of Availability, Demand, Operating Margin, and the Capacity Adequacy Index for each weekly peak of Year 2. If, for any week, there are indications of a capacity deficit, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) issues the corresponding Capacity Adequacy Warning.

The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may suspend any of its previous decisions designating any time period within Year 2 as a Maintenance Embargo Period, provided that, in its judgment, this is no longer deemed necessary for ensuring the safe operation of the system during that time period.

### T4.6.3
For Year 1, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), based on the proposals for Maintenances and the Probabilities of Unplanned Outages submitted by Users, as well as the available Demand forecasts, prepares forecasts of Availability, Demand, Operating Margin, and the Capacity Adequacy Index for each weekly peak of Year 1. If, for any week, there are indications of a capacity deficit, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) issues the corresponding Capacity Adequacy Warning.

The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may suspend any of its previous decisions designating any time period within Year 1 as a Maintenance Embargo Period, provided that, in its judgment, this is no longer deemed necessary for ensuring the safe operation of the system during that time period.

### T4.6.4
The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may suspend any of its previous decisions designating any time period within the Maintenance Embargo Period as a Maintenance Embargo Period, provided that, in its judgment, this is no longer deemed necessary for ensuring the safe operation of the system during that time period.

---

## T4.7 CHANGES TO THE APPROVED GENERATING UNIT MAINTENANCE SCHEDULE DURING THE IMPLEMENTATION YEAR

### T4.7.1
The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and any Producer may at any time request a change to the Maintenance of a Generating Unit included in the Approved Generating Unit Maintenance Schedule or the addition of a new maintenance to this schedule.

### T4.7.2 Request for Change by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC)

#### T4.7.2.1
The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may at any time request from a Producer a change in the execution time or duration of any Maintenance of one of its Generating Units that has been included in the relevant Approved Generating Unit Maintenance Schedule.

#### T4.7.2.2
A Producer may reject the request for change or accept it (in which case the appropriate change will be made to the Approved Generating Unit Maintenance Schedule, (AGUMS)). Producers are required to pay...

<!-- PAGE 136 -->

**Edition 1.1.0** | **T4 – Maintenance Scheduling Rules** | **Page 136**

---

...every possible effort to cooperate on the changes requested by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC).

**T4.7.2.3** If a Producer states that it agrees to the change subject to specific conditions, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may either accept the conditions set by the Producer and confirm the agreement, or reject this agreement. In the event that the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) accepts the agreement, the appropriate modification will be made to the Approved Generation Unit Maintenance Program (AGUMP). In the event that the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) refuses to agree to the Producer's terms, negotiations on the terms may take place in order to agree on a mutually acceptable alternative solution.

**T4.7.2.4** If no agreement is reached between the Producer and TSOC, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall make the final decision, which is final and binding on Producers, unless reasons relating to the operational safety of Facilities or personnel apply.

### T4.7.3 Request for Change by a Producer

**T4.7.3.1** Producers may at any time request from the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) a change to the execution timing or duration of any Maintenance of one of their Generation Units that has been incorporated into the relevant Approved Maintenance Program.

**T4.7.3.2** When a Producer proposes a modification to the Approved Generation Unit Maintenance Program (AGUMP), the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall assess, within a reasonable period, whether the proposed modification may adversely impact Power Adequacy or the safe operation of the Transmission System, taking into account the extent of the change and the execution timing of the Maintenance.

**T4.7.3.3** When the proposed modification, in its judgment and in accordance with T4.6, will not result in adverse impacts on Power Adequacy or the safe operation of the Transmission System, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall appropriately amend the Approved Generation Unit Maintenance Program (AGUMP) and inform the Producer of the acceptance of its request.

**T4.7.3.4** When the proposed modification, in the judgment of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and in accordance with T4.6, will cause adverse impacts on Power Adequacy or create issues for the safe and economical operation of the Transmission System, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall not modify the Approved Generation Unit Maintenance Program (AGUMP) and shall inform the Producer that its proposal is not accepted. The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall enter into negotiations with the Producer, upon the Producer's request, to determine a mutually acceptable alternative modification that satisfies the Producer's requirements without causing unacceptable impacts on Power Adequacy or the safe operation of the Transmission System. If the Producer desires an alternative modification, it shall submit its application for change following the procedure specified in T4.7.3.1.

<!-- PAGE 137 -->

**Version 1.1.0**  
**T4 – Maintenance Scheduling Rules**  
**Page 137**

***

**T4.7.3.5** In the event that a Producer, having been informed that their application for a change to the Approved Generation Unit Maintenance Schedule (AGUMS) is not accepted, considers that their Generation Unit must be placed in an unavailable status due to technical problems or for safety reasons, the Producer shall immediately inform the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) following the procedure for submitting Unavailability Declarations (Total or Partial), as described in the Electricity Market Rules.

**T4.8 CHANGES TO THE APPROVED TRANSMISSION SYSTEM MAINTENANCE SCHEDULE DURING THE IMPLEMENTATION YEAR**

**T4.8.1** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), the Transmission System Owner, and any Consumer connected to the Transmission System may at any time request a change to a Maintenance included in the Approved Transmission System Maintenance Schedule (ATSMS) or the addition of a new Maintenance to this schedule.

**T4.8.2** Request for change by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC):

(a) The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may at any time request from the Transmission System Owner, and any Consumer connected to the Transmission System, a change in the execution time or duration of any Maintenance of an Asset of the Transmission System or of any Consumer connected to the Transmission System, which has been included in the Approved Transmission System Maintenance Schedule (ATSMS).

(b) The Transmission System Owner or the Consumer connected to the Transmission System may reject the request for change or accept it (in which case the appropriate modification will be made to the Approved Transmission System Maintenance Schedule, ATSMS). The Transmission System Owner, and any Consumer connected to the Transmission System, shall make every reasonable effort to cooperate regarding changes requested by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC).

(c) If the Transmission System Owner or the Consumer connected to the Transmission System declares agreement with the change but subject to specific conditions, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may accept the conditions set by the Transmission System Owner or the Consumer connected to the Transmission System and confirm the agreement, or may refuse this agreement.

In the event that the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) accepts the agreement, the appropriate modification shall be made to the Approved Transmission System Maintenance Schedule (ATSMS). In the event that the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) refuses to agree to the terms of the Transmission System Owner, or any Consumer connected to the Transmission System, consultations may take place regarding the relevant terms in order to agree upon a mutually acceptable alternative solution.

**T4.8.3** Request for change by the Transmission System Owner

<!-- PAGE 138 -->

| **Edition 1.1.0** | **T4 – Maintenance Scheduling Rules** | **Page 138** |

The Transmission System Owner may at any time request from the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) a change in the execution time or duration of any Maintenance of a Transmission System Element that has been included in the Approved Transmission System Maintenance Plan (ATSMP).

### T4.8.3.1
When the Transmission System Owner proposes a change to the Approved Transmission System Maintenance Plan (ATSMP), the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall assess, within a reasonable period of time, whether the proposed change is likely to have an adverse impact on the safe and economic operation of the Transmission System, taking into account the scope of the change and the execution time of the Maintenance. When the proposed change, in the judgment of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), is not expected to cause an adverse impact on the safe and economic operation of the Transmission System, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall appropriately modify the Approved Transmission System Maintenance Plan (ATSMP) and inform the Transmission System Owner of the acceptance of their request.

### T4.8.3.2
When the proposed change, in the judgment of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), is likely to create problems for the safe and economic operation of the Transmission System, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall not modify the Approved Transmission System Maintenance Plan (ATSMP) and shall inform the Transmission System Owner that their proposal is not acceptable. The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall enter into negotiations with the Transmission System Owner, if requested by the latter, to determine a mutually acceptable alternative modification that satisfies the requirements of the Transmission System Owner and does not have an adverse impact on the safe and economic operation of the Transmission System. In the event that the Transmission System Owner desires an alternative modification, they shall submit an application for this change.

### T4.8.3.3
In the event that the Transmission System Owner, having been informed that their application for a change to the Approved Transmission System Maintenance Plan (ATSMP) was not accepted, considers that the Transmission System Element must be placed in an unavailable status due to technical problems or for safety reasons, the Transmission System Owner shall immediately notify the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC).

### T4.8.4 Request for change from a Consumer connected to the Transmission System

A Consumer connected to the Transmission System may at any time request from the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) a change in the execution time or duration of any Maintenance of their installations.

### T4.8.4.1
When a Consumer connected to the Transmission System proposes a change to a Maintenance of their installations, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall assess, within a reasonable period of time, whether the proposed change is likely to have an adverse impact on the safe

<!-- PAGE 139 -->

**Edition 1.1.0** | **T4 – Maintenance Scheduling Rules** | **Page 139**

---

...and economic operation of the Transmission System, taking into account the extent of the change and the execution time of the Maintenance.

**T4.8.4.2** When the proposed change, in the judgment of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), will not adversely affect the safe and economic operation of the Transmission System, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall appropriately amend the corresponding maintenance schedule and inform the Consumer connected to the Transmission System regarding the acceptance of their request.

**T4.8.4.3** When the proposed change, in the judgment of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), is likely to create problems for the safe and economic operation of the Transmission System, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall not modify the corresponding maintenance schedule and shall inform the Consumer connected to the Transmission System that their proposal is not accepted. The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) is obligated to enter into negotiations with the Consumer connected to the Transmission System, if the latter so requests, in order to determine a mutually acceptable alternative modification that meets the requirements of the Consumer Connected to the Transmission System without adversely affecting the safe and economic operation of the Transmission System. In the event that the Consumer connected to the Transmission System desires an alternative modification, they shall submit an application for such a change.

### T4.9 OTHER INFORMATION PROVIDED

**T4.9.1** Users shall inform the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) of any proposed maintenance of equipment not included in the Maintenance Schedule, which may impact the availability of Ancillary Services. It is emphasized that this notification does not negate the obligations of Users to submit Non-Availability Declarations under the Electricity Market Rules, nor does it substitute for such Declarations.

**T4.9.2** When operational security or security of supply of the Transmission System is at risk of being disrupted, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may request modifications to the maintenance schedule notified in accordance with T4. The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall make every reasonable effort for the timely notification of these modifications. When the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) requests modifications for this reason, Users shall make every reasonable effort to comply with the request of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) so that a final schedule for these maintenance operations is achieved.

**T4.9.3** The Distribution System Operator (DSO) shall cooperate with the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) at all stages of maintenance scheduling to improve Generation Adequacy and ensure the safe operation of the system.

**T4.9.4** In the event that the Distribution System Operator (DSO) schedules Operations on the Distribution System that are expected to cause...

<!-- PAGE 140 -->

...load reduction exceeding five (5) MW, shall immediately and with due justification inform the Transmission System Operator.

\<End of T4\>

# TRANSMISSION RULES

## T5 – ANCILLARY SERVICES

### Version 1.1.0

---

| Version 1.1.0 | T5 – Ancillary Services <br> Page 141 |
| :--- | :--- |

<!-- PAGE 142 -->

# T5 – ANCILLARY SERVICES

## T5.1 INTRODUCTION

### T5.1.1
Ancillary Services are services additional to the provision of electrical power, the supply of electrical energy, and the transmission and distribution of this energy, which are necessary for the smooth operation of a stable and reliable Power System. Ancillary Services ensure the response capability of the Power System both under normal operating conditions and during disturbances.

### T5.1.2
The constituent parts of Ancillary Services are the following:
(a) Frequency Control
(b) Voltage Regulation
(c) Re-start after Total Blackout

### T5.1.3
To ensure safe and reliable operation, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) has supervisory control over all Ancillary Services, meaning that the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall determine which Ancillary Services must be provided, when, and by whom, on the basis of the provisions set out in T5 and the Electricity Market Rules.

### T5.1.4
It is clarified that the provisions of T5 regarding Ancillary Services cover, without being limited to, the services provided in accordance with the Electricity Market Rules, both within the framework of the Integrated Scheduling Process and the Real-Time Balancing Market.

### T5.1.5
The procedures for Monitoring, Testing, and Investigation regarding Ancillary Services are set out in Chapter T11 of the Transmission Rules.

## T5.2 SCOPE OF APPLICATION

### T5.2.1
T5 applies to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and to the following, each of whom is a User in accordance with T5:
(a) Balancing Service Providers
(b) Consumers connected to the Transmission System
(c) Distribution System Operator
(d) Transmission System Owner

In addition, T5 is applied by the Market Operator to the extent that it relates to interface matters between the provisions hereof and the Electricity Market Rules.

## T5.3 FREQUENCY CONTROL

### T5.3.1 INTRODUCTION

#### T5.3.1.1
To maintain the security and integrity of the Transmission System, both under normal operation and during disturbances, it is necessary for the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) to operate the Transmission System in such a manner that adequate Frequency Control is provided with the objective of achieving operation within acceptable limits of...

<!-- PAGE 143 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T5 – Ancillary Services**  
**Page 143**

---

Frequency at any given point in time. Frequency Control is achieved through the management of the Operating Margin by the TSOC.

### T5.3.2 OBJECTIVE

The objective of T5.3 is to establish the required procedures that will enable the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) to achieve and maintain the balance of the active power balance in the Power System.

### T5.3.3 DEFINITIONS RELATING TO FREQUENCY CONTROL

**T5.3.3.1** An Incident that causes a Frequency deviation outside the normal operation range as defined in T1.8.2.1 is designated as a Disturbance Event.

**T5.3.3.2** Users providing Frequency Control, specifically Fast Frequency Response (FFR) Reserve, Frequency Containment Reserve (FCR), Frequency Restoration Reserve (FRR), and Replacement Reserve (RR), constitute Balancing Service Providers (BSPs).

**T5.3.3.2** A Disturbance Event is designated as a Severe Disturbance Event if the frequency deviates beyond ±0.50 Hz from the Target Frequency.

**T5.3.3.3** The Time of Occurrence of a Disturbance Event is defined as the last point in time at which the Frequency deviates from the normal operation range, as defined in T1.8.2.1, prior to the time at which the minimum/maximum Frequency value is recorded.

**T5.3.3.4** The Pre-Disturbance Frequency Value is defined as the average value of the Transmission System Frequency within the time interval between 60 and 30 seconds prior to the occurrence of the Disturbance Event.

**T5.3.3.5** The Pre-Disturbance Active Power Generation/Injection or Demand/Absorption by a User is defined as the average value of Active Power within the time interval between 60 and 30 seconds prior to the occurrence of the Disturbance Event.

### T5.3.4 OPERATING MARGIN

#### T5.3.4.1 Description

**T5.3.4.1.1** To cover deviations in Generation or Demand forecasting and/or losses of Generation or Demand from the Transmission and/or Distribution System, under both normal operating conditions and system disturbances, an Operating Margin for reserve must be maintained during the operation of the Power System so that adequate Frequency Control is achieved. The Operating Margin is the necessary available amount of Active Power reserve (upward or downward) after covering the expected Power System Demand.

**T5.3.4.1.2** Balancing requirements between Generation and Demand require the utilization of the Operating Margin in order to correct Frequency deviations. This correction is achieved by adjusting the Active Power output of Generating Units and Energy Storage Facilities, as well as the Active Power absorption by...

<!-- PAGE 144 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T5 – Ancillary Services** | **Page 144**

---

...Direct Control Loads or Electricity Storage Facilities and through Demand Shedding by the TSOC.

**T5.3.4.1.3** Section T5.3.4 describes the different types of reserves that the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may utilize as part of the Operating Margin, along with the technical requirements governing the provision of these reserves.

**T5.3.4.1.4** The minimum requirements for the provision of reserves by Users are specified in the Operating Margin Policy and in paragraph T5.3.4 of this Chapter.

**T5.3.4.1.5** The Operating Margin consists of:
(i) Operating Reserve, which includes:
* Fast Frequency Response Reserve (FFR)
* Frequency Containment Reserve (FCR)
* Frequency Restoration Reserve (FRR)
(ii) Replacement Reserve (RR) (spinning and non-spinning)
(iii) Contingency Reserve

**T5.3.4.1.6** The type and minimum number of Balancing Service Providers (BSPs) providing each type of reserve included in the Operating Margin are specified in the Operating Margin Policy.

### T5.3.4.2 Fast Frequency Response Reserve (FFR)

**T5.3.4.2.1** Fast Frequency Response Reserve (FFR) is the total Active Power reserves available prior to the activation of FCR to contain the Frequency following a Disturbance Event.

**T5.3.4.2.2** Fast Frequency Response Reserve (FFR) is provided through the automatic adjustment of Active Power injection/withdrawal of Generation / Demand Entities.

**T5.3.4.2.3** The main technical requirements for Fast Frequency Response Reserve are specified in the Operating Margin Policy.

### T5.3.4.3 Frequency Containment Reserve (FCR)

**T5.3.4.3.1** Frequency Containment Reserve (FCR) is the total Active Power reserves available to contain the Frequency following the occurrence of a power balance imbalance. Since Frequency may deviate in either an upward or downward direction, upward and downward Frequency Containment Reserve is defined in each case.

**T5.3.4.3.2** Frequency Containment Reserve is provided both under normal operating conditions and during a Disturbance Event, and is achieved through automatic corrective actions to Frequency fluctuations as follows:
(a) The automatic adjustment of the Active Power output of Generating Units via their speed governor, or the corresponding adjustment of Active Power injection/withdrawal of Electricity Storage Facilities...

<!-- PAGE 145 -->

(b) Automatic regulation of Active Power absorption of AZ Loads
(c) Automatic load shedding
(d) The operation of other automatic corrective mechanisms

**T5.3.4.3.3** The main technical requirements for Frequency Containment Reserve (FCR) are specified in the Operational Margin Policy.

---

### T5.3.4.4 Requirements for Speed Governors

**T5.3.4.4.1** To provide adequate capability for automatic adjustment of the Active Power output of Generating Units at all times, Producers must comply with the terms of T5.3.4.4, where the capability exists and as required by the TSOC.

**T5.3.4.4.2** In any case other than those permitted under T5.3.4.4:

* (a) Generating Units synchronized with the Transmission System shall be capable of operating continuously under speed governor control within the framework of normal operation of the Real-Time Balancing Market, or as requested by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus. In the case of such operation, the relevant characteristics of the Generating Units must remain within the appropriate range of values as defined in the Connection Conditions to the Transmission System.
* (b) When Generating Units operate under speed governor control, no time delays shall be introduced into their response, other than those inherent to the design of their speed governors.
* (c) Operation of speed governors with deadbands to Frequency fluctuations may be applied following a relevant Instruction from the TSOC.

**T5.3.4.4.3** The Producer may restrict the operation of the Speed Governor, by way of exception to the terms of T5.3.4.4.2, in the following cases:

* (a) When necessary for personnel safety reasons and/or to avoid damage to the Generating Unit Facilities. In this case, the Producer shall immediately inform the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus regarding the required restriction.
* (b) To ensure the safe and reliable operation of the Generating Unit, applying the rules of Good Industry Practice.
* (c) When there is a prior relevant agreement for the restriction between the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus and the Producer.
* (d) When the restriction is performed pursuant to a relevant Instruction issued by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus.

**T5.3.4.4.4** In the cases of T5.3.4.4.3, when the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus agrees to the restriction of the operation of the Speed Governor or issues an Instruction for such restriction, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus shall record the nature of the restriction, the reasons that caused it, its time, and its duration.

---
*Edition 1.1.0 | T5 – Ancillary Services | Page 145*

<!-- PAGE 146 -->

Version 1.1.0 T5 – Ancillary Services  
Page 146

### T5.3.4.5 Frequency Restoration Reserve (FRR)

**T5.3.4.5.1** The Frequency Restoration Reserve (FRR) is the total Active Power reserves available for regulating the Frequency of the Power System under normal operating conditions and for restoring the Frequency of the Power System to the Target Frequency following a Disturbance Incident. Frequency Restoration Reserve may be upward or downward and is distinguished into automatic (aFRR) and manual (mFRR), according to its activation mode.

**T5.3.4.5.2** Automatic Frequency Restoration Reserve (aFRR) is achieved by adjusting the Active Power Generation and/or Demand (MW) of Balancing Service Providers and/or other Consumers, compared to the pre-Disturbance Incident Generation and/or Demand via:
* (a) The automatic adjustment of the Active Power of Balancing Service Providers operating under Automatic Generation Control (AGC)
* (b) The automatic shedding of Demand
* (c) The operation of other corrective mechanisms

**T5.3.4.5.3** Manual Frequency Restoration Reserve (mFRR) is achieved by adjusting the Active Power Generation and/or Demand (MW) of Balancing Service Providers and/or other Consumers, compared to the pre-Disturbance Incident Generation and/or Demand via:
* (a) The manual adjustment of the Active Power of Balancing Service Providers as a result of Instructions issued by TSOC
* (b) The manual shedding of Demand
* (c) The operation of other corrective mechanisms

**T5.3.4.5.4** The main technical requirements for the Frequency Restoration Reserve are defined in the Operating Margin Policy.

---

### T5.3.4.6 Replacement Reserve (RR)

**T5.3.4.6.1** The Replacement Reserve (RR) consists of all Active Power reserves (spinning or non-spinning) available for restoring or supporting the required level of Operating Reserve.

**T5.3.4.6.2** Replacement Reserve is achieved by increasing Active Power Generation/Injection and/or reducing Demand of Balancing Service Providers or other Consumers, compared to the previous Generation/Injection and/or Demand via:
* (a) The Start-up and Synchronization (in the case of non-synchronized Generating Units) and/or the adjustment of the Active Power of Balancing Service Providers as a result of Instructions issued by TSOC
* (b) The manual shedding of Demand
* (c) The operation of other corrective mechanisms

**T5.3.4.6.3** Replacement Reserve is always upward (increase in Generation and/or decrease in Demand).

**T5.3.4.6.4** The main technical requirements for the Replacement Reserve are defined in the Operating Margin Policy.

<!-- PAGE 147 -->

**Issue 1.1.0 T5 – Ancillary Services**  
**Page 147**

### T5.3.4.7 Contingency Reserve (CR)

**T5.3.4.7.1** The Contingency Reserve (CR) consists of the additional available Generation capacity so that the requirements of Demand and Ancillary Services are covered under Extreme Power System Conditions. The Contingency Reserve may be provided by Contracted Units in accordance with the provisions of the Electricity Market Rules, in particular Chapter 5 and Annex 5.3 thereof, or by other means.

**T5.3.4.7.2** The Contingency Reserve is achieved by:
* (a) The Start-up of Contracted Units as a result of Dispatch Instructions sent by TSOC
* (b) Manual Demand shedding
* (c) The operation of other corrective mechanisms

**T5.3.4.7.3** Main technical requirements for Contingency Reserve:
* (a) The Contingency Reserve is activated in accordance with manual Demand shedding or the Dispatch Instruction sent by TSOC to the relevant Contracted Unit.
* (b) The CR becomes available and remains maintainable according to the time periods defined in the Operating Margin Policy.
* (c) The Operating Margin Policy further defines the requirements and limits for activation and the capability to provide the full capacity of Contingency Reserve.

### T5.3.4.8 Demand Shedding

**T5.3.4.8.1** In conditions of Underfrequency or violation of the Limit Rate of Change of Frequency (RoCoF), the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus is entitled, for reasons of safe operation, to follow Demand shedding procedures, so that the Rate of Change of Frequency as well as the Frequency value are restrained or restored within acceptable limits.

**T5.3.4.8.2** Automatic Demand shedding may take place:
* In Underfrequency conditions
* In conditions of violation of the Limit Rate of Change of Frequency

**T5.3.4.8.3** In addition to the automatic Demand shedding mechanisms, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus may proceed with manual Demand shedding when this is reasonably deemed necessary for reasons of maintaining the safety and reliability of the Power System.

### T5.3.4.9 Operating Margin Management

**T5.3.4.9.1** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus, using appropriate methods and studies, defines the quantities and specific requirements of the Operating Reserve (Fast Frequency Response, Frequency Containment Reserve, and Frequency Restoration Reserve), Replacement Reserve, and Contingency Reserve, required to ensure...

<!-- PAGE 148 -->

| Version 1.1.0 | T5 – Ancillary Services | Page 148 |
|---|---|---|

...smooth operation of the Power System at any given moment. This determination is implemented through the Operating Margin Policy issued by the TSOC, is not subject to constraints imposed by the Electricity Market Rules, and is carried out taking into account the relevant factors, the principal ones of which are the following:

(a) The relevant operating tactic followed by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus in each time period.  
(b) The range of permissible Demand curtailments that have been executed during the considered time period.  
(c) The time elapsed since the last Demand curtailment incident.  
(d) Special incidents at national or international level that may justify the requirement for additional Reserves.  
(e) The cost of Reserves.  
(f) The size and number of the largest supply sources in operation on the Transmission System at any given moment. Additionally, the largest quantities of Generation connected to radially supplied sections of the Transmission System.  
(g) Weather conditions affecting (directly or indirectly) the operational reliability of Generation Units, Electricity Storage Facilities, and/or the Transmission System.  
(h) The penetration of Renewable Energy Sources (RES) and the fluctuation of their Generation by technology.  
(i) The uncertainties of Demand forecasting.  
(j) The projected Frequency drop in the event of occurrence of the Reference Incident, as this may be determined through simulation using a dynamic model of the Power System.  
(k) The projected Frequency rise in the event of disconnection or shutdown of Consumer Demand installations, as this may be determined through simulation using a dynamic model of the Power System.  
(l) Historical data on the Availability and operational reliability of each Generation Unit and Electricity Storage Facility.  
(m) Observations/notices regarding the operational reliability of certain Generation Units or Electricity Storage Facilities.  
(n) The historical response of RES Producers/Units to Instructions.  
(o) Existing automatic load shedding (UFLS) schemes as well as the capability for manual Demand shedding by the NCC.  

**T5.3.4.9.2** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus shall record significant changes in the tactics followed for the Operating Margin, as defined in accordance with T5.3.4.

**T5.3.4.9.3** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus shall make every effort to perform the Dispatch of generation and harmonize the operation of the Transmission System in accordance with the tactic established for maintaining an Operating Margin for reserve in each time period.

**T5.3.4.9.4** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus is responsible for fulfilling its obligations as referred to in the preceding clauses of T5.3, such that:

(a) Power System Demand is met.

<!-- PAGE 149 -->

---
**Edition 1.1.0** | **T5 – Ancillary Services** | **Page 149**
---

(b) Ensure that the Operating Reserve levels required by the current policy followed by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) are secured.

**T5.3.4.9.5** The management scheduling of the Operating Margin is performed through the Integrated Scheduling Process and based on the results of the Replacement Reserve (RR) and Contingency Reserve auctions, as described in the Electricity Market Rules.

### T5.4 VOLTAGE CONTROL

#### T5.4.1 INTRODUCTION

**T5.4.1.1** To maintain the safe operation and reliability of the Transmission System, prevent faults in the Transmission System and in Users' Facilities, and maintain Voltages at Users' Connection Points within the limits specified in the Transmission System Connection Conditions, it is necessary for the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) to regulate the Transmission System Voltage in accordance with the limits defined in T1.8.3.1.

**T5.4.1.2** Voltage Control aims to cover the Reactive Power Demand and ensure the existence of sufficient dynamic Voltage control capability in the Transmission System, so as to accommodate any variations in Reactive Power injection or absorption and to limit the duration and extent of Voltage fluctuations under fault conditions. For these purposes, adequate static and dynamic Reactive Power reserves are required. To control Transmission System Voltages, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) implements various methods of dynamic and static control.

**T5.4.1.3** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) primarily uses the following Voltage Control methods:

* (a) Transformer tap position changing, switching in or out of cables, reactors, and capacitors, as well as other methods of utilizing Transmission System Facilities;
* (b) Changing the tap positions of generator step-up transformers;
* (c) Changing the tap positions of inter-busbar transformers;
* (d) Utilization of Users' capability for Reactive Power generation/consumption, either via the Automatic Voltage Regulators (AVR) of Generating Units or via Instructions issued by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC);
* (e) Utilization of Electricity Storage Facilities' capability for Reactive Power injection/absorption, either as described in T5.4.5.4 or via Instructions issued by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC).

#### T5.4.2 OBJECTIVE

**T5.4.2.1** The objective of T5.4 is to specify the control procedures used by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), in cooperation with Users where required, for the Voltage Control of the Transmission System.

<!-- PAGE 150 -->

**Version 1.1.0 | T5 – Ancillary Services**  
**Page 150**

---

**T5.4.2.2** T5.4 defines the required procedures (in conjunction with those of T15 and T16) that will enable the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) to achieve the following:  
(a) Maintenance of voltage stability in the Transmission System;  
(b) Maintenance of Transmission System Voltages at User Connection Points within the operational limits specified in the Connection Terms to the Transmission System.

**T5.4.2.3** T5.4 defines the procedures for the utilization of User Facilities by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), where necessary, for the purpose of Transmission System Voltage Regulation.

**T5.4.2.4** Some of the implementation procedures for Voltage Regulation methods (e.g., Reactive Power generation/absorption Instructions) are specified in the terms of T15; therefore, this T5.4 is correlated with those terms.

### T5.4.3 DESCRIPTION OF VOLTAGE REGULATION

**T5.4.3.1** Voltage Regulation is achieved by ensuring adequacy of Reactive Power, to the static and dynamic provision of which the factors referred to in T5.4.3.2 contribute. The most suitable means for regulation by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) are the Reactive Power generated/injected or absorbed by Generating Units or Electricity Storage Facilities, as well as equipment installed specifically for this purpose.

**T5.4.3.2** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall maintain adequacy for the static and dynamic provision of Reactive Power so that, during operation, Transmission System Voltages at User Connection Points are maintained within the limits specified in T1.8.3 at all times. The main factors affecting the capability to provide Reactive Power are the following:  
(a) The capacitive loading of the Transmission System.  
(b) The generation/injection or absorption of Reactive Power by Generating Units, Electricity Storage Facilities, or Consumers.  
(c) Transmission System Reactive Power losses.  
(d) Dedicated equipment for Voltage Regulation, such as capacitor banks and reactors, Reactive Power compensation systems, etc.

**T5.4.3.3** Due to the electrical characteristics of the Transmission System, the Voltage (for Facilities operating at the same nominal Voltage) will not be the same at all points of the Transmission System.

### T5.4.4 METHODS OF VOLTAGE REGULATION

**T5.4.4.1** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) regulates system Voltage to ensure the smooth operation of the Transmission System. The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) defines and appropriately amends the general procedures for Voltage Regulation in the Transmission System. Specifically, for Voltage Regulation, daily, weekly, and seasonal factors shall be taken into account, and the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall specify the following:

<!-- PAGE 151 -->

**Edition 1.1.0** | **T5 – Ancillary Services** | **Page 151**

---

(a) The target Voltage levels aiming to limit or control the effect of the Transmission System capacity  
(b) The optimal use of available equipment for Voltage Regulation, and  
(c) The dynamic reserve requirements in Reactive Power.  

### T5.4.5 IMPLEMENTATION OF VOLTAGE REGULATION METHODS

**T5.4.5.1** The specifications of T5.4.5 apply to Generating Units, excluding RES Units for which the relevant provisions of T16 apply.

The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) continuously monitors the Transmission System Voltages and determines the appropriate operational Voltage levels, taking into account T5.4.4 and in particular the conditions of the Transmission System during its operation.

**T5.4.5.2** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) regulates the System Voltage using available regulation means so that during operation, the Transmission System Voltages at Connection Points remain within the levels specified in T1.8.3, and maintains sufficient dynamic Reactive Power supply capability to cope with any changes in Transmission System operating conditions resulting from changes in Demand or structural changes in transmission and generation due to control switching operations or faults. For this purpose, modification of the Active Power Output of Generating Units may also be imposed, and/or the implementation of other measures necessary for Voltage Regulation, which may concern both Generating Units and Consumers.

**T5.4.5.3** Each Generating Unit must operate under the continuous control of the Automatic Voltage Regulator (AVR), which must maintain a constant Voltage at the output of the Generating Unit. The Producer shall not deactivate or restrict the operation of the Automatic Voltage Regulator except in the cases of T5.4.5.5, in which case they must inform the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) without delay.

**T5.4.5.4** The Producer may deactivate or restrict the operation of the Automatic Voltage Regulator only in the following cases:

(a) When this is necessary for personnel safety reasons and/or to prevent damage to the Generating Unit Facilities.  
(b) To ensure the reliable operation of the Generating Unit, applying the rules of Good Utility Practice.  
(c) When there is a prior relevant agreement with the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC).  

**T5.4.5.5** In the event that a Generating Unit is not operating under the control of the Automatic Voltage Regulator, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may impose restrictions on its operation in accordance with Good Utility Practice, provided this is deemed necessary to ensure the safe operation of the Transmission System within predefined limits. In this case, and if deemed necessary, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may also issue an Instruction for Deactivation of the Generating Unit. When a restriction needs to be imposed, the

<!-- PAGE 152 -->

**Edition 1.1.0 | T5 – Ancillary Services | Page 152**

...the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus shall consult, in the most direct practical manner, with the Producer to determine the safe operational state that would cause the minimum restriction on the operation of the Generating Unit.

**T5.4.5.6** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus issues Instructions to Users for the production/injection/consumption/absorption of Reactive Power, in accordance with the terms of T15.

**T5.4.5.7** The extent of utilization of the means for Voltage Regulation may be constrained by prevailing conditions on the Transmission System and other limitations dictated by the Facilities and Plant/Equipment.

**T5.4.5.8** In certain cases, it is necessary to alter the scheduled production/injection/absorption of Users—as determined under the procedures of the Electricity Market Rules—in order to maintain Transmission System Voltages at Connection Points within the limits specified in T1.8.3.

---

### T5.4.6 VOLTAGE REGULATION UNDER EMERGENCY CONDITIONS

**T5.4.6.1** Producers may be requested to operate certain Generating Units at Reactive Power production or absorption levels exceeding their Registered Operational Characteristics. This may occur in an emergency situation and subject to agreement between the Producer and the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus. Corresponding measures for Reactive Power production/injection or absorption regulation may also be requested from Consumers, Storage Facility Operators, or Storage Representatives.

**T5.4.6.2** Adjustments to the Transmission System Voltage may be achieved by issuing Instructions to Producers in accordance with T5.4 for the simultaneous tap position changes of Transformers of certain Generating Units. This operational practice is known as "Simultaneous Tap Changing of a Number of Transformers." In the event that the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus deems it necessary to implement this practice, Producers shall comply with its Instructions.

**T5.4.6.3** Demand shedding is permitted for the purpose of preventing Voltage drop at Connection Points below specified limits (as defined in T6).

---

### T5.5 BLACK START / SYSTEM RECOVERY AFTER TOTAL SHUTDOWN

#### T5.5.1 INTRODUCTION

**T5.5.1.1** For the restoration of Transmission System operation following a Total or Partial Blackout, it is necessary to have available predetermined Power Stations, generators within Power Stations, Electricity Storage Facilities, or portfolios thereof ("Black Start Units") possessing the capability of Starting from Shutdown State and energizing a section of the Transmission System, or Synchronizing to the Transmission System, within 45 minutes following an Instruction from the System Operator.

<!-- PAGE 153 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T5 – Ancillary Services** | **Page 153**

---

...Transmission System of Cyprus, without supply from an external electrical power source. Black Start Units are operationally managed in accordance with the Unified Action Plan for the Restoration of the Electrical System after Blackout.

### T5.5.2 OBJECTIVE

**T5.5.2.1** The objective of T5.5 is to specify the requirements regarding Black Start Units to enable the restoration of the operation of the Transmission System following a Total or Partial Blackout.

### T5.5.3 OPERATION OF BLACK START UNITS AFTER A TOTAL BLACKOUT

**T5.5.3.1** If, during the restoration of Demand supply, any Black Start Unit cannot meet the required Demand while respecting its safe operating limits, the Generators, Storage Facility Operators, or Storage Representatives shall inform the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC). To address the existing problem, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may issue Instructions for Demand shedding, Transmission System reconfiguration, or User System reconfiguration. In any case, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) accepts that any decision regarding the operation of a Black Start Unit outside its safe operating limits shall be taken solely by the Generator, the Storage Facility Operator, or the Storage Representative, who may alter its generation/injection if deemed necessary for safety reasons (relating either to personnel or to their Facilities and Equipment). In the event that such a change is made without prior notification, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) must be informed in the most immediate practical manner.

**T5.5.3.2** The operation of Black Start Units is scheduled in accordance with the provisions of the Electricity Market Rules, the requirements of T10, as well as the Unified Action Plan for the Restoration of the Electrical System after Blackout.

\<End of T5\>
