# T9 - OPERATIONAL TESTS

## T9.1 INTRODUCTION

**T9.1.1** T9 covers the responsibilities and procedures for organizing and conducting Operational Tests, which may affect the Transmission System and/or User Systems.

**T9.1.2** Operational Tests, by their nature, may involve either or both of the following two factors:
* (a) The responsibilities of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) in relation to the Transmission System, including Generation Dispatch.
* (b) The operations of Users and the quality and continuity of electricity supply to Users.

**T9.1.3** To minimize impacts on the operation of the Transmission System and User Systems, tests that affect the operation of the Transmission System or User Systems in accordance with T9.1.2 must be subject to central coordination and control.

**T9.1.4** To achieve the purpose as defined in T9.2.1, T9 establishes the procedures for describing and notifying Operational Tests.

## T9.2 PURPOSE

**T9.2.1** The primary purpose of T9 is to establish the procedures for the central coordination and control of Operational Tests required by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) or the User, where such tests may affect:
* (a) the safe operation of the Transmission System
* (b) the operation of the Transmission System or the User System to a significant extent
* (c) the economical operation of the Transmission System or the User System
* (d) the quality or continuity of electricity supply to Users
* (e) the proper functioning of the Electricity Market.

**T9.2.2** Indicatively, tests characteristic of those expected to be covered by this T9 are referred to in T9.4 to T9.5. This reference is not intended to cover all tests exhaustively, and other tests falling under the definition of Operational Tests may also be covered by the provisions of this T9.

**T9.2.3** This T9 does not deal with routine tests requested by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) to achieve compliance of Users with the design, operation, and connection requirements as defined in the Transmission Code and in each User Connection Agreement and any other agreement, or to determine whether the Generation / Demand Entities represented by Users comply with the Registered Operational Characteristics, as notified by Operational Characteristics Modification Declarations where required, or to determine whether they meet the Ancillary Services Provision Obligations.

<!-- PAGE 187 -->

**Issue 1.1.0 | T9 – Operational Tests**
*Page 187*

---

...or that it is in compliance with the Instructions. These matters are covered in T11 (Monitoring, Testing and Investigation).

### T9.3 SCOPE OF APPLICATION

The provisions of T9 apply to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and all Users, who in this T9 are defined as:

(a) Producers operating Licensed Generating Stations of Very Small Capacity and Generating Stations of Small, Medium, and Large Capacity  
(b) Storage Facility Operators connected to the Transmission System  
(c) Independent Aggregation Services Entities  
(d) Distribution System Operator (DSO)  
(e) Transmission System Owner (TSO Owner)  
(f) Consumers connected to the Transmission System  

---

### T9.4 OPERATIONAL TESTS REQUIRED BY THE TRANSMISSION SYSTEM OPERATOR OF CYPRUS (TSOC)

**T9.4.1**  
The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), as operator of the Transmission System, in accordance with Good Utility Practice, shall perform the necessary Operational Tests for the purpose of maintaining and developing operational procedures, training personnel, and gathering information regarding the behavior of the Power System under abnormal operating conditions. The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus shall restrict the frequency of execution, purpose, scope of impact, and type of Operational Tests to those required by Good Utility Practice.

**T9.4.2**  
The Operational Tests required by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus from time to time shall include, but not be limited to, the following:

(a) Tests involving controlled application of changes in Frequency and/or Voltage for the purpose of gathering information regarding the behavior of the Power System.  
(b) Power System Restoration Tests.  
(c) Tests regarding applicable procedures in Emergency States and Alert States.  
(d) Testing and monitoring procedures for Power Quality under various Power System conditions and Dispatch schedules.

**T9.4.3**  
The provisos of T9.6, T9.7, T9.8, T9.10.4, and T9.11 shall not apply to Operational Tests required by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus in accordance with T9.4.

**T9.4.4**  
Where the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus intends to conduct an Operational Test in accordance with T9.4 and, in the reasoned opinion of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus, such an Operational Test may have Operational Effects on a User System, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus shall notify the User, in accordance with the provisions of T8, regarding the planned schedule and the effects of the Operational Test reasonably expected under all conditions. Furthermore, it shall...

<!-- PAGE 188 -->

...informs the User of any change in the schedule and nature of the Operational Test.

**T9.4.5** Following the notification of the User regarding the conduct of an Operational Test, in accordance with T9.4.4, the User, acting reasonably, may contact the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and request a time frame, if required, to study the impact of the test on them (the User). The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall cooperate with the User to assess the risks. The Operational Test shall not be performed until all Operationally Affected Users give their consent. In the event of a disagreement, the matter of executing the Operational Test shall be referred to CERA, whose decision shall be final.

---

### T9.5 OPERATIONAL TESTS REQUIRED BY USERS

**T9.5.1** The operation of a User's Facilities in accordance with Good Industry Practice rules requires Operational Tests for the purpose of maintaining and developing Operational Procedures, improving and measuring Facility performance, complying with obligations arising from licences or other industrial obligations, and training personnel.

**T9.5.2** In accordance with Good Industry Practice rules, each User shall limit the frequency of Operational Tests and their impact on the Transmission System.

**T9.5.3** Generation / Demand Entities for which an Operational Test request has been submitted and approved may potentially enter into a Trial Operation status during these Tests. It is the sole responsibility of the User to comply with their respective obligations as defined in the Electricity Market Rules.

---

### T9.6 APPLICATION PROCEDURE FOR OPERATIONAL TESTS

**T9.6.1** To conduct an Operational Test, Users shall submit relevant proposals in a timely manner in accordance with the provisions of T8 or by adhering to alternative procedures agreed with the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC).

**T9.6.2** Upon submitting a proposal to conduct an Operational Test, the User shall provide sufficient details to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) so that the TSOC can accurately evaluate any operational consequences of the test. The following information must strictly be included:

* **T9.6.2.1** The reason for conducting the proposed test, i.e., whether the Operational Test is required by Law, by the need to comply with the terms of the User's licence, by legislative regulations, or by Safety Regulations—in which latter case the conduct of the Operational Test must be expedited and prioritized over other Operational Tests.
* **T9.6.2.2** The preferred time or times for conducting the Operational Test.

---

*Issue 1.1.0 | T9 – Operational Tests | Page 188*

<!-- PAGE 189 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T9 – Operational Tests**  
**Page 189**

---

**T9.6.2.3** Key time milestones for the individual stages of the Operational Test (if any) that can be completed independently and/or do not need to be repeated once completed, should the Operational Test be interrupted by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) following the completion of each stage.

**T9.6.2.4** Any adverse material impacts that post-short-notice postponement or interruption of the Operational Test may have on the User (provided that a reasonably detailed description of the impact has been provided by the User to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC)).

**T9.6.2.5** If the User is a Producer, Storage Facility Operator, or Storage Representative, the Special Generation Schedule specifying the Generation/injection during the Operational Test shall also be submitted to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) as part of the proposal. If the User is unable to know with precision the Special Generation Schedule required to complete the test, then until the test is completed, the User shall, when proposing the test:  
(a) divide the test into appropriate stages  
(b) indicate and discuss with the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) which stages of the test can be completed incrementally and which cannot  
(c) indicate potential modifications of the tests for stages that can be completed incrementally. Furthermore, the proposal to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) must specify the factors affecting the completion of the individual stages, namely identifying whether the procedure to be followed for a given stage depends on the outcome of a preceding stage.

---

### T9.7 EVALUATION OF OPERATIONAL TEST PROPOSALS

**T9.7.1** Following the submission of a request to conduct an Operational Test by a User, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall assess the impacts of the proposed test on the operation of the Power System. The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may request additional information from the User that it deems necessary for evaluating the impact or impacts of the test.

**T9.7.2** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall evaluate the impacts of the Operational Test (regarding continuity and quality of supply) with the Operationally Affected Users. The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall take into account any reasonable objection from Operationally Affected Users. When discussing an Operational Test with an affected User, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall not disclose commercially confidential or potentially confidential information to Users without the consent of the User submitting the request to conduct the Operational Test.

**T9.7.3** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may approve an Operational Test proposed by a User in relation to one of the Generation/Demand Entities it represents, which involves operation outside the limits of the declared Availability values,

<!-- PAGE 190 -->

...capability of providing Ancillary Services or Operational Characteristics of the corresponding Generation/Demand Entity.

---

### T9.8 APPROVAL OF OPERATIONAL TEST PROPOSALS

**T9.8.1** Following the submission of a proposal for an Operational Test and the evaluation of the potential impacts of the Operational Test, including consultation with the User requesting the Operational Test and with the Operationally Affected Users, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall decide whether approval will be granted for conducting the Operational Test.

**T9.8.2** The criteria for the approval of an Operational Test include, but are not limited to:
* (a) the impacts of the Operational Test on the operational security of the Transmission System
* (b) the impacts of the Operational Test on the economic operation of the Transmission System
* (c) the impacts of the Operational Test on the Systems of other Users
* (d) the effect of the Operational Test on the continuity of supply and the quality of power supplied
* (e) the effect on the effective operation of the Electricity Market.

**T9.8.3** Following approval by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) of the Operational Test proposed by a User who is a Generator, Storage Facility Operator, or Storage Representative, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall send to the User a proposal that includes the Special Generation Schedule.

**T9.8.3.1** The User Proposing the Test, after receiving the Special Generation Schedule and the scheduling of the Operational Test, shall notify the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) without undue delay as to whether they accept or reject the proposed Special Generation Schedule and the scheduling of the Operational Test.

**T9.8.3.2** If the User Proposing the Test notifies that they reject the Special Generation Schedule or the scheduling for the Operational Test, then the Operational Test shall not be conducted. The User Proposing the Test may negotiate with the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) an alternative scheduling for the Operational Test, a different Operational Test, or the execution of the Operational Test at an alternative time.

**T9.8.3.3** If the User Proposing the Operational Test accepts the Special Generation Schedule or the scheduling for the Operational Test, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall inform those Users, who in its judgment will be affected or may be significantly affected by the test, regarding the planned time and nature of the test.

**T9.8.3.4** If the Operationally Affected Users have objections regarding the proposed Operational Test, they are entitled to submit their concerns to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC). The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall not cancel the 

---
*Version 1.1.0 | T9 – Operational Tests | Page 190*

<!-- PAGE 191 -->

| Edition 1.1.0 | T9 – Operational Tests | Page 191 |
| :--- | :--- | ---: |

... proposed Operational Test, unless the objections are well-founded. If the Operationally Affected Users still maintain objections regarding the approved Operational Tests, they have the right to appeal to CERA's arbitration, in accordance with T9.12.

**T9.8.3.5**  
Notifications from the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) to the User Proposing the Test regarding the proposed Special Generation Schedule or the scheduling of the Operational Test, or notifications from the User Proposing the Test to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) accepting the proposed Special Generation Schedule or the scheduling of the Operational Test, do not constitute Instructions from the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) to the User Proposing the Test.

**T9.8.4**  
If the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) rejects an Operational Test, the User Proposing the Test has the right to submit a proposal to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) for an alternative schedule for the Operational Test, or other Operational Tests, or the execution of the proposed Operational Tests at another time. If the alternative proposal for an Operational Test is approved by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and the User Proposing the Test is a Producer, Energy Storage Facility Operator, or Storage Representative, T9.8.3 shall apply.

**T9.8.5**  
If the User Proposing the Test does not consider the rejection of their new proposal for an Operational Test to be correct, they have the right to appeal to CERA's arbitration in accordance with T9.12.

**T9.8.6**  
In the case of an Operational Test concerning a Generation Unit, Power Station, or Electricity Storage Facility or portfolio thereof, it is the exclusive responsibility of the Producer, Energy Storage Facility Operator, or Storage Representative, as applicable, that the Special Generation Schedule, as finalized for a Settlement Day in accordance with T9.8, fully corresponds to the schedule submitted to the Market Operator for the Unit, Electricity Storage Facility, or portfolio thereof, which may be placed under Trial Operation status within the framework of the Electricity Market Rules during the Operational Test. It is also the exclusive responsibility of the User to submit any relevant notification and/or declaration required by the Electricity Market Rules to the Market Operator.

### T9.9 SCHEDULING OF OPERATIONAL TESTS

**T9.9.1**  
Operational Tests are usually, but not necessarily, scheduled by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC).

**T9.9.2**  
When an Operational Test is required by a Producer, an Energy Storage Facility Operator, or a Storage Representative, the User must propose the generated/injected amount of Active Power (MW) per Settlement Period and other constraints in accordance with the Electricity Market Rules concerning the scheduled Operational Test, which must be approved by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC).

<!-- PAGE 192 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T9 – Operational Tests**  
*Page 192*

---

**T9.9.3** When an Operational Test is required by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), either:

(a) the User proposes the produced/injected Active Power quantity per Trading Period and other constraints, which must be approved by the TSOC;

(b) when the cost and conditions of the Operational Test have been agreed in advance between the User and the TSOC, the User shall comply with the requirements of the TSOC for the Operational Test.

**T9.9.4** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall grant priority to Operational Tests when the Test Proposing User informs the TSOC that conducting the tests is required due to the terms of their license, Safety Regulations, or when delayed performance of these tests may have significant material consequences for the User.

---

### T9.10 DISPATCH DURING OPERATIONAL TESTS

**T9.10.1** Dispatch Instructions during the conduct of Operational Tests shall be issued by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) through the standard procedure for issuing Instructions, in accordance with the provisions of T15 and the Balancing Market, particularly regarding the operation of Generation / Demand Entities that are in Trial Operation / Commissioning.

**T9.10.2** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall ensure that approved Operational Tests are dispatched in accordance with the procedures of any agreed Special Generation Schedule and the agreed Operational Test schedule.

**T9.10.3** If the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) foresees the need or possibility of interruption, postponement, or other significant modification of an agreed procedure and Special Generation Schedule or Operational Test schedule, it shall inform the Test Proposing User as soon as possible. In this case, T9.10.4 and T9.10.5 shall apply.

**T9.10.4** Where the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) estimates that the impact of an Operational Test on the operational security of the Transmission System or on the continuity and quality of supply or on the operation of a User is or may be significantly greater than the initial estimate, it shall jointly review with the Test Proposing User the revision of the test procedure or its rescheduling.

**T9.10.5** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may, if deemed necessary at any time, cancel, interrupt, or postpone an Operational Test, but shall, if practicable, apply the procedures of T9.10.4 before doing so, when such cancellation, interruption, or postponement is carried out for reasons other than technical reasons.

**T9.10.6** If the Test Proposing User, before or during the conduct of the Operational Test, wishes to cancel it, they shall inform the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) in accordance with the provisions of T8. Instructions that have been issued remain valid even after the interruption of Operational Tests.

<!-- PAGE 193 -->

| **Edition 1.1.0** | **T9 – Operational Tests** | **Page 193** |
|:---|:---|:---|

---

### T9.11 NOTIFICATION OF OPERATIONAL TEST RESULTS

**T9.11.1**  
Following the completion of the scheduled duration for conducting an Operational Test, the Test Proposing User shall inform the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) whether the test has been completed, or, in cases where the test has been divided into stages in accordance with T9.6.2.3, regarding which stages of the test have been concluded.

**T9.11.2**  
Following the completion of the Operational Test, the Test Proposing User is responsible for preparing a written report regarding the conducted Operational Test (Final Test Report), which shall be delivered within three months after the completion of the Operational Tests to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), to the Operationally Affected Users, and to CERA upon its request.

**T9.11.3**  
The Final Test Report shall not be disclosed to persons who do not represent the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) or the Test Proposing User, unless the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and the Test Proposing User unanimously agree to it, after taking into account any confidentiality issues.

**T9.11.4**  
The Final Test Report shall include a description of the Facilities and Equipment that have been tested, a description of the System Tests conducted, as well as the results, conclusions, and recommendations concerning the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and the Operationally Affected Users.

---

### T9.12 MISCELLANEOUS

**T9.12.1**  
Operationally Affected Users who consider that the execution of an Operational Test will have a significant adverse impact on them may appeal to CERA, detailing their objections in full.

**T9.12.2**  
The Test Proposing User has the right of appeal to CERA if they do not accept the rejection of the proposed Operational Test.

---

\<End of T9\>

# T10 - POWER SYSTEM EMERGENCY OPERATION CONTROL

## T10.1 INTRODUCTION

### T10.1.1
The normal operation of the Transmission System by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) is conducted in accordance with the principles and procedures defined in the Transmission Rules and the obligations under the Law. Emergency Conditions will arise when the security of the Transmission System falls to abnormal risk levels (for example, during severe thunderstorms or adverse weather conditions), and this T10 provides the specific procedures required to deal with such conditions.

### T10.1.2
Experience has demonstrated that electric power supply systems are subject to Partial or Total Blackouts. These conditions may be caused by a multitude of factors, but are usually attributable to the failure of Generation and/or Transmission Facilities due to severe weather conditions, maloperation of protection systems, or a variety of other reasons.

### T10.1.3
It is therefore necessary for the Transmission Rules to provide procedures for managing a Partial or Total Blackout of the Transmission System and to ensure that the necessary procedures and facilities are capable of supporting the immediate reconnection of de-energized sections and the restoration of supply to Consumers.

### T10.1.4
A Partial or Total Blackout represents one of the most critical contingency conditions that can occur on the Transmission System, having severe impacts on Users and Consumers. Due to the significance of such an event and the imperative urgency to restore supply to all Consumers, it is mandatory that all Users maintain a high level of information awareness and training regarding Power System Restoration matters.

### T10.1.5
The procedure required for restoration following a Total Blackout or a Partial Blackout is known as System Black Start / Restoration, and its primary objective is the restoration of the Power System as an integrated whole as quickly as possible, taking into account the restoration of supply to Consumers. The procedure for the case of a Partial Blackout is similar to that of a Total Blackout, with the distinction that it applies only to a portion of the Power System. It is noted that a Partial Blackout may affect parts of the Power System that have not been tripped out of service.

## T10.2 PURPOSE

### T10.2.1
The purpose of T10 is to ensure that in the event of a Partial or Total Blackout of the Transmission System, normal supply is restored to all Consumers with the maximum possible security and in the shortest feasible time. This objective can be subdivided into the following categories:

<!-- PAGE 196 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T10 – Emergency Operation Control of the Electrical System** | **Page 196**

---

(a) to describe the general restoration strategy that will be adopted by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) in the event of a Partial or Total Blackout of the Transmission System;

(b) to establish the responsibility of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) for the development and maintenance of the Unified Action Plan for the Restoration of the Electrical System Following a Total Blackout;

(c) to establish the responsibility of Users to cooperate in the formulation and execution of the Unified Action Plan for the Restoration of the Electrical System Following a Total Blackout, covering both Partial and Total Blackouts;

(d) to define the specific role of Black Start Units following a Total Blackout;

(e) to ensure that the personnel of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and Users who may be involved in the Unified Action Plan for the Restoration of the Electrical System Following a Total Blackout are adequately trained and fully familiar with the relevant details of the plan.

---

### T10.3 SCOPE OF APPLICATION

T10 applies to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and Users, who under T10 include:

(a) **Generators**, who for the purposes of T10 include Generators operating Licensed Very Small Scale Power Generating Stations and Small, Medium, and Large Scale Power Generating Stations. In particular, Generators operating Generating Units providing Black Start Service following a Total Blackout are included;

(b) **Independent Aggregators**;

(c) **Storage Facility Operators**;

(d) **Distribution System Operator (DSO)**;

(e) **Transmission System Owner**;

(f) **Distribution System Owner**;

(g) **Consumers** connected to the Transmission System.

---

### T10.4 SYSTEM ALARMS

**T10.4.1** In the event of an Emergency State or an imminent interruption of Active Power (MW) supply capability, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall issue certain Alarms to inform Users, Distribution System Control Operators, as well as Control Operators. These Alarms include Amber, Red, or Blue Alarms, or other Alarms agreed upon at another time.

**T10.4.2** The Alarm shall trigger an alarm signal at the recipient's premises, which must be acknowledged by the User in accordance with the Alarm procedures. Alarms are normally transmitted to the User via an Electronic Alarm Transmission System, except in cases where this system is inoperative, in which case they are transmitted verbally.

<!-- PAGE 197 -->

**Edition 1.1.0** | **T10 – Power System Emergency Operation Control** | **Page 197**

---

### T10.4.3 ORANGE ALERTS

**T10.4.3.1** An Orange Alert is issued when a single Incident increases the logical probability of failure to satisfy the Electrical System Demand, or when the Frequency or Voltage deviates significantly from their nominal value, as specified in the Connection Conditions to the Transmission System, or if multiple Incidents are likely to occur due to prevailing weather conditions.

**T10.4.3.2** The standing procedures activated as a result of receiving an Orange Alert are defined by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), taking into account the opinion of the Users, and are duly notified to each User. These standing procedures cannot impose obligations on the User other than those already included in the Transmission Rules.

**T10.4.3.3** Each User is responsible for the internal procedures necessary for readiness to execute the standing procedures.

---

### T10.4.4 RED ALERTS

**T10.4.4.1** A Red Alert is issued when the Frequency or Voltage deviates significantly from their nominal value, or when User Demand is not met, or when, in the near future, there is a high probability of failure to satisfy the Electrical System Demand or to maintain Frequency or Voltage at normal levels.

**T10.4.4.2** The standing procedures activated as a result of receiving a Red Alert are defined by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), taking into account the opinion of the Users, and are duly notified to each User.

**T10.4.4.3** Each User is responsible for the internal procedures necessary for readiness to execute the standing procedures.

---

### T10.4.5 BLUE ALERTS

**T10.4.5.1** The issuance of a Blue Alert by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), except for the case provided for in T10.5.9, indicates that either a Partial or a Total Blackout of the Electrical System has occurred.

**T10.4.5.2** The standing procedures activated as a result of receiving a Blue Alert are defined by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), taking into account the opinion of the Users, and are duly notified to each User. These standing procedures cannot impose obligations on the User other than those already included in the Transmission and Distribution Rules.

**T10.4.5.3** Each User is responsible for the internal procedures necessary for readiness to execute the standing procedures. For the development of the internal procedures to be implemented following the activation of the Blue Alert standing procedures, each User shall consult the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC).

<!-- PAGE 198 -->

| Revision 1.1.0 | T10 – Emergency Operation Control of Electrical System | Page 198 |
|:---|:---|:---|

---

## T10.5 ELECTRIC POWER SYSTEM RESTORATION FOLLOWING A BLACKOUT

### T10.5.1
The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) develops and updates the Unified Action Plan for the Restoration of the Electric Power System Following a Blackout, in cooperation with the Distribution System Operator and other Users. The Unified Action Plan for the Restoration of the Electric Power System Following a Blackout describes the relevant standing operating procedures for the restoration of the Electric Power System.

### T10.5.2
In cases of a Blackout, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall implement, to the extent possible, the current Unified Action Plan for the Restoration of the Electric Power System Following a Blackout; however, it may deviate from it if special conditions so require at its discretion. Each User is obligated to execute the instructions of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) during the restoration procedure, provided that the safety of personnel, Facilities, and Equipment of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and the User is not compromised.

### T10.5.3
The Unified Action Plan for the Restoration of the Electric Power System Following a Blackout ensures the safe Black Start / re-energization of the Electric Power System following a Blackout.

### T10.5.4
In the Unified Action Plan for the Restoration of the Electric Power System Following a Blackout, regarding the re-energization/restoration of the Electric Power System, Generation Units, Energy Storage Facilities, or portfolios of Energy Storage Facilities capable of providing Black Start Service Following a Blackout may participate. The relevant requirements for the provision of Black Start Service Following a Blackout are defined by TSOC and, among others, include the following factors:

* **(a)** the number of Generation Units and Energy Storage Facilities or portfolios thereof,
* **(b)** their geographical distribution,
* **(c)** the minimum required Maximum Continuous Loading,
* **(d)** the available stored energy and Maximum Discharge Capacity for Energy Storage Facilities or portfolios thereof,
* **(e)** the connection of Generation Units and Energy Storage Facilities to the Transmission System or to the Distribution System,
* **(f)** the results of Black Start tests following a Blackout, pursuant to T11.5.8,
* **(g)** the results of operational analyses of the Electric Power System,
* **(h)** other requirements.

### T10.5.5
In addition to the requirements of TSOC specified in T10.5.4, and following the conduct of the annual auction for the provision of the Black Start Service Following a Blackout, in accordance with the provisions of Annex 5.2 of the Electricity Market Rules, and the determination of the specific Generation Units and Energy Storage Facilities or portfolios thereof that will provide Black Start Service Following a Blackout within the specific year, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) updates the Unified Action Plan...

<!-- PAGE 199 -->

Version 1.1.0 | T10 – Control of Emergency Operation of the Power System | Page 199

...Action Plan for Power System Restoration after a Blackout.

**T10.5.6** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) gives priority to Generating Units and Energy Storage Facilities, or portfolios thereof, that provide Black Start Service following a Blackout through participation in auctions pursuant to Annex 5.2 of the Electricity Market Rules, both during the drafting and updating of the Unified Action Plan for Power System Restoration after a Blackout and during the issuance of instructions under Black Start conditions following a Blackout. However, if conditions so require at its discretion, it may include in the Black Start procedure following a Blackout other Units, Facilities, or portfolios thereof capable of providing this Service in accordance with their Registered Operational Characteristics.

**T10.5.7** It is the responsibility of the User to ensure that its personnel expected to participate in the Power System Restoration after a Blackout is familiar, adequately trained, and experienced regarding standing instructions and other obligations, so as to be capable of executing procedures and complying with any procedures notified by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) in accordance with T10.5.2.

**T10.5.8** The completion of the Black Start procedure following a Blackout and the time instance of normal operation of the Power System are determined by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), which must inform Users accordingly.

**T10.5.9** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall, in cooperation with each User and at least once a year, issue a Blue Alarm to the User for training purposes. The content of these Tests shall be notified in advance to Users, and the date and time of execution of the Tests shall be agreed upon. The User is obliged, acting with due justification, to cooperate for any such Test. It is noted that the above Tests are conducted for training purposes and differ from the Black Start Tests following a Blackout that the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may require in accordance with the provisions of T11.5.8.

### T10.6 TRANSMISSION SYSTEM CONTROLS

#### T10.6.1 TRANSMISSION SYSTEM OPERATIONAL SWITCHING

**T10.6.1.1** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall perform operational switching for various purposes, including but not limited to:
* **(a)** Outages/Isolation of Facilities and Plant for purposes of safe operation, maintenance, construction of new projects, Transmission System Tests, protection testing, and User works.
* **(b)** Outages/Isolation of Transmission System Facilities due to suspected or probable faults and for emergency repairs.
* **(c)** Voltage Control.

<!-- PAGE 200 -->

| Document Reference | Code Title | Page |
|---|---|---|
| Version 1.1.0 | T10 – Power System Emergency Operation Control | Page 200 |

***

(d) Restriction of power flows on the Transmission System to levels compatible with the operational capacities of the Transmission System Facilities and system security.

**T10.6.1.2** Additionally, an automatic change in switchgear status may occur without prior notice due to the operation of protection devices for isolating or clearing faults in the Transmission Facilities or in the Facilities of a User connected to the Transmission System.

**T10.6.1.3** An automatic sequence of switchgear status changes may be performed to limit deviations in power flows, Voltage, or Frequency in the event of faults occurring elsewhere on the Transmission System.

### T10.6.2 NOTIFICATION TO USERS FOR SWITCHING OPERATIONS ON THE TRANSMISSION SYSTEM

**T10.6.2.1** All Switching Operations carried out on the Transmission System have the potential, under given circumstances, to affect Users. Attempting to inform Users of every Switching Operation is not practically feasible and, in most cases, the information is of no value to Users, considering that the User will not invoke any specific action as a result of receiving such information.

**T10.6.2.2** When it has been identified and agreed that, pursuant to the terms of the Connection Agreements and/or Operating Agreements between the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and a User, a specific Switching Operation (typically an operation affecting the settings of the Transmission System) has Operational Effects on another User and that there is a need to notify the User prior to carrying out the Switching Operation, then the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall notify the User of the Switching Operation (if planned and practically feasible), in accordance with any standing agreement reached with the User.

**T10.6.2.3** When it is necessary to carry out urgent changes in switchgear status or other Switching Operations as a result of Transmission System conditions or faults, it may not be possible for the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) to notify Users in advance of the changes in switchgear status or other Switching Operations. The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall endeavor to notify Users when practically feasible and provided it does not cause a time delay in executing the Switching Operations as required. If the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) is unable to notify Users prior to the Switching Operations, the terms of T10.6.3 shall apply.

### T10.6.3 CONTROL FOLLOWING FAULT OR EMERGENCY CONDITIONS

**T10.6.3.1** In the event that there occurs:
* (a) a fault or protection operation;
* (b) an automatic procedure such as Automatic Under-Frequency Demand Shedding (UFLS), Automatic Demand Restoration Following...

<!-- PAGE 201 -->

**Edition 1.1.0**  
**T10 – Power System Emergency Operation Control**  
**Page 201**

---

...Frequency Restoration, Automatic Demand Shedding under Low Voltage, or Automatic Demand Shedding under Overvoltage, it is not possible to apply standard procedures in accordance with T10.6.2 prior to performing the Switching Operations.

**T10.6.3.2** In cases referred to in T10.6.3.1 or where the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) must urgently perform certain Switching Operations without prior notification to Users, the TSOC shall inform Users ex-post regarding the execution of these actions, unless the condition was transient and has been fully restored to normal levels.

**T10.6.3.3** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall also notify Users regarding the estimated duration of emergency conditions and update this estimate when required. The TSOC shall additionally inform Users regarding the termination of emergency conditions.

---

### T10.6.4 DE-ENERGISATION OF USER FACILITIES AND EQUIPMENT BY THE TRANSMISSION SYSTEM OPERATOR

**T10.6.4.1** The De-energisation of the Facilities and Equipment of a User may take place at any time and to any extent that the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) reasonably considers necessary to ensure the safe and reliable operation of the Transmission System within defined specifications.

The De-energisation of User Facilities and Equipment takes place under circumstances which cause or, in the opinion of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), are likely to cause, including but not limited to, at least one of the following:

* **(a)** danger to personnel safety;
* **(b)** danger to the stability of the Transmission System;
* **(c)** danger to the Transmission System or to the Facilities and Equipment of any User;
* **(d)** overloading of Transmission System elements beyond emergency limits;
* **(e)** Voltage deviations in the Transmission System beyond the limits specified in T1;
* **(f)** any behavior causing continuous operation outside the normal operating Frequency limits of the Transmission System;
* **(g)** any breach of T1 or other relevant chapters of the Transmission Code;
* **(h)** any act or omission of a User preventing the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) from complying with its legal, statutory, or regulatory obligations.

**T10.6.4.2** Instructions for the De-energisation of a specific User connected to the Distribution System under the terms of T10.6.4.1 may be issued by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and addressed to the Distribution System Operator (DSO), who shall act in accordance with the instructions without undue delay.

---
`<End of T10>`

# TRANSMISSION RULES

## T11 - MONITORING, TESTING AND INVESTIGATION

### Version 1.1.0

---
| Version 1.1.0 | T11 – Monitoring, Testing and Investigation | Page 202 |

<!-- PAGE 203 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T11 – Monitoring, Testing and Investigation** | **Page 203**

# T11 - MONITORING, TESTING AND INVESTIGATION

## T11.1 INTRODUCTION

### T11.1.1
The responsibility of TSOC regarding the safeguarding of the safe and economical operation of the Transmission System is the execution of specific Monitoring, Testing, and Investigation procedures concerning the performance of User Facilities.

### T11.1.2
T11 does not include Operational Tests, which are required by TSOC or by Users. The procedures by which Operational Tests are notified, approved, executed, and reported are covered under Operational Tests T9.

## T11.2 PURPOSE

### T11.2.1
The purpose of T11 is to establish the procedures required for conducting Tests verifying that Users operate in accordance with design, operational, and connection requirements, as specified in the Transmission Code, Connection Agreements, Electricity Market Rules, or other contracts between Users and the Transmission System Owner, or other contracts between Users and TSOC.

### T11.2.2
To achieve the primary purpose established in T11.2.1, T11 establishes procedures for Monitoring, Testing, and Investigation. Specifically, adequate checks are performed on each of the following:
* (a) whether Users comply with Instructions,
* (b) whether Users comply with Availability declarations, the capability to provide Ancillary Services, Registered Operational Characteristics, and other necessary data submitted by them in accordance with the Transmission Code and Electricity Market Rules,
* (c) whether the Power Quality of Users complies with International Electrotechnical Commission (IEC) Standards regarding Electromagnetic Compatibility,
* (d) whether Users are in compliance with ripple control signal levels,
* (e) whether Users comply with protection requirements and settings in accordance with the Transmission Code, User Connection Agreements, and other contracts between Users and TSOC or other contracts between Users and the Transmission System Owner.

## T11.3 SCOPE OF APPLICATION

T11 applies to TSOC and to Users, which include:
* (a) **Producers**, who for the purposes of T11 include all Producers operating Licensed Very Small Scale Power Generating Stations and Small, Medium, and Large Scale Power Generating Stations;
* (b) **Storage Facility Operators**;
* (c) **RES and CHP Producers**.

<!-- PAGE 204 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T11 – Monitoring, Testing and Investigation**  
*Page 204*

(d) Distribution System Operator and Owner  
(e) Transmission-connected Consumers  
(f) Independent Aggregators  
(g) Transmission System Owner  

---

### T11.4 MONITORING

**T11.4.1**  
Monitoring is conducted continuously or for continuous time intervals and includes the monitoring, recording of relevant data, and their analysis, or the use of other suitable methods as determined with justification by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) to be appropriate for the specific cases. The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) is not obligated to notify Users of Monitoring in advance, unless requested by the User and approved by TSOC.

**T11.4.2**  
If a data recording and analysis system is used for Monitoring, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall inform the User that such a data recording and analysis system is being used and, upon request by the User, shall provide the User with any reasonably requested information regarding the data recording and analysis system.

**T11.4.3**  
Monitoring is conducted by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) at any time and may result, without carrying out additional Tests, in a determination by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) of non-compliance by the User. When the User disputes the determination of their non-compliance, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall provide the User with any relevant data collected during Monitoring during the period of alleged non-compliance, as well as any other documentation reasonably necessary to serve as evidence of non-compliance.

**T11.4.4**  
The performance parameters that the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall include within the scope of Monitoring include, but are not limited to, the following:

a. Compliance with Instructions  
b. Compliance with Registered Operating Characteristics, without limitations  
c. Compliance with Power Quality standards  
d. Provision of static and dynamic Reactive Power  
e. Monitoring systems and procedures  

The procedures and systems used to assess compliance shall either be general procedures (which shall be specified by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC)) or shall be agreed upon between the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and the User.

---

### T11.5 TESTING

**T11.5.1**  
Tests may be performed following a decision by TSOC or a request by the User and approval by TSOC. Tests may include the presence of a representative of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) at the User Sites, such that...

<!-- PAGE 205 -->

**Edition 1.1.0** | **T11 – Monitoring, Testing and Investigation** | **Page 205**

---

...to perform Tests in accordance with the testing procedures defined in T11.5.7.

**T11.5.2** For the purposes of T11, Tests may be performed in accordance with Instructions from the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) or in accordance with such alternative procedures as required or specified in T11.

**T11.5.3** Tests may require the User to carry out specific switching operations in response to a given Instruction.

**T11.5.4** The results of the Tests are collected from operational Monitoring during the Tests.

**T11.5.5** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may, at regular intervals, carry out Tests to verify that the User has complied with the Transmission System Connection Conditions, the Registered Operating Characteristics, and the Declarations of Change of Operating Characteristics. The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may:

(a) at regular intervals and for Testing purposes, issue an Instruction  
(b) induce controlled conditions or deviations of Power System Frequency or Voltage in order to ascertain whether the response of a specific User is in accordance with the declared Availability and Registered Operating Characteristics  
(c) having given three Business Days' prior notice, or a shorter period if agreed, (acknowledging the Ancillary Services and/or Operating Characteristics to be tested), dispatch a representative to the User's Connection Site to verify through Tests consistent with the Testing procedures defined in T11.5.7 that the User complies with the registered values of its Operating Characteristics  
(d) carry out checks of under-frequency relays operating at User installations  
(e) carry out checks of another type, at its reasonable discretion and following consultation with the User  

**T11.5.6** If the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) assigns the Testing work at the User's Connection Site to a third party, then the User and the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) must reach an agreement regarding the selection of the appropriate third party.

**T11.5.7 Testing Procedures**

The proposed procedure for the Tests is notified to the User by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) prior to conducting the Tests. For an existing procedure, notification is given three Business Days prior to conducting the Tests. For a new procedure, notification is given five Business Days prior to conducting the Tests. Upon receipt of this notification, the User may, acting in good faith, three Business Days prior to conducting the Tests, raise reasoned objections to the proposed procedure, if it considers that a substantial risk exists to the safety of the User's Connection Site or personnel, or

<!-- PAGE 206 -->

**Version 1.1.0**  
**T11 – Monitoring, Testing, and Investigation**  
**Page 206**

---

...judging that the proposed procedure cannot be implemented for technical reasons or is unsuitable for the intended purpose (in accordance with Good Industry Practice), and providing full details regarding these judgments. In the event that the User objects to this notice, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), if it deems necessary, shall modify the procedures and notify the User again, who in this case is obliged to comply with the requirements of the revised procedure.

**T11.5.7.1** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall respect the confidential nature of the information collected from Users during the conduct of Monitoring and Testing.

**T11.5.7.2** The results of the Tests must be managed in accordance with T11.7.

**T11.5.7.3** Payment of the costs of carrying out the Tests shall be covered as specified below:

(a) If the User requires Testing to be conducted, then the User shall cover the expenses of the Tests, as determined by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC).

(b) If the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) requires Testing to be conducted, the User shall submit to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) an estimate of the cost of the Tests, and the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall cover their direct cost if it agrees with this estimate, except in the cases described in T11.5.7.3 (c) below. The direct cost may be subject to an audit if required by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), and the User is obliged to submit any relevant information pertaining to such an audit.

(c) If the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) requires Testing to be conducted, the User shall cover the expenses of the Tests in the following cases:

i) if the Tests are re-runs/repeats of previous Tests required by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) that were not satisfactorily completed by the User in the opinion of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC).

ii) if the Tests are required by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) following the issuance of a Notice of Non-Compliance to the User by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC).

---

### T11.5.8 Black Start Testing

**T11.5.8.1** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall be entitled to require a User with a Black Start Unit to conduct tests (Black Start Tests) in order to demonstrate the capability to provide Black Start Service.

**T11.5.8.2** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall be entitled to require a User to conduct Black Start Tests at any point in time, but shall not be entitled to require the conduct of Black Start Tests more than twice...

<!-- PAGE 207 -->

**Version 1.1.0**
**T11 – Monitoring, Testing, and Investigation**
**Page 207**

...in each calendar year for each Dispatchable Generating Unit or each Electricity Storage Facility or each portfolio of Electricity Storage Facilities, unless special reasons exist necessitating additional tests or if the additional tests constitute a re-test of a specific test.

**T11.5.8.3** When the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) wishes to conduct Black Start Tests, it shall notify the relevant Users prior to the time of conducting the Black Start Tests with details concerning the proposed Black Start Tests.

**T11.5.8.4** All Black Start Tests that are announced must be conducted at the time specified by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) in the notice issued pursuant to T11.5.8.3 and shall be carried out in the presence of a reasonable number of representatives appointed and authorized by TSOC, who shall be granted access to all information related to the Black Start Tests.

**T11.5.8.5** Unannounced Black Start Tests shall be conducted by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) if deemed necessary.

### T11.6 INVESTIGATION

**T11.6.1** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall, if it reasonably considers that an issue of non-compliance exists for a specific User, conduct an Investigation to obtain or verify information relating to the design of the Facilities and/or Plant, operation, or connection requirements in accordance with the Transmission Rules, Connection Agreements, and Electricity Market Rules, or in accordance with other agreements between Users and the Transmission System Owner or between Users and TSOC.

**T11.6.2** An Investigation by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) typically concerns information that is not routinely collected through Monitoring and Testing. TSOC shall, having given timely notice, send a representative or a third party to the Users' Site to Inspect the equipment or operating procedures applied at the Users' Site to the extent that the condition of the equipment or operating procedure relates to compliance with the Transmission Rules, Connection Agreement, and/or other agreements between Users and TSOC or other agreements between Users and the Transmission System Owner.

### T11.7 CONSEQUENCES OF MONITORING, TESTING, AND INVESTIGATION

#### T11.7.1 NON-COMPLIANCE WITH INSTRUCTIONS

**T11.7.1.1** If the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) considers that a User repeatedly fails to comply with Instructions, then TSOC shall issue a warning to the User, with...

<!-- PAGE 208 -->

**Issue 1.1.0 | T11 – Monitoring, Testing and Investigation**  
**Page 208**

---

...agreed methods. This warning is known as a **"Non-Compliance Warning"**, and includes the identification of the relevant Generation / Demand Entity, and the identification of the Instructions with which TSOC considers that the User has failed to comply, as well as the time of their issuance. The Non-Compliance Warning includes an Instruction which may be identical to or differ from the original one. The event of the Non-Compliance Warning with Instructions is recorded by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and by the User.

**T11.7.1.2** Following receipt of the Non-Compliance Warning, the User must as soon as possible:

* **(a)** Commence compliance with the Instruction included in the Non-Compliance Warning.
* **(b)** Respond to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), disputing in good faith the validity of the non-compliance assessment. In such case, the User shall inform the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) as soon as possible in detail regarding the reasons questioning the non-compliance assessment.
* **(c)** Respond to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), providing the reason for their inability to comply with the Instructions and submit a Declaration of Change of Operating Characteristics, in accordance with the Availability, the capability to provide Ancillary Services, or the Registered Operating Characteristics of the relevant Generation / Demand Entity.

**T11.7.1.3** If the User complies with T11.7.1.2 (a), the relevant provisions of the Market Rules apply and no additional action is taken.

**T11.7.1.4** In the case where the User submits a Declaration of Change of Operating Characteristics pursuant to T11.7.1.2 (c), the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) issues new Instructions in accordance with the Declaration of Change of Operating Characteristics. In any case, the submission of a Declaration of Change of Operating Characteristics does not negate the fact that the User has failed to comply with the Instructions included in the Non-Compliance Warning.

**T11.7.1.5** In cases where T11.7.1.2 (b) applies, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall examine the material grounds of the User's objection. The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall, whenever deemed necessary, communicate with the User in order to clarify matters regarding the issuance and receipt of the Instruction and the User's actions. The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall, acting with due justification, determine the validity of the User's objection and inform the User regarding its decision. The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall record its decision and all information related to the event, including the User's objection. This information is considered Registered Information.

**T11.7.1.6** When the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), acting with due justification, is of the opinion that the User's objection is invalid or not fully valid, or if the User has not responded in accordance with T11.7.1.2, then it shall inform the User that it is not complying with its Registered Operating Characteristics and issue an Update...

<!-- PAGE 209 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T11 – Monitoring, Testing and Investigation** | **Page 209**

... Operating Characteristics by the TSOC, which remains valid until the User submits an additional Notice of Change of Operating Characteristics.

**T11.7.1.7** Non-compliance regarding certain Instructions issued by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus shall be recorded in the supplementary document "Non-Compliance Report", which is prepared in accordance with paragraph T11.9.

**T11.7.2 User Non-compliance regarding Operating Reserve**

**T11.7.2.1** During the assessment of the adequacy of performance of the Generation / Demand Entity represented by a User, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus shall compare the actual performance, as measured, with the expected performance for the specific Generation / Demand Entity. The expected performance is calculated using the Frequency deviation from the Pre-Disturbance Event Frequency Value, in relation to the Registered Operating Characteristics of the Generation / Demand Entity, with respect to the provision of Fast Frequency Response Reserve, Frequency Containment Reserve, and Frequency Restoration Reserve, the activation time, and the delivery time of 100% of the corresponding Ancillary Service, as well as the relevant Availability.

**T11.7.2.2** If, in the assessment of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus, the Generation / Demand Entities do not operate in accordance with their Registered Operating Characteristics for the provision of FFR, FCR, aFRR, and/or mFRR, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus shall inform the corresponding User of the non-compliance, identifying the system or procedure through which the non-compliance was measured. In this case, an Update of Operating Characteristics may be issued by the TSOC.

**T11.7.2.3** Following notification of non-compliance, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus must make available to the User, within three Business Days, the relevant data concerning system Frequency and the performance of the Facilities, which may be reasonably requested by the User and demonstrate the validity of its assessment regarding non-compliance.

**T11.7.2.4** User non-compliance regarding the Registered Operating Characteristics of the Generation / Demand Entity it represents for the provision of FCR, aFRR, and/or mFRR, shall be recorded in the supplementary document "Non-Compliance Report", which is prepared in accordance with paragraph T11.9.

**T11.7.3 User Non-Compliance with the Transmission System Connection Terms or the Registered Operating Characteristics**

**T11.7.3.1** In the event that the performance of the User's Generation / Demand Entity is judged by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus, in accordance with the terms of T11, not to agree with the Registered Operating Characteristics or with the Transmission System Connection Terms, then the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus shall inform the User regarding its non-compliance and the User must...

<!-- PAGE 210 -->

**Document Header Information**  
*Version 1.1.0 | T11 – Monitoring, Testing and Investigation | Page 210*

---

...to act immediately to correct this non-compliance. In any case, an Update of Operational Characteristics may be issued by TSOC. The terms of this T11.7.3, without any prejudice, give the right to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus to Deactivate the Facilities and Plant of any Generation / Demand Entity represented by the User in accordance with the terms of T10.6.

**T11.7.3.2** The non-compliance of a User's Generation / Demand Entity with the Connection Conditions to the Transmission System or the Registered Operational Characteristics is recorded in the supplementary document "Non-Compliance Report", which is prepared in accordance with paragraph T11.9.

**T11.7.4 Failure of Black Start Tests**

**T11.7.4.1** A Black Start Unit fails a Black Start Test if the Black Start Test demonstrates that it does not have the capability to provide Black Start Service (for example, if the relevant Generation Unit fails to Synchronize with the Transmission System within the declared Synchronization time).

**T11.7.4.2** If a Black Start Unit fails a Black Start Test, the User shall provide the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus with a Written report specifying in sufficient detail up to that specific point in time and following the required and careful investigation the reasons for the failure of the test, as far as these are known to the User. This report must be provided within five Business Days after the test. If there is an objection regarding the failure, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus and the involved User must attempt to resolve the reasons for the objection through discussion, and if they fail to reach an agreement, the User may request the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus to conduct an additional Black Start Test, which shall be carried out following the agreed procedure as applicable and with 48 hours' notice. It shall be deemed that the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus has issued the relevant instructions at the time of notification by the User.

**T11.7.4.3** If the relevant Black Start Unit fails the re-test and there is an objection regarding the result of this re-test, either party may refer to the Dispute Resolution Procedure for a final determination regarding the objection, which shall be binding.

**T11.7.4.4** If, following the procedure described in T11.7.6.2 and T11.7.6.3, it is accepted that the Black Start Unit has failed the Black Start Test (or the re-test carried out in accordance with T11.7.6.2, within a period of 10 Business Days following the test, or a longer period if agreed with reasoned justification by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus), the corresponding User shall submit a Written application to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus for approval of

<!-- PAGE 211 -->

**Version 1.1.0 | T11 – Monitoring, Testing and Research**  
**Page 211**

---

...of the date and time by which the User's Black Start Unit will have returned to a suitable condition, so as to be capable of providing Black Start Service and achieving success in the Black Start Test. The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall not unreasonably delay approval of the proposed date and time submitted by the User. If the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) does not approve the proposed date and time (or any revised proposal) submitted by the User, the User shall revise its proposals, taking into account any relevant comments made by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), and resubmit them for approval.

**T11.7.4.5** When the User indicates to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) that its Unit has the capability to provide Black Start Service, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall either accept this information or require the User to demonstrate that the relevant Black Start Unit has restored its capability to provide Black Start Service, by repeating the Black Start Test referred to in T11.5.8.4 following the same procedure followed for the initial Black Start Test. The provisions of T11.5.8 shall apply to such a test.

**T11.7.4.6** Failure of Black Start Tests of a Generating Unit is recorded in the supplementary document "Non-Compliance Report", which is prepared in accordance with paragraph T11.9.

**T11.7.4.7** The financial consequences of User non-compliance with the above, as well as other details, are defined in accordance with T11.9 and the Electricity Market Rules.

**T11.7.5** In no case do the provisions of subsection T11.7 relieve the User from the provisions of the Electricity Market Rules regarding any consequences of non-compliance, and in particular the provisions regarding Balancing Market Settlement and Imbalance Settlement.

### T11.8 OBJECTIONS AGAINST NON-COMPLIANCE ASSESSMENT BY A USER

**T11.8.1** In the event that the User receives a notification from the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) concerning an assessment of non-compliance and/or an Update of Operational Characteristics by TSOC, the User may respond in good faith to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) disputing the validity of the non-compliance assessment and/or the content of the Update of Operational Characteristics by TSOC, detailing the reasons for objecting to their validity. Any objection must be submitted within 12 hours, while additional supporting information for the objection may be submitted within the following two Business Days.

**T11.8.2** If a User submits an objection to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) in accordance with T11.8.1, then the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall examine the substantive points of the User's objection. The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may, where...

<!-- PAGE 212 -->

[...] considers necessary, to communicate with the User in order to clarify matters related to the non-compliance assessment and the User's objection.

**T11.8.3** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall decide on the validity of the User's objection and shall inform the User accordingly within a period of five (5) Business Days. The TSOC may alter or appropriately revise any non-compliance assessment.

**T11.8.4** In the event that a disagreement regarding the result persists, the objection must, if requested by either the TSOC or the User, be referred to CERA for arbitration.

---

### T11.9 REPORT ON NON-COMPLIANCES BY USER

**T11.9.1** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall prepare, if deemed necessary, the supplementary document "Report on Non-Compliances" and submit it to CERA for the potential imposition of fines. These fines operate independently of the Imbalance Settlement procedure specified in the Electricity Market Rules. The document must include:

* (a) A description of the events in cases of Non-Compliance referred to in paragraphs T11.7.1, T11.7.2, T11.7.3, and T11.7.4.
* (b) Details regarding the conditions and rules based on which the non-compliances have arisen and been evaluated.

**T11.9.2** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may, concurrently with the submission of the document "Report on Non-Compliances" to CERA, proceed to review specific provisions of the Transmission Rules in accordance with the provisions of T0, in order to address non-compliance situations more effectively.

<End of T11>

# TRANSMISSION AND DISTRIBUTION RULES

## T12 - SAFETY RULES

**Version 1.1.0**

---

| | |
| :--- | ---: |
