# TRANSMISSION RULES

## T14 – Additional Provisions for Energy Storage Systems

**Version 1.1.0**

---

*Version 1.1.0 | T14 – Additional Provisions for Energy Storage Systems | Page 242*

<!-- PAGE 243 -->

Version 1.1.0 | T14 – Additional Provisions for Storage Systems  
Page 243

# T14 - ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS FOR STORAGE SYSTEMS

## T14.1 INTRODUCTION

**T14.1.1** Due to the fact that Storage Systems have unique characteristics, it is necessary to establish specific provisions regarding these systems.

## T14.2 PURPOSE

**T14.2.1** The primary purpose of T14 is to define the technical requirements and terms that must be complied with by Storage Systems, aiming to ensure the safe and reliable operation of the Electricity System.

## T14.3 SCOPE OF APPLICATION

**T14.3.1** The provisions of T14 apply to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), and to the following Users:

*   **(a)** Storage Facility Operators connected to the Transmission System.
*   **(b)** RES Producers with RES Units with Integrated Storage connected to the Transmission System.

**T14.3.2** Storage Systems installed in existing RES Units, following a modification of the relevant License obtained from CERA, must satisfy the requirements of T14.

**T14.3.3** In cases of Storage Facilities with Integrated RES and RES Units with Integrated Storage, the requirements of T14 apply exclusively to the Storage System (and not to the entire Storage Facility or RES Unit) and refer to the maximum output power of the Storage System, unless stated otherwise.

At the Connection Point of the Storage Facility or RES Unit with the Transmission System, the requirements pertaining to all components of each Storage Facility or RES Unit must be satisfied cumulatively.

**T14.3.4** The User may install supplementary Equipment along with the Storage System for the purpose of fulfilling the requirements of T14 (e.g. capacitor banks, static synchronous compensators, harmonic filters, protective devices, etc.). This Equipment is considered part of the Storage System with respect to satisfying the requirements of T14.

## T14.4 TECHNICAL REQUIREMENTS FOR STORAGE SYSTEMS

### T14.4.1 Operating limits

#### T14.4.1.1 Operating limits based on Frequency

Storage Systems must be capable of remaining connected to the grid and operating in steady state within

<!-- PAGE 244 -->

... frequency ranges and for a duration as specified in Table 14.1.

##### Table 14.1: Operating limits based on Frequency

| Frequency Range | Minimum Operational Duration |
|---|---|
| 47.0 Hz – 47.5 Hz | 10 seconds |
| 47.5 Hz – 49.5 Hz | 60 Minutes |
| 49.5 Hz – 50.5 Hz | Unlimited |
| 50.5 Hz – 52 Hz | 60 Minutes |

The TSOC and the Storage Facility Operator or the RES Producer may agree upon wider Frequency ranges, longer minimum operational durations, or specific requirements for combinations of Frequency and Voltage deviations, in order to ensure the optimal utilization of the technical capabilities of the Storage System, if this is necessary to maintain or restore the security of the Electrical System.

The Storage Facility Operator shall not unreasonably withhold consent for the implementation of wider Frequency ranges or longer minimum operational durations, taking into account their economic and technical feasibility.

#### T14.4.1.2 Operating limits based on Voltage

Storage Systems must be capable of remaining continuously connected to the grid and operating in steady state when the Voltage is within the normal operating range as specified in Paragraph T1.8.3.1. For Voltage values below the normal operating range, the requirements are specified in Paragraph T14.5.1, and for values above the normal operating range, the requirements are specified in Paragraph T14.5.2.

The TSOC and the Storage Facility Operator or the RES Producer may agree upon wider Voltage ranges, longer minimum operational durations, or specific requirements for combinations of Frequency and Voltage deviations, in order to ensure the optimal utilization of the technical capabilities of the Storage System, if this is necessary to maintain or restore the security of the Electrical System.

The Storage Facility Operator shall not unreasonably withhold consent for the implementation of wider Voltage ranges or longer minimum operational durations, taking into account their economic and technical feasibility.

#### T14.4.1.3 Operating limits based on Rate of Change of Frequency

Storage Systems must be capable of remaining connected to the grid and operating in steady state in the event that the Rate of Change of Frequency does not exceed the values specified in Table 14.2.

---
*Edition 1.1.0 | T14 – Additional Provisions for Storage Systems | Page 244*

<!-- PAGE 245 -->

**Document Code:** Version 1.1.0  
**Section:** T14 – Additional Provisions for Storage Systems  
**Page:** 245  

---

### Table 14.2: Operating Limits based on Rate of Change of Frequency (RoCoF)

| Rate of Change of Frequency (RoCoF) |
|---|
| ±4 Hz/s for a period of 0.25 seconds |
| ±2 Hz/s for a period of 0.5 seconds |
| ±1.5 Hz/s for a period of 1 second |
| ±1.25 Hz/s for a period of 2 seconds |

---

### T14.4.1.4 Active Power Injection/Absorption Requirements

The control system of the Storage System shall be capable of adjusting and maintaining the Active Power injection/absorption of the Storage System based on Instructions issued by the TSOC within the framework of the Balancing Market operation or based on a signal received from the TSOC, unless one of the automatic frequency control functions referred to in paragraph T14.6 is activated.

The Instructions and signals referred to above may relate either to the Active Power injection/absorption of the Storage System or to the total Active Power injection/absorption of the Storage Facility or the RES Unit. During the connection process stage, the relevant requirement to be satisfied by the Electricity Storage Facility or the RES Unit shall be specified.

In the case of receiving a signal from the TSOC, the Storage System must adjust the Active Power with an accuracy tolerance of ±5%.

In the event that the control system is out of service, the Active Power shall be adjusted via local regulation within a timeframe of 15 minutes with an accuracy of ±10%.

---

### T14.4.1.5 Active Power Operating Range

Storage Systems shall be capable of varying their Active Power injection/absorption across the entire nominal range (0–100%), provided there are no limitations regarding the Available Energy.

---

### T14.4.1.6 Active Power Ramp Rate Limits

The Active Power Ramp Rate is divided into three categories in order of priority as defined below:

1. **Ramp Rate for operation with limitations due to Available Energy**
2. **Ramp Rate for FSM, LFSM-O, or LFSM-U operation**
3. **Ramp Rate for normal operation**

In case of operation with limitations due to Available Energy, the Storage System shall be capable of reducing the Active Power injection/absorption to zero linearly, with a Ramp Rate between 1% – 100% of the Maximum Charging/Discharging Capacity per minute (with a default value of 50% or according to an instruction received from the TSOC).

<!-- PAGE 246 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T14 – Additional Provisions for Energy Storage Systems** | **Page 246**

---

The requirements regarding the Ramp Rate for FSM, LFSM-O, or LFSM-U operation are specified in Paragraph T14.6.

The Ramp Rate for normal operation shall be capable of being set between 1% - 100% per minute in accordance with the instruction received from the TSOC (with a default value of 20% per minute) or in accordance with the Instructions issued by the TSOC in the context of the operation of the Balancing Market.

The above limits shall apply to all operating stages (start-up, shutdown, etc.) unless specified otherwise.

The transition from injection mode to absorption mode and vice versa shall take place as quickly as possible. The transition time shall not exceed two seconds unless specified otherwise by the TSOC.

### T14.4.1.7 Reactive Power Injection/Absorption Requirements

The Energy Storage System shall have the capability to inject or absorb Reactive Power for the purpose of Voltage control at the Connection Point.

The TSOC may specify particular requirements for Reactive Power provision depending on the prevailing conditions at the Connection Point. The requirements shall be capable of being met by automatic Reactive Power control devices.

### T14.4.1.8 Reactive Power Operating Range

The minimum acceptable Reactive Power operating range is defined as the value $|Q/P| = 0.33$.

When the Active Power injection/absorption is equal to the Maximum Charging/Discharging Capacity, the limits of the Reactive Power operating range depend on the operating Voltage at the Connection Point as specified in Diagram 14.1.

> [Technical Diagram Architecture Note: Diagram 14.1: Reactive Power Operating Range for P=Pmax]
> The diagram illustrates the required Reactive Power operating envelope as a profile of $V/V_n$ (per-unit voltage at the Connection Point) against $Q/P_{max}$ (normalized reactive power ratio):
> - **Vertical Axis ($V/V_n$):** Operating voltage ratio ranging from 0.9 to 1.1 p.u., with marked thresholds at 0.90, 0.95, 1.05, and 1.10.
> - **Horizontal Axis ($Q/P_{max}$):** Reactive power ratio ranging from -0.33 (absorption) to +0.33 (injection).
> - **Operating Boundary:** A closed profile defining the required capability area:
>   - At $V/V_n = 1.10$, reactive power capability ranges from $0$ to $+0.33$.
>   - Slopes downwards from $V/V_n = 1.05$ at $Q/P_{max} = -0.33$ to $V/V_n = 1.10$ at $Q/P_{max} = 0$.
>   - At $V/V_n = 0.90$, reactive power capability ranges from $-0.33$ to $0$.
>   - Slopes upwards from $V/V_n = 0.90$ at $Q/P_{max} = 0$ to $V/V_n = 0.95$ at $Q/P_{max} = +0.33$.

**Diagram 14.1:** Reactive Power Operating Range for $P = P_{max}$

<!-- PAGE 247 -->

When the Active Power injection/absorption is less than the Maximum Charging/Discharging Capacity (including zero value), the Storage System shall be capable of operating within the entire range defined in the P-Q/Pmax diagram in Figure 14.2, while simultaneously adhering to the setting of the U-Q/Pmax profile defined in Figure 14.1 (for P=P_max).

> [Technical Diagram Architecture Note: Figure 14.2: Reactive Power operating range for P<Pmax]

## T14.5 ROBUSTNESS REQUIREMENTS

### T14.5.1 Low Voltage Ride Through (LVRT) Capability

In the event of a symmetrical or asymmetrical fault resulting in the Voltage at the Connection Point dipping below the normal operating limit, Storage Systems shall operate in accordance with the requirements specified below.

In accordance with the fault ride through requirements in the case of a Voltage dip at the Connection Point below the normal operating limit due to a disturbance, the Storage System must be capable of remaining connected to the grid and continuing to operate stably as long as the Voltage magnitude (in this case defined as the minimum value among all measured line-to-line and phase voltages) remains above the lower limit specified in Figure 14.3.

The parameter values shown in Figure 14.3 are specified in Table 14.3.

---

| Parameter | Value |
|---|---|

| Umin | 0.05–0.15 p.u. (default 0.05 p.u.) |
| U1 | 0.9 p.u. |
| U2 | 0.85 p.u. |
| t1 | 0.15–0.25 seconds (default 0.2 seconds) |
| t2 | 1.5–3 seconds (default 3 seconds) |
| t3 | 60 minutes |

The variable parameters shall be set locally.

### T14.5.2 High Voltage Ride Through (HVRT) Capability

Storage Systems must have the capability to operate stably without disconnecting from the grid in cases of symmetrical or asymmetrical voltage rise at the Connection Point, in accordance with Diagram 14.4.

Diagram 14.4 specifies the upper Voltage limit for which Energy Storage Systems must remain connected, as a function of time before, during, and after the occurrence of a disturbance causing a Voltage swell. The relevant parameters are specified in Table 14.4.

### Table 14.4: Parameters for capability of continuous operation under high Voltage

| Parameter | Value |

| U2 | 1.2 p.u. |
| U3 | 1.1 p.u. |

TSOC may specify longer operating times if required to maintain or restore the security of the Electrical System. The User shall not unreasonably refuse to implement longer operating times, taking into account the technical capability of the Equipment.

The variable parameters shall be set locally.

### T14.5.3 Active Power Recovery after Voltage Disturbance

Energy Storage Systems that have not disconnected from the grid during a potential fault shall be capable of restoring Active Power injection/absorption according to the selected current priority, as specified in T14.5.4.

### T14.5.4 Fast Fault Current Control for Voltage Support after a Voltage Disturbance

The provision of Fast Fault Current aims at fault detection by protection systems in the initial phase of the fault, Voltage support and maintenance in a subsequent phase, and Voltage restoration after fault clearance.

<!-- PAGE 250 -->

**Edition 1.1.0** | **T14 – Additional Provisions for Energy Storage Systems** | **Page 250**

---

The requirements for Fast Fault Current injection shall apply to both symmetrical faults and asymmetrical voltage variation faults.

The Storage System must be capable of activating Fast Fault Current supply by measuring Voltage deviations, either at the Connection Point or at the terminals of individual units of the Storage System.

The provision of Fast Fault Current shall be activated when the Voltage deviates outside the normal operating range specified in paragraph T1.8.3.1.

The provision of Fast Fault Current shall be in addition to the pre-fault operating condition of the Storage System.

Under Voltage dip conditions, Fast Fault Current shall be supplied (injected), whereas under Voltage swell conditions, it shall be absorbed.

The detection/activation time for the provision of Fast Fault Current shall be less than $t_1 = 20\text{ ms}$, as shown in Diagram 14.5.

The Storage System shall reach 90% of the additional reactive current in less than $t_2 = 50\text{ ms}$ and settle within a tolerance band of $\pm 10\%$ in less than $t_3 = 80\text{ ms}$ from the onset of the fault, as shown in Diagram 14.5.

> [Technical Diagram Architecture Note: Diagram 14.5: Response Time for Fast Fault Current Control]
> *Diagram Details:*
> - **Y-axis:** Current (indicating 90% Setpoint, Setpoint, and 110% Setpoint)
> - **X-axis:** Time ($0$, $t_1$, $t_2$, $t_3$)
> - **Key Parameters:**
>   - $t_1 = 20\text{ ms}$ (Detection / Activation time)
>   - $t_2 = 50\text{ ms}$ (Time to reach 90% of additional reactive current)
>   - $t_3 = 80\text{ ms}$ (Settling time within $\pm 10\%$ tolerance band)

The provision of reactive current shall be maintained for an additional 20–30 ms after the Voltage recovers within normal operating limits.

<!-- PAGE 251 -->

**Version 1.1.0**  
**T14 – Additional Provisions for Storage Systems**  
*Page 251*

---

The maximum total current during the provision of Fast Fault Current Injection ($I_{total}^{max}$), as defined by the manufacturer, shall be equal to or greater than the nominal operating current of the unit.

The Fast Fault Current shall be proportional to the Voltage variation at the Connection Point of the Storage System, as defined in Diagram 14.6.

> [Technical Diagram Architecture Note: Diagram 14.6: Fast Fault Current Control — Illustrates the relationship between the additional reactive current ($\Delta I_{q,1}, \Delta I_{q,2}$) and the sequence voltage deviation ($\Delta u_1, \Delta u_2$). The curve indicates a voltage deadband ("Νεκρή Ζώνη") between -0.1 and 0.1 p.u., with adjustable gain slope parameters $2 \le k \le 6$.]

where:

$$\Delta I_{q,1} = -K_1 * (U_1^{fault} - U_1^{pre})$$

* $\Delta I_{q,1}$: Additional positive sequence reactive current  
* $U_1^{fault}$: Positive sequence fault voltage (p.u.)  
* $U_1^{pre}$: Positive sequence voltage prior to the fault (p.u.)  
* $K_1$: Positive sequence control gain factor  

$$\Delta I_{q,2} = -K_2 * (U_2^{fault} - U_2^{pre})$$

* $\Delta I_{q,2}$: Additional negative sequence reactive current  
* $U_2^{fault}$: Negative sequence fault voltage (p.u.)  
* $U_2^{pre}$: Negative sequence voltage prior to the fault (p.u.)  
* $K_2$: Negative sequence control gain factor  

The total positive and negative sequence reactive current after the fault is defined as:

$$I_{q,1} = \Delta I_{q,1} + I_{q,1}^{pre}, \qquad I_{q,2} = \Delta I_{q,2} + I_{q,2}^{pre}$$

<!-- PAGE 252 -->

Version 1.1.0 | T14 – Additional Provisions for Energy Storage Systems | Page 252

TSOC may examine any variation in the calculation equations of $\Delta I_{q,1}$ and $\Delta I_{q,2}$ upon request of the User.

In the event that the total current ($\sqrt{I_q^2 + I_d^2}$) reaches the maximum value in accordance with the Registered Operating Characteristics of the Energy Storage System, TSOC shall be able to define priority for Active and Reactive Power according to the requirements at the respective Connection Point. For this purpose, three predefined priority settings are defined:

I. **Reactive current priority**: Priority is given to the provision of reactive current. If the reactive current value calculated from the negative and positive sequence values (as derived from the above equations) is greater than $I_{\text{total}}^{\text{max}}$, then the negative and positive sequence values are reduced proportionally. If the value is less than $I_{\text{total}}^{\text{max}}$, the remaining capacity is used for the provision of active current (positive sequence). Energy Storage Systems shall be able to recover 90% of the pre-fault Active Power as quickly as possible and no later than 150 ms after the restoration of Voltage at the Connection Point within normal operating limits.

II. **Active current priority**: Priority is given to the positive sequence Active Power (i.e., maintaining the pre-fault active current value without allowing its reduction). The remaining capacity (with respect to $I_{\text{total}}^{\text{max}}$) is used for the provision of reactive current. If the value of the reactive current calculated from the negative and positive sequence values (as derived from the above equations) is greater than the remaining capacity, then the negative and positive sequence values are reduced proportionally. Active Power must remain proportional to the maintained Voltage level.

III. **Reactive current priority for the first 150 ms**: Priority is given to reactive current as explained in point I for the first 150 ms, and subsequently priority reverts to active current as described in point II.

The parameters regarding the provision of Fast Fault Current are summarized in Table 14.5.

**Table 14.5: Fast Fault Current Adjustment Requirements**

| Parameter | Value |
|---|---|
| Deadband | $\pm 0.1$ p.u. |
| $K_1^*$ | 0 – 6 (Default 2) |
| $K_2^*$ | 0 – 6 (Default 0) |
| Step $K$ | 0.5 |
| Current injection initiation time after fault ($t_1$) | $\le 20$ milliseconds (msec) |
| Time to supply 90% of the setpoint ($t_2$) | $\le 50$ milliseconds (msec) |
| Settling time ($t_3$) | $\le 80$ milliseconds (msec) |

<!-- PAGE 253 -->

| Parameter / Operational Feature | Specification / Requirement |
|---|---|
| $I_{\text{total}}^{\text{max}}$ | Maximum total current during Fast Fault Current provision |
| Current priority | Default active current priority (point II above) |

\* Ability to adjust the range of constants $K_1$ and $K_2$ shall be provided.

---

### T14.6 ACTIVE POWER CONTROL REQUIREMENTS

#### T14.6.1 Active Power Regulation

Storage Systems must be equipped with a communication interface to adjust active power injection/absorption upon receiving a signal at the input port from the TSOC. Additionally, they must possess the capability to receive and execute Instructions issued by the TSOC within the framework of the Balancing Market operation.

The Instructions and signals referred to above may concern either the Active Power injection/absorption of the Storage System or the total Active Power injection/absorption of the Storage Facility or RES Unit. During the connection process stage, the specific requirement that the Electricity Storage Facility or RES Unit must satisfy shall be defined.

Storage Systems shall have the capability, in the event of a Disturbance Event, to adjust Active Power injection/absorption in order to restore Frequency to desired levels, provided that the Frequency and relevant time periods are within the limits specified in paragraph T14.4.1.1.

The TSOC shall have the capability:

1) To adjust the parameters of Active Power control functions via a signal sent from the SCADA/EMS.
2) To enable and disable Active Power control capabilities via a signal sent from the SCADA/EMS.

Storage Systems that form part of an Electricity Storage Facility shall have the capability to provide Automatic Frequency Restoration Reserve (aFRR) (i.e., capability to operate with an Automatic Generation Control - AGC system) and Manual Frequency Restoration Reserve (mFRR).

When in standby mode, Storage Systems must have the capability to execute Dispatch Instructions or provide Ancillary Services without additional delay due to the standby state. It is highlighted that Storage Systems are obliged to be in standby mode for all time intervals during which they participate in the Balancing Market (participation in the Balancing Market is defined as the submission of bids for balancing energy and provision of reserves).

<!-- PAGE 254 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T14 – Additional Provisions for Storage Systems** | **Page 254**

---

### T14.6.2 Limited Frequency Sensitivity Mode — Overfrequency (LFSM-O)

When the Power System is in an Overfrequency state, the Storage System shall have the capability to operate in **Limited Frequency Sensitivity Mode — Overfrequency (LFSM-O)**, during which Active Power is adjusted according to the variation in Frequency in order to restore Frequency to values below a predefined setpoint $f_1$.

Depending on the operational state of the Storage System prior to exceeding the predefined setpoint $f_1$, the following cases are distinguished:

1) **If the Storage System was in Active Power injection mode**, it shall decrease its injected power or even transition to absorption mode until the Frequency falls below the predefined setpoint $f_1$ or until constraints regarding Available Energy arise.

2) **If the Storage System is in Active Power absorption mode**, it shall increase its absorbed power until the Frequency falls below the predefined setpoint $f_1$ or until constraints regarding Available Energy arise.

During operation under constraints due to Available Energy, the Rate of Change shall satisfy the criteria defined in paragraph T14.4.1.6.

The transition between Active Power injection and absorption states must take place within a time limit and Rate of Change as specified in paragraph T14.4.1.6.

The Storage System shall be capable of applying droop settings between 2% and 12%, with a default setpoint of 4%, and an Overfrequency threshold between 50.2 Hz and 50.5 Hz inclusive, with a default threshold of $f_1 = 50.5 \text{ Hz}$. The operational tolerance range at the maximum contribution level is $\pm 5\%$ of the maximum output power $P_{max}$.

When **Limited Frequency Sensitivity Mode — Overfrequency** is activated, control commands from LFSM-O regulation shall override any other Active Power control setpoint target that tends toward results opposite to those intended.

An indicative response characteristic for LFSM-O operation is presented in Diagram 14.7, with parameters specified in Table 14.6.

<!-- PAGE 255 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T14 – Additional Provisions for Energy Storage Systems** | **Page 255**

---

> [Technical Diagram Architecture Note: Diagram 14.7: Response characteristic in LFSM-O operation]

### Table 14.6: Response parameters in LFSM-O operation

| Parameter | Value |
| :--- | :--- |
| $f_1$ | 50.2 - 50.5 Hz (default 50.5 Hz) |
| Droop<br>$$s(\%) = 100 \cdot \left( \frac{\lvert\Delta f\rvert - \lvert\Delta f_1\rvert}{f_n} \cdot \frac{P_{\max}}{\lvert\Delta P\rvert} \right)$$ | 2 – 12 % (default 4%) |
| Response time<br>(time to 90% of the target value) | $\le 2\text{ seconds}$ for a 50% change of maximum power |
| $P_{\max}$ | The maximum active power output |

---

### T14.6.3 Limited Frequency Sensitive Mode — Underfrequency (LFSM-U)

When the Power System is in an Underfrequency state, the Energy Storage System shall have the capability to operate in Limited Frequency Sensitive Mode — Underfrequency (LFSM-U), during which Active Power varies according to the Frequency variation in order to restore the Frequency to values greater than a predefined threshold ($f_1$).

Depending on the operating state of the Energy Storage System prior to crossing the predefined threshold ($f_1$), the following cases are distinguished:

<!-- PAGE 256 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T14 – Additional Provisions for Energy Storage Systems** | **Page 256**

---

1) If the Energy Storage System was in Active Power injection mode, it shall increase the injected power until the Frequency recovers above the predefined value $f_1$ or until limitations regarding Available Energy arise.

2) If the Energy Storage System was in Active Power charging/absorption mode, it shall decrease the absorbed power or even transition to injection mode until the Frequency recovers above the predefined value $f_1$ or until limitations regarding Available Energy arise.

The Energy Storage System shall comply with the requirements regarding the Rate of Change of Active Power set forth in paragraph T14.4.1.6 during operation under constraints due to Available Energy.

The transition between Active Power injection and absorption mode shall take place within a time limit and Rate of Change as specified in paragraph T14.4.1.6.

The Energy Storage System shall be capable of applying droop settings between 2–12% with a default value of 4% and an Underfrequency threshold between 49.8 Hz and 49.5 Hz inclusive, with a default threshold of $f_1 = 49.5\text{ Hz}$. The operational tolerance range at maximum contribution level is $\pm 5\%$ of the maximum active power output $P_{\text{max}}$.

When Limited Frequency Sensitivity Mode — Underfrequency (LFSM-U) is activated, the control commands from LFSM-U regulation shall override any other target Active Power control setpoint that produces counteracting effects.

An indicative response characteristic for LFSM-U operation is presented in Figure 14.8, with parameters specified in Table 14.7.

> [Technical Diagram Architecture Note: Figure 14.8: Response characteristic curve for LFSM-U operation mode, illustrating active power variation $\Delta P / P_{\text{max}}$ (p.u.) versus Frequency (Hz). It depicts generation/discharging and consumption/charging operational regions, the droop slope, threshold frequency $f_1$, and deadband $\Delta f_1$.]

**Figure 14.8: Response characteristic in LFSM-U mode**

**Table 14.7: Response parameters in LFSM-U mode**

| Parameter | Value |
|---|---|
| $f_1$ | 49.8 – 49.5 Hz (default 49.5 Hz) |

<!-- PAGE 257 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T14 – Additional Provisions for Storage Systems** | **Page 257**

---

| Parameter | Value / Requirement |
|---|---|
| **Droop**<br>$s(\%) = 100 \cdot \left( \frac{\vert\Delta f\vert - \vert\Delta f_1\vert}{f_n} \cdot \frac{P_{max}}{\vert\Delta P\vert} \right)$ | 2 – 12 % (default 4%) |
| **Response time**<br>(time to 90% of the target value) | $\le 2$ seconds for a 50% change of maximum power |
| $P_{max}$ | Maximum active power output |

---

### T14.6.4 Frequency Sensitivity Mode (FSM)

In case of small Frequency variations around the Target Frequency, the Storage System shall have the capability to operate in Frequency Sensitivity Mode (FSM) to participate in Frequency Containment Reserve (FCR).

Active Power injection or absorption shall be regulated for Frequency deviations within a predefined range ($\Delta f_2$).

To avoid controller action for very small Frequency variations, a "deadband" $\Delta f_1$ is defined relative to the acceptable Frequency sensitivity, during which the Frequency does not affect the Active Power of the Storage System. The deadband range shall be capable of being modified via local setting.

When the Frequency is lower than $(50 - \Delta f_1)\text{ mHz}$, the Storage System shall, depending on its operating state prior to the violation of the predefined setpoint:
1) Increase Active Power if it was in injection mode;
2) Decrease Active Power if it was in absorption mode, or even transition to injection mode.

When the Frequency is higher than $(50 + \Delta f_1)\text{ mHz}$, the Storage System shall, depending on its operating state prior to the violation of the predefined setpoint:
1) Increase Active Power if it was in absorption mode;
2) Decrease Active Power if it was in injection mode, or even transition to absorption mode.

The Active Power change shall be maintained until the Frequency returns to the "deadband", or until the Active Power value becomes equal to the Maximum Charging/Discharging Power Capacity, or until constraints regarding Available Energy arise.

In case of further Frequency variation (beyond $\Delta f_1$), beyond operation in FSM mode, the Storage System shall additionally operate in LFSM-O or LFSM-U mode (depending on the Frequency value), in accordance with paragraphs T14.6.3 and T14.6.4. This requirement applies when the parameters of FSM operation in combination with the operating conditions of the Storage System prior to the fault permit it.

<!-- PAGE 258 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T14 – Additional Provisions for Storage Systems** | **Page 258**

---

The Storage System shall be capable of activating Frequency Sensitive Mode (FSM) operating mode in accordance with Diagram 14.9 and values specified by Table 14.8.

> [Technical Diagram Architecture Note: Diagram 14.9: Response characteristic in FSM mode]
> * **Vertical Axis:** Active Power $\Delta P / P_{max}$ (p.u.) / $\frac{|\Delta P|}{P_{max}}$
> * **Horizontal Axis:** Frequency (Hz)
> * **Key Parameters & Curves:**
>   * Generation / Discharging Curve (Upper Quadrant)
>   * Consumption / Charging Curve (Lower Quadrant)
>   * Droop Slope (Κλίση)
>   * Deadband ($\Delta f_1$) [Νεκρό σημείο]
>   * Frequency deviation limit ($\Delta f_2$)

In the event of a step change in Frequency, the change in Active Power shall commence at time $t_1$ and reach its maximum value at time $t_2$, as specified in Table 14.8 and depicted in Diagram 14.10.

> [Technical Diagram Architecture Note: Diagram 14.10: Response time in case of a frequency step change]
> * **Vertical Axis:** Active Power $\Delta P / P_{max}$ (p.u.) / $\frac{|\Delta P|}{P_{max}}$
> * **Horizontal Axis:** Time (seconds)
> * **Key Parameters & Curves:**
>   * Initial response delay time ($t_1$)
>   * Full activation time / maximum value time ($t_2$)
>   * Deadband ($\Delta f_1$) indication

<!-- PAGE 259 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T14 – Additional Provisions for Energy Storage Systems** | **Page 259**

---

### Table 14.8: Response parameters in FSM mode

| Parameters | Values |
|---|---|
| **Slope/Droop**<br>$s(\%) = 100 \cdot \left( \frac{|\Delta f| - |\Delta f_1|}{f_n} \cdot \frac{P_{max}}{|\Delta P|} \right)$ | 2–27% |
| **Operating range ($\Delta f_2$)** | 0–±500 mHz (predefined by OMP) |
| **Maximum combined frequency sensitivity and deadband ($\Delta f_1$)** | 10–50 mHz |
| $\frac{P_{max}}{\| \Delta P_1 \|}$ | Maximum active power of the unit:<br>1.5 – 100 % |
| $\frac{P_{max}}{t_1}$ | $\le 0.5$ seconds for units without inertia |
| $t_2$ | $\le 5\text{–}20$ seconds (predefined by OMP) |
| **Minimum supply duration** | 15 – 30 minutes (predefined by OMP) |

If required by TSOC, the Storage System shall provide Frequency Containment Reserve (FCR) with parameters within the range specified in this paragraph, which shall be specified by TSOC.

---

### T14.6.5 Fast Frequency Response (FFR) Reserve

The requirements regarding the provision of Fast Frequency Response (FFR) Reserve by Storage Systems are specified in Chapter T5 and the Operational Margin Policy.

---

### T14.7 REACTIVE POWER CONTROL REQUIREMENTS

#### T14.7.1 Reactive Power Control

Storage Systems shall be equipped with a communication interface to be capable of receiving external signals from TSOC regarding target operational values concerning Reactive Power injection/absorption.

All Storage Systems shall have the capability to provide Reactive Power control operating in one of the three following operating conditions:

1) Reactive Power control mode with respect to Voltage at the Connection Point
2) Reactive Power control mode
3) Power Factor control mode

<!-- PAGE 260 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T14 – Additional Provisions for Energy Storage Systems** | **Page 260**

---

Storage Systems shall have the capability to provide Reactive Power control in one of the three aforementioned operating modes, including under conditions of zero Active Power injection/absorption.

The operating mode of the Storage System with respect to Reactive Power control shall be determined based on a control signal or instruction received from TSOC through other means.

The Instructions and signals referred to above may apply either to the Reactive Power injection/absorption of the Storage System itself or to the total Reactive Power injection/absorption of the Electricity Storage Facility or the RES Unit. During the connection process stage, the relevant requirement to be satisfied by the Electricity Storage Facility or the RES Unit shall be specified.

In the event of loss of communication with TSOC or any fault preventing the reception of control signals, the control unit shall automatically switch to Reactive Power control mode as a function of Voltage at the Connection Point, in accordance with the Reactive Power–Voltage characteristic curve shown in Diagram 14.11, unless otherwise specified by TSOC.

Any other Voltage/Reactive Power control device of the facility (such as fixed or variable capacitor banks, SVC, STATCOM, or other FACTS devices, etc.) shall properly coordinate with the remaining equipment of the facility to satisfy the requirements.

### T14.7.1.1 Response time for Reactive Power provision

Upon receipt of a signal from TSOC regarding targeted operating setpoints for Reactive Power injection/absorption, the target setpoint value of the controlled parameter shall be implemented by the Storage System within 30 seconds following receipt of the corresponding signal.

### T14.7.2 Reactive Power control mode based on Voltage at the Connection Point

The Storage System shall have the capability to regulate Reactive Power flow at the Connection Point with a constant Voltage–Reactive Power droop slope when the Voltage at the Connection Point deviates from a desired setpoint, as shown in Diagram 14.11, based on the parameters specified in Table 14.9.

<!-- PAGE 261 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T14 – Additional Provisions for Storage Systems** | **Page 261**

---

> [Technical Diagram Architecture Note: Diagram 14.11: Reactive Power – Voltage Characteristic]

#### Table 14.9: Operating parameters for reactive power – voltage control

| Parameters | Value Range |
| :--- | :--- |
| Voltage setpoint (target value) range | 0.95 – 1.05 p.u. (default 1 p.u.) |
| Voltage setpoint step | <0.01 p.u. |
| Deadband | ±0 − 5% (default 0%) |
| Deadband step | 0.5% |
| Slope | 2% - 7% (default 5%) |
| Slope step | <0.5% |
| Activation time t1 | 1 second |
| Settling time t2 | 5 seconds |

The operating parameters shall be specified in accordance with the default values defined in Table 14.9, unless the TSOC, taking into account the grid characteristics and requirements at the Connection Point, deems it necessary to specify different requirements. In such case, the operating parameters shall be determined by the TSOC and communicated to the User during the technical study stage or after the execution of the Connection Agreement.

The operating parameters may be modified in accordance with the requirements of the TSOC within the limits specified in Table 14.9, and the User is obligated to comply.

The Voltage – Reactive Power slope ($\Delta V/\Delta Q$) is defined as the ratio of the Voltage deviation $\Delta V$ from the Voltage setpoint at the Connection Point (expressed in p.u. of the reference value at the Connection Point) to the corresponding change in Reactive Power flow $\Delta Q$ at the Connection Point (expressed in p.u. of the maximum Reactive Power of the Storage System).

The Voltage setpoint can operate with or without a deadband.

<!-- PAGE 262 -->

In the event of a step change in Voltage, the Storage System shall be capable of achieving 90% of the Reactive Power value specified pursuant to Diagram 14.11 within time $t_1$ and stabilizing the Reactive Power at this value (as specified pursuant to Diagram 14.11) within a time limit $t_2$.

### T14.7.3 Reactive Power Control Operation

During the Reactive Power control operation mode, the Storage System shall be capable of regulating the Reactive Power at the Connection Point such that it is equal to the target value set by the TSOC.

The operating Reactive Power may be set anywhere within the range of values specified in paragraph T14.4.1.8 with a step size not exceeding 5% of the maximum Reactive Power.

### T14.7.4 Power Factor Control Operation

During the Power Factor control operation mode, the Storage System shall be capable of controlling the Power Factor at the Connection Point such that it is equal to the target value set by the TSOC.

The operational setpoint values shall be applied within the range of values specified in paragraph T14.4.1.8, with a step size $\Delta\cos\phi \le 0.01$ of the target Power Factor value of the Storage System. When no Power Factor value is specified at the Connection Point, it shall be set equal to unity (1.0).

---

## T14.8 SYNCHRONISATION REQUIREMENTS WITH THE NETWORK

### T14.8.1 Synchronisation procedure

The synchronisation procedure of the Storage System shall be executed by Users upon receiving relevant approval from the TSOC.

The TSOC and the User shall agree on the settings of the synchronisation devices prior to putting the Storage System into operation. This agreement covers the following parameters with indicative values specified in Table 14.10:

1. Voltage
2. Frequency
3. Phase sequence matching
4. Observation time
5. Active power rate of change

The observation time shall be capable of being adjusted locally.

---
*Version 1.1.0 | T14 – Additional Provisions for Energy Storage Systems | Page 262*

<!-- PAGE 263 -->

### Table 14.10: Synchronization parameter requirements

| Parameter | Value |
|---|---|
| Voltage | 0.9 – 1.1 p.u. |
| Frequency for connection in injection mode | 48 – 50.5 Hz |
| Frequency for connection in absorption mode | 49.5 – 52 Hz |
| Observation time | Adjustable from 0 to 300 seconds (default 60 seconds) |
| Active power rate of change | Within the range defined in paragraph T14.4.1.6 |

> [Technical Diagram Architecture Note: Diagram 14.12: Characteristic curve of Active Power ramp rate following synchronization of the Storage System]
> * **Y-Axis:** $|P|$ Active Power
> * **X-Axis:** Time
> * **Observation Window:** Adjustable observation time between 0–300 seconds before ramping commences.
> * **Slope:** Active power ramp rate (power rate of change) constraint after synchronization.

---

### T14.8.2 Disconnection procedure

Disconnection of Storage Systems is permitted upon approval by the TSOC.

Storage Systems shall be equipped with a logic interface for disconnection based on a signal received from the TSOC.

During disconnection, they shall comply with the power Rate of Change limits specified in paragraph T14.4.1.6.

> [Technical Diagram Architecture Note: Diagram 14.13: Characteristic curve of Active Power ramp-down rate during disconnection of the Storage System]
> * **Y-Axis:** $|P|$ Active Power, starting from operating active power $|P_{oper}|$
> * **X-Axis:** Time
> * **Slope:** Ramp-down slope (power rate of change) during controlled disconnection.

---

### T14.8.3 Automatic reconnection

---
*Version 1.1.0 | T14 – Additional Provisions for Electricity Storage Systems | Page 263*

<!-- PAGE 264 -->

**Edition 1.1.0** | **T14 – Additional Provisions for Storage Systems** | **Page 264**

---

In cases of automatic disconnection due to the occurrence of a network disturbance, automatic reconnection of the Storage System is not permitted.

### T14.9 IMPACT ON THE TRANSMISSION AND DISTRIBUTION SYSTEM

#### T14.9.1 Steady-State Voltage Variation

The steady-state Voltage variation (increase or decrease) at the Connection Point (at both High Voltage and Medium Voltage), resulting from the connection and operation of the Storage System, must not exceed 2%.

#### T14.9.2 Rapid Voltage Changes

Rapid Voltage changes at the Connection Point that may be caused by switching operations (connection and disconnection) of the Storage System must not cause a violation of the limits specified in EN 61000-3-7.

#### T14.9.3 Short-Circuit Level

Users are required to provide information regarding the single-phase-to-ground, phase-to-phase-to-ground, and three-phase contribution to short-circuit currents at the Connection Point under nominal conditions.

The connection of the Storage System is expected to increase network short-circuit levels. If, following studies, it is demonstrated that a Storage System increases network short-circuit levels beyond the desired thresholds or beyond the short-circuit breaking capacity of the network switching equipment, the User must take appropriate measures, subject to approval by the TSOC, to limit short-circuit levels.

It is noted that short-circuit levels for the Transmission System are specified accordingly in paragraph T1.8.6 of the Transmission Rules.

At specific points (where required), different limits are specified by the TSOC on a case-by-case basis in the Connection Agreement.

#### T14.9.4 Power Quality – Harmonic Distortion

To monitor Power Quality at the Connection Point, the User is obligated to install a recording system in accordance with the requirements of the TSOC and the terms of the Connection Agreement.

Harmonic currents injected by the Storage System must not cause Individual Voltage Harmonics at the Connection Point exceeding 1.5% for harmonic orders from 2 to 50, nor a Total Harmonic Distortion (THD) exceeding 2%. Harmonic currents shall be measured in accordance with standard IEC 61000-4-7.

#### T14.9.5 Main Transformer Requirements

<!-- PAGE 265 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T14 – Additional Provisions for Storage Systems** | **Page 265**

***

MV/HV transformers of Storage Systems must be equipped with an On-Load Tap Changer (OLTC), capable of remote activation.

The design specifications of the OLTC and the applied settings shall be approved by the TSOC.

The tap step change is not allowed to vary the Voltage ratio at the High Voltage terminal busbars by more than 1.6% in the section of the Transmission System operating at 132 kV and more than 2.5% in the section of the Transmission System operating at 66 kV, unless otherwise agreed with the TSOC.

### T14.10 SIMULATION MODELS

#### T14.10.1 Introduction

This subsection supplements T2.8, without replacing it.

All Users shall provide suitable and accurate simulation models in accordance with the requirements specified in the Transmission Rules and/or the Connection Agreement.

The Storage System model must be validated against laboratory test results. The model must be compatible with the software packages used by the TSOC and must not depend on additional software.

#### T14.10.2 Model Requirements

The model must be capable of representing facility behavior in load flow studies, short-circuit studies, balanced positive-sequence root-mean-square time-domain simulations (Phasor RMS time domain simulations), harmonic studies, and, where specified, electromagnetic transient studies.

The model shall be capable of implementing any simulation compliance requirements defined by the TSOC.

The validation of the simulation model is the responsibility of the User and may be performed by third parties (manufacturer, consultant, authorized certification body in accordance with Regulation (EC) No 765/2008 and accreditation standard ISO/IEC 17065).

In addition to the main equipment of the Storage System, all remaining equipment up to the Connection Point must also be modeled. This may include transformers, auxiliary equipment, reactive power compensation plant, central controllers, and any other controllers or active components.

<!-- PAGE 266 -->

**Version 1.1.0 | T14 – Additional Provisions for Energy Storage Systems | Page 266**

For the representation of the harmonic study, the harmonic source type “IEC 61000” shall be used, and all integer and non-integer harmonics from the 2nd harmonic up to the 50th harmonic with a step of 0.5 shall be updated.

The model shall be accompanied by an appropriate manual (documentation) describing in detail its development and verification process.

### T14.10.3 Model Standards

The Storage System model shall be compatible with the PowerFactory DIgSILENT system analysis software, appropriately parameterizing the WECC library.

The protective devices and remaining equipment used shall originate from the standard DIgSILENT library, and a detailed description of each model element shall be provided.

Different model types are acceptable only for modeling specific functions that cannot be modeled through parameterization of the WECC library.

### T14.11 INFORMATION EXCHANGE REQUIREMENTS

#### T14.11.1 Data and signal exchange requirements

All Storage Systems shall have the capability to exchange real-time information with the TSOC. This information may consist of Commands, indications, and/or measurements of the respective Storage System.

The provision of signals and indications that Users are required to provide to the TSOC shall be achieved via a telecommunications connection and the installation of a Remote Terminal Unit (RTU).

Remote Terminal Units shall support the communication protocols specified by the TSOC.

The precise list of required signals shall be determined by the TSOC prior to entering into a Connection Agreement. In this part of the Transmission Code, indicative signals are mentioned.

When the TSOC reasonably determines that due to a Modification to the Transmission System or in order to satisfy a Transmission System requirement, supplementary signals and/or indications related to the User's Facilities and Plant are required, the TSOC shall inform the User accordingly. Upon receipt of this notification, the User shall ensure that the supplementary signals and/or indications are made available.

The required signals may be either analog or digital signals, depending on their function.

1) Digital signals are signals defining the operational status of controllers, protective devices, or any other equipment utilizing 2-state signals (“0” or “1”).

<!-- PAGE 267 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T14 – Additional Provisions for Storage Systems** | **Page 267**

---

2) Analog signals are signals used to represent continuous real values of system parameters.

All measurement data must be time-synchronized with Eastern European Time (GMT +2 hours) and adjusted annually for daylight saving time.

### T14.11.2 Analog signals

All Storage Systems shall be capable of exchanging at least the following analog signals regarding Active/Reactive Power control functions (where a positive sign is defined as injection and a negative sign as absorption at the respective Connection Point):

1. Active Power control setpoint (MW) (see paragraph T14.6.1)
2. Reactive Power control setpoint (MVAr) (see paragraph T14.7.1)
3. Rate of change of injected/absorbed active/reactive power (MW/min and MVAr/min)
4. FRR setpoint (MW) (Where applicable)
5. Droop values for LFSM-O/U, FSM (%)
6. Under-frequency/over-frequency threshold for LFSM-O/U, deadband for FSM, operating threshold for FSM (Hz)
7. FFR activation threshold (Hz)
8. Maximum FFR power (MW)
9. Voltage setpoint of Q-V characteristic (pu), deadband of Q-V characteristic (pu), droop value of Q-V characteristic (%).

All Storage Systems shall be capable of providing at least the following analog signals regarding measurements (where a positive sign is defined as injection and a negative sign as absorption at the respective Connection Point):

1. Active/Reactive Power (MW/MVAr)
2. Current (A)
3. Frequency (Hz)
4. Voltage (kV)
5. Bus coupler breaker current (A) (in case of substations with multiple MV busbars)
6. Reactive Power of any other compensation devices (MVAr)
7. Meteorological condition measurements (Incident solar irradiance per square meter, Ambient Temperature, Wind speed)
8. Available Energy (% of total)
9. Power Quality Recorder measurements

All Storage Systems shall be capable of providing at least the following analog signals regarding indications (where a positive sign is defined as injection and a negative sign as absorption at the respective Connection Point):

1. Minimum/Maximum active power injection/absorption capability (MW)
2. Minimum/Maximum reactive power injection/absorption capability (MVAr)

<!-- PAGE 268 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T14 – Additional Provisions for Storage Systems** | **Page 268**

***

3. Minimum/Maximum Available Energy (% of total)
4. Transformer tap position indication

In order to alter an indication value concerning the Registered Operational Characteristics of the Storage System, the User must inform TSOC in accordance with the provisions of the Transmission Rules.

### T14.11.3 Digital Signals

All Storage Systems must be capable of providing at least the following digital signals concerning indications:

1. Operating position of circuit breakers / disconnectors
2. Operating position of earthing switches
3. Operating position of the circuit breaker of any compensation equipment
4. Various fault and alarm signals
5. Storage System availability
6. Availability of remote Active/Reactive Power control

All Storage Systems must be capable of providing at least the following digital signals concerning active/reactive power control functions:

1. Control operating mode/status of LFSM-O / LFSM-U / FSM
2. Availability of remote FRR control (where applicable)
3. Reactive power operating mode/status (see T14.7.4)
4. Status of protection relays
5. Electricity Market participation indication (where applicable)

---

### T14.12 METERING ARRANGEMENTS

All provisions of T13 shall apply.

Additionally, in cases of Electricity Storage Installations with Integrated RES and RES Units with Integrated Storage, the User, in addition to the Meters installed pursuant to the provisions of T13, must install additional Meters to record data separately for the Storage System and the Wind Park or Photovoltaic Park (or other component) that form part of the Electricity Storage Installation or the RES Unit, in accordance with the terms to be specified in the Connection Agreement.

For these Meters, all provisions of the Metering Specifications and Procedures shall apply, unless otherwise specified in the Connection Agreement.

\<End of T14\>

<!-- PAGE 269 -->

**Version 1.1.0 | T14 – Additional Provisions for Storage Systems | Page 269**

# T14.A - APPENDIX TO CHAPTER "T14"

## T14.A1 Special requirements for the design of earthing systems in Battery Storage Systems

### T14.A1.1 
During the design of earthing systems for Battery Storage Systems, in addition to the requirements of T1, a detailed study is required, which shall be conducted in accordance with the standards specified in this Appendix, for the evaluation of AC and DC faults.

During the design, various factors must be taken into account, such as for example:

i. Whether the Storage System constitutes an Electricity Storage Facility without Integrated RES, or whether it constitutes a component of an Electricity Storage Facility with Integrated RES or a RES Unit with integrated Storage.

ii. In the latter two cases mentioned in point (i), whether the coupling of the Storage System with the Wind or PV Park (or other component) is "AC coupling" or "DC coupling".

iii. The method of connecting the battery containers in relation to the power converters and transformers (e.g., whether each battery container is connected to one power converter, whether the converter is inside the container, etc.).

### T14.A1.2 
For the evaluation of earthing systems in Battery Storage Systems, it is important to take into account AC and DC faults.

i. A comprehensive earthing study of the Storage System must evaluate faults occurring on the AC side of the facility or the grid.

ii. DC faults will be primarily confined within the Storage System Installation Area. However, the dynamics of DC faults differ significantly from the dynamics of AC faults. DC faults are described as transient faults, and the fault current contains a broad frequency spectrum, ranging from relatively low frequencies up to several million Hertz (MHz). Therefore, the impacts of DC faults must be evaluated through transient phenomena studies.

### T14.A1.3 Compliance with standards

#### T14.A1.3.1 Faults on the Alternating Current (AC) side
The earthing requirements for Storage Systems when examining faults on the AC side (at the MV or HV terminals) must comply with the requirements defined in standard "IEEE Guide for Safety in AC Substation Grounding," IEEE Std 80-2013.

<!-- PAGE 270 -->

TSOC may additionally request compliance with the relevant provisions of the following standards:

* CYS EN IEC 61936-1:2021, Power Installations exceeding 1 kV AC and 1,5 kV DC – Part 1: AC
* EN 50522:2022 – Earthing of power installations exceeding 1 kV a.c.

### T14.A1.3.2 Direct Current (DC) Side Faults

The earthing requirements for Storage Systems when considering DC side faults are defined by the Voltage level of the Storage System as follows:

* If the Voltage is less than 1500 V DC, earthing requirements shall comply with standards BS 7671 (IET Wiring Regulations) and BS 7430:2011+A1:2015 “Code of practice for protective earthing of electrical installations” (regarding the provision of additional earthing when the Storage System is intended to operate in islanded mode and/or when operating autonomously (off-grid)).
* If the Voltage is greater than 1500 V DC, earthing requirements shall comply with standard CYS EN IEC 61936-2:2023, Power Installations exceeding 1 kV AC and 1,5 kV DC – Part 2: DC.

### T14.A1.4 Lightning Protection

The lightning protection requirements for Storage Systems shall follow the EN 62305 series standards, specifically the following:

* EN 62305-1:2011: Protection against lightning – General principles
* EN 62305-2:2011: Protection against lightning – Risk management
* EN 62305-3:2011: Protection against lightning – Physical damage to structures and life hazard
* EN 62305-4:2011: Protection against lightning – Electrical and electronic systems within structures

### T14.A1.5 General and Operational Requirements

* The earthing system, its components, and interconnection conductors must be capable of distributing and discharging fault current without exceeding thermal and mechanical stress limits—based on the backup protection operating time (§10.2.2 – IEC 61936-2:2023).
* In general, AC and DC earthing systems must be interconnected (§10.2.2 – IEC 61936-2:2023).
* The earthing system must maintain its mechanical and electrochemical resistance for the expected design life of the DC installation, taking into account corrosion margins as well as other thermo-mechanical constraints (§10.2.2 – IEC 61936-2:2023).
* The operation of the earthing system must ensure that equipment damage due to excessive earth potential rise (EPR) is avoided.

---
| Version 1.1.0 | T14 – Additional Provisions for Storage Systems | Page 270 |
|---|---|---|

<!-- PAGE 271 -->

...of the earth, due to potential differences within the earthing system itself and due to high-magnitude currents flowing in conductors that are not appropriately sized to carry fault current. (§10.2.2 – IEC 61936-2:2023).

* The earthing system, in combination with appropriate measures (e.g., potential rise control, local isolation), must maintain safety limits for touch, step, and transferred voltages based on the requirements of the IEEE Std 80 standard.

<End of Annex T14>
