# T15 – DISPATCH INSTRUCTIONS

## T15.1 INTRODUCTION

**T15.1.1**  
In order for the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) to meet its statutory obligations in the context of the smooth and safe operation of the Electricity System and the Balancing Market, it is necessary to establish a structured procedure for issuing and notifying Dispatch Instructions to all Users who are required to respond to these Instructions.

**T15.1.2**  
T15 describes in detail the content and format of the Dispatch Instructions issued by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) within the framework of the Balancing Market, the provision of other Ancillary Services, and the safe operation of the Electricity System.

**T15.1.3**  
T15 also describes the procedures to be followed by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and Users for communicating regarding Dispatch Instructions. It is noted that T8 specifies the requirements and procedures for communication between the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and Users.

## T15.2 PURPOSE

**T15.2.1**  
The objective of T15 is to standardize the content, format, and method of notification of Dispatch Instructions issued by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) to Users.

## T15.3 SCOPE OF APPLICATION

**T15.3.1**  
T15 applies to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and to the following Users receiving Dispatch Instructions from the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC):  
(a) Producers (including those operating Black Start Units after a Total Shutdown and Contracted Units)  
(b) Independent Aggregators  
(c) Transmission System Owner  
(d) Storage Facility Operators  

## T15.4 CONTENT OF DISPATCH INSTRUCTIONS

**T15.4.1**  
A Dispatch Instruction issued by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) to a User may entail a change in Active Power generation/injection/offtake, a change in Reactive Power, or a Dispatch Instruction for the provision of an Ancillary Service, as well as a change in the operating mode of Generation / Demand Entities represented by the User.

**T15.4.2**  
A Dispatch Instruction issued by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) must contain a reference to the Instruction Type to which it belongs. The Instruction Type refers to the subject matter concerned by the Instruction, which may be one or more of the following:

* **Commitment Instruction (for Startup, Synchronization, or Desynchronization)**

<!-- PAGE 274 -->

**Edition 1.1.0** | **T15 – Instructions** | **Page 274**

* Dispatch Instruction (for Active Power Generation / Injection / Offtake)
* RES / CHP Curtailment Instruction
* Frequency Control Instruction (regarding reserves)
* Emergency Contingency Reserve Instruction (for provision of EAC)
* AGC Inclusion Instruction
* Automatic Generation Control Instruction (or AGC Instruction)
* Voltage Regulation Instruction
* Reactive Power Generation / Offtake Instruction
* Black Start Instruction following a Blackout
* Emergency Instruction
* General Type Instruction (type for any Instruction that does not belong to any of the above types)

**T15.4.3** When an Instruction to a Producer for the Synchronization of a specific Dispatchable Generating Unit does not include a reference to a specific Active Power Generation (MW), the Instruction is deemed to be to increase generation (after Synchronization) up to the level of Minimum Generation.

---

### T15.5 DISPATCH AND RECEIPT OF INSTRUCTIONS

**T15.5.1** An Instruction issued by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) to a User is dispatched using the Dispatch Information Management System as defined in the Electricity Market Rules. In the event that dispatching the Instruction using the Dispatch Information Management System is impossible, the Instruction may be given verbally via telephone or by electronic or other means, in accordance with the procedures described in T8.

**T15.5.2** For every Instruction, receipt must be formally confirmed immediately by the recipient, and in case of non-acceptance, the reason for non-acceptance must be communicated immediately. Non-acceptance of an Instruction may only be due to safety reasons (relating to personnel or Facilities) or because it is invalid according to the provisions of T15.5.3 and T15.5.4. For confirming or communicating non-acceptance of an Instruction, the same communication medium through which the User received the Instruction shall be used, as specified in T15.5.1.

**T15.5.3** For an Instruction to be valid, it must be consistent with the Availability limits, the capability to provide Ancillary Services, and the Registered Operational Characteristics of the Generation / Demand Entity to which it pertains, with the exception of the cases referenced in T15.8.

**T15.5.4** When the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) issues an Instruction to a User that is not consistent with the provisions of T15.5.3, the User must immediately point out the error to the Transmission System Operator.

**T15.5.5** In the case of a Producer who determines that a specific Dispatchable Generating Unit cannot be Synchronized within

<!-- PAGE 275 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T15 – Instructions** | **Page 275**

... period of 10 minutes relative to the Synchronization time, which was specified by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) via an Instruction, then the Producer must immediately inform the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) regarding the above deviation and state, according to their estimation, the new Synchronization time.

**T15.5.6** When, during the execution of an Instruction, an unforeseen problem arises due to safety reasons (related to personnel or Facilities), the User must inform the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) without delay.

**T15.5.7** It is very important that there are no misunderstandings regarding anything related to an Instruction given by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) to a User, as this may have financial implications. To ensure clarity in this communication, a specified wording format must be used, as described in the Annex of T15, by the following:
(a) Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), when issuing an Instruction  
(b) Users, when receiving an Instruction  
(c) Users, when submitting a Declaration of Change of Operational Characteristics.

---

### T15.6 INSTRUCTIONS FOR REACTIVE POWER

**T15.6.1** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may issue Instructions to Producers, Energy Storage Facility Operators, and Storage Representatives for the injection/absorption of Reactive Power (MVAr), taking into account their Synchronization status, in order to provide Voltage Regulation Ancillary Services in accordance with T5.

Regarding Energy Storage Facilities and RES Units, in the event of a discrepancy between the specifications defined in this part of the Transmission Rules and the corresponding specifications of Chapters T14 and T16, the provisions of T14 and T16 shall prevail.

**T15.6.2** The adjustment of Reactive Power (MVAr) loading of any Generation/Demand Entity receiving Instructions to achieve the target set by an Instruction must be performed without delay, in accordance with its Registered Operational Characteristics, within a tolerance margin of +/- 2% of the instructed value or +/- 4 MVAr, whichever is greater. The Reactive Power loading of a Generation/Demand Entity shall change, beyond the effect of the Automatic Voltage Regulator (AVR), only in execution of an Instruction from the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC).

**T15.6.3** Generation/Demand Entities that achieve the Reactive Power (MVAr) generation target set by an Instruction must allow the fluctuation of Reactive Power generation under the control of the Automatic Voltage Regulator (AVR), in accordance with their Registered Operational Characteristics, and must not maintain the same level of Reactive Power (MVAr) generation while the Transmission System Voltage varies.

**T15.6.4** In the majority of cases, Instructions for Reactive Power generation set new Reactive Power (MVAr) generation targets for Generation/Demand Entities.

<!-- PAGE 276 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T15 – Instructions** | **Page 276**

In certain cases, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may also issue Instructions to Generators or Storage Representatives for tap changing of the Transformers of Generating Units and Electricity Storage Facilities. These Instructions may concern the Simultaneous Tap Changing of a number of Transformers, in which case Users proceed with simultaneous changing of the winding ratios of the Transformers of Generating Units and the transformers of Electricity Storage Facilities in execution of a relevant Instruction from the TSOC. This Instruction, which is normally issued by the TSOC after prior notification, must be executed within one minute of its receipt.

**T15.6.5** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may also, in certain cases, issue Instructions to Generators, Storage Representatives, Storage Facility Operators, or to the Transmission System Owner for tap changing of inter-bus transformers. The Instructions for tap changing may concern the Simultaneous Tap Changing of a number of Transformers, in which case Users proceed with simultaneous changing of the winding ratios of the Transformers of Generating Units or the transformers of Electricity Storage Facilities in execution of a relevant Instruction from the TSOC. This Instruction, which is normally issued by the TSOC after prior notification, must be executed within one minute of its receipt.

**T15.6.6** Instructions related to Reactive Power may also set targets for Voltage levels that a Generating Unit or an Electricity Storage Facility must achieve in the Transmission System at the Connection Point with the Transmission System (or more generally for a User System at the Connection Point of Distributed Generation of the User System, in the case of a Distributed Generator, and specifically on the high-voltage side of the Generator's step-up transformer). Under normal operating conditions, once the voltage level target has been achieved, the User shall not perform any further transformer tap changes without prior communication and obtaining the consent of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC).

**T15.6.7** When, under certain operating conditions, such as low system Voltage, an Instruction is issued for maximum Reactive Power generation (MVAr), with Active Power generation/injection (MW) as set by a corresponding Instruction, the User must take appropriate measures to maximize Reactive Power generation (MVAr), unless constrained by the operational limits of the Facilities or by safety reasons related to personnel or Facilities.

**T15.6.8** When, under certain operating conditions, such as high system Voltage, an Instruction is issued for maximum Reactive Power absorption (MVAr), with Active Power generation/injection (MW) as set by a corresponding Instruction, the User must take appropriate measures to maximize Reactive Power absorption (MVAr), unless constrained by the operational limits of the Facilities or by safety reasons related to personnel or Facilities.

<!-- PAGE 277 -->

**Edition 1.1.0** | **T15 – Instructions**  
**Page 277**

---

**T15.6.9** When issuing Instructions concerning Active Power, any impact on Reactive Power generation capability must be taken into account. In certain cases, Instructions may be issued to reduce Active Power generation in order to increase Reactive Power generation capability.

**T15.6.10** For Generating Units, during excitation system operation, a constant terminal Voltage must be regulated with the reactive loading limiter in operation, to enforce loading restrictions when necessary. Operational control systems for constant Reactive Power or constant Power Factor must always be disabled unless otherwise agreed with the TSOC. In the event of a change in Voltage, the Producer shall not take any action to override the automatic response of the Automatic Voltage Regulator (AVR), which adjusts Reactive Power loading (MVAr) to maintain a constant terminal Voltage, unless a different Instruction is given by the TSOC or if necessary to avoid breaching stability limits, or if constraints exist due to the operational limits of the Facilities or for safety reasons related to personnel or Facilities.

**T15.6.11** When an Instruction concerning Reactive Power is issued, it shall be implemented without delay and must, provided it is issued in accordance with T15.5 and subject to T15.8, be executed no later than two minutes after receipt of the Instruction or within any longer time period specified in the Instruction by the TSOC.

**T15.6.12** When two Instructions concerning Active Power and Reactive Power, respectively, are issued simultaneously to a Generation/Demand Entity, and the execution of the Instruction concerning Reactive Power could cause the Generation/Demand Entity to operate outside its Registered Operating Characteristics, then the Instruction for Reactive Power shall be implemented with a delay so that the desired changes can be achieved without breaching operational limits. In any case, Instructions for Active Power and Reactive Power must be implemented as quickly as possible in accordance with the provisions specified in the Instructions.

**T15.6.13** In cases where the TSOC issues new Instructions concerning more than one Generating Unit at a Power Station or the generators of a Combined Cycle Generating Unit for the simultaneous tap changing of the respective transformers, the changing of transformer taps shall be executed by the Producer either simultaneously or progressively in steps, changing one tap at a time alternately among the transformers of the Generating Units or the generators of the Combined Cycle Generating Unit or inter-bus transformers of the Power Station, as applicable.

**T15.6.14** When Instructions require changing transformer taps by two positions for each Generating Unit of a Power Station, or for each generator of a Combined Cycle Generating Unit, or for inter-bus transformers, the two minutes following receipt of the Instructions (or any longer time period specified in the Instructions of the TSOC) may not be sufficient for

<!-- PAGE 278 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T15 – Instructions** | **Page 278**

---

...the execution of these Instructions. In this case, each of these Instructions must be executed as soon as possible.

**T15.6.15** Upon receipt of a new Instruction concerning Active Power, changing transformer tap positions to alter Reactive Power (MVAr) generation is not permitted, unless a new Instruction concerning Reactive Power is issued.

**T15.6.16** When an Instruction for Synchronization is issued or when a Dispatchable Generating Unit is Synchronized and receives an Instruction concerning its Active Power, an Instruction concerning its Reactive Power may also be issued that is compatible with the relevant parameters of the Dispatchable Generating Unit. When no Instruction concerning Reactive Power is issued alongside the Instruction for Synchronization, then at the moment of synchronization, Reactive Power (MVAr) generation must be zero.

**T15.6.17** When an Instruction for Desynchronization is issued, an Instruction concerning Reactive Power may also be given prior to achieving Desynchronization, which shall be compatible with the unit's impending Out-of-Service State. When no Instruction concerning Reactive Power is issued alongside the Instruction for Desynchronization, it is deemed that the Instruction for Desynchronization includes the requirement that Reactive Power (MVAr) generation must be zero at the moment of Desynchronization.

**T15.6.18** When required by Transmission System conditions, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may issue Instructions to achieve maximum Reactive Power (MVAr) generation in the shortest possible time. These Instructions must be executed no later than two minutes after their issuance, subject to the provisions of T15.6.14.

**T15.6.19** Upon receipt of an Instruction for Reactive Power generation, the User may take any action necessary to maintain the integrity of the Generation/Demand Entity, including requesting the revision of an Instruction, and shall communicate accordingly with the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) without delay.

**T15.6.20** When issuing Instructions for Reactive Power generation, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) must take into account Declarations regarding the capability to provide Ancillary Services and the Registered Operating Characteristics of the Generation/Demand Entity.

**T15.6.21** When, under abnormal Transmission System conditions, the operation of the Automatic Voltage Regulator (AVR) leads the Reactive Power loading of a Generation/Demand Entity to levels outside the limits specified in its Registered Operating Characteristics, the User shall immediately inform the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) of this situation. When the User reasonably believes that this situation may be hazardous to personnel or Facilities, they may take appropriate actions to mitigate the problem.

### T15.7 ALARMS

<!-- PAGE 279 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T15 – Instructions** | **Page 279**

---

**T15.7.1** When an Emergency State occurs or is highly likely to occur, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) shall inform Users accordingly using one of the designated means.

**T15.7.2** An Emergency State may be notified via a Red Alarm, Orange Alarm, or Blue Alarm, as well as by any other means agreed upon for each specific case (as described in T10).

**T15.7.3** Users shall respond without delay to any Alarm received, in accordance with the Dispatch Instructions and the provisions set forth in T10.

### T15.8 DISPATCH INSTRUCTIONS IN EMERGENCY STATES

**T15.8.1** To maintain the integrity of the Transmission System during Emergency States, the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) may issue Dispatch Instructions to Users for the operation of the Generation / Demand Entities they represent, outside the boundaries defined by their Registered Operating Characteristics. When TSOC issues such a Dispatch Instruction, it shall inform each recipient that the Dispatch Instructions are being issued within the framework of an Emergency State.

**T15.8.2** When the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) issues a Dispatch Instruction pursuant to T15.8 for the operation of Generation / Demand Entities represented by Users outside the boundaries defined by their Registered Operating Characteristics, each recipient must comply with this Instruction, provided that, in their judgment, the safety of personnel and their Facilities is not compromised.

### T15.9 NON-COMPLIANCE WITH DISPATCH INSTRUCTIONS

**T15.9.1** When a User cannot comply with a Dispatch Instruction properly issued by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), the User must inform TSOC accordingly by telephone and without delay. In such case, the User must submit a new Declaration of Change of Operating Characteristics and/or Declaration of Unavailability, through standard practice in accordance with the provisions of T8.3 and the Market Rules.

**T15.9.2** When the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) establishes that a User has failed to comply with a Dispatch Instruction, without having been previously informed by the User of their inability to comply with that specific Instruction, the provisions of T11.7.1 shall apply.

\<End of T15\>

<!-- PAGE 280 -->

**Issue 1.0.0 | T15 – Dispatch Instructions - Annexes**  
**Page 280**

---

### T15.A - ANNEX TO CHAPTER "DISPATCH INSTRUCTIONS"

#### T15.A1 FORM OF DISPATCH INSTRUCTIONS

**T15.A1.1** Every Dispatch Instruction must be, as far as possible, simple and drafted in accordance with T15.4 and the templates described below.

**T15.A1.2** In a Dispatch Instruction, all Ramp-Up and Ramp-Down Rates and all Loading Rates and Reduction Rates for Disconnection are considered constant and in accordance with the Registered Operating Characteristics of the corresponding Generation / Demand Entities. The TSOC is entitled to issue a Dispatch Instruction specifying a Rate of Change smaller than the maximum defined in the Declared Operating Characteristics of the Generation / Demand Entities if it deems it necessary.

**T15.A1.3** Upon placing into operation the Dispatch Information Management System to be installed pursuant to the Electricity Market Rules, the provisions of this Annex are repealed to the extent that they conflict with the operation of the Dispatch Information Management System. The provisions of this Annex which are not covered by the operation of the new Dispatch Information Management System or whose application is necessary until the completion of the development of the functionality of the Dispatch Information Management System continue to apply.

**T15.A1.4** Dispatch Instructions must normally include at least the following elements:
* (a) Exchange of operators' names.
* (b) The name of the User to whom the Dispatch Instruction relates.
* (c) The name of the specific Generation / Demand Entity to which the Dispatch Instruction relates.
* (d) The Type of the Dispatch Instruction.
* (e) The magnitude of the output (MW, MVAr, kV) for which the Dispatch Instruction is given (where applicable).
* (f) The start time of execution of the Dispatch Instruction, if it does not coincide with its issue time.
* (g) The specific time for achieving the target of the Dispatch Instruction.
* (h) The issue time of the Dispatch Instruction.

---

#### T15.A2 DISPATCH INSTRUCTIONS FOR INCREASING OR DECREASING GENERATION OR ENERGY ABSORPTION

**T15.A2.1** If the issue time of a Dispatch Instruction is 14:00 and concerns User 1, Generation / Demand Entity 1 for the purpose of changing the corresponding loading or output to 50 MW within 20 minutes (final loading or output value of 50 MW) at the declared Rate of Change, then this Dispatch Instruction must have the following form:  
`"<User 1>, <Entity 1> at 50 MW at 14:20, Instruction at 14:00"`.

**T15.A2.2** If the start time for the execution of the above Dispatch Instruction is 14:15, then the Dispatch Instruction must have the following form:  
`"<User 1>, <Entity 1>, at 50 MW, at 14:20, Instruction at 14:00, start time at 14:15"`.

---

#### T15.A3 OTHER DISPATCH INSTRUCTIONS

<!-- PAGE 281 -->

**Edition 1.0.0** | **T15 – Instructions - Appendices** | **Page 281**

---

### T15.A3.1 Synchronization of Dispatchable Generating Unit

#### T15.A3.1.1 
When issuing an Instruction for Synchronization to a Generator for a Dispatchable Generating Unit, the time required from the issuance of the Instruction until Synchronization must be taken into account, as declared to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) by the Generator. The Instruction must have the following form: "Unit <Unit 1> to Synchronize at 16:00, Instruction issued at 13:00".

#### T15.A3.1.2 
When an Instruction to a Generator for the Synchronization of a specific Dispatchable Generating Unit does not include a reference to a specific loading, it is deemed that immediately after Synchronization the Generating Unit shall increase its Active Power Generation up to the level of Minimum Generation, while at the moment of synchronization the Reactive Power (MVAr) generation must be zero. When the Generator reports that the Unit has Synchronized, further Instructions may be issued for its loading.

#### T15.A3.1.3 
When an Instruction to a Generator for the Synchronization of a specific Dispatchable Generating Unit is cancelled prior to the Synchronization of the unit, then the Instruction must have the following form: "<Generator 1>, <Unit 1>, cancellation of Instruction for Synchronization, Instruction issued at 14:00".

---

### T15.A3.2 Desynchronization of Dispatchable Generating Unit

#### T15.A3.2.1 
The Instruction normally must have the following form: "<Generator 1>, <Unit 1>, Instruction for Desynchronization, Instruction issued at 13:00". If the Instruction for Desynchronization must start being executed at 14:00, then it must have the following form: "<Generator 1>, <Unit 1>, commencement of shutdown procedure at 14:00, Instruction issued at 13:00". The two preceding cases presuppose that the Ramp Down Rate for Disconnection is in accordance with the Registered Operating Characteristics of the said Generating Unit. Otherwise, the Instruction must state a specific time at the end in the following form: "...and Desynchronization at 15:00". When a different Instruction regarding Reactive Power is not given together with the Instruction for Desynchronization, then it is deemed that the Reactive Power (MVAr) generation of the Dispatchable Generating Unit must be zero at the moment of Desynchronization. It is clarified that if, when an Instruction for Desynchronization is activated, the corresponding Generating Unit generates Power greater than Minimum Generation, the Generating Unit is obligated to reduce its generation to Minimum Generation in accordance with the declared Ramp Rate, and then continue the desynchronization procedure.

---

### T15.A3.3 Frequency Control

#### T15.A3.3.1 
All the above Instructions are deemed to be issued and executed in the Target Frequency environment. Specifically, when a Generation / Demand Entity operates providing Frequency Containment Reserve (FCR), then the relevant Instructions refer to the target loading at the Target Frequency. The Target Frequency is specified by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) in accordance with T1.8.2.2, is notified to Users, and must lie within the limits specified in the Connection Terms to the Transmission System.

<!-- PAGE 282 -->

**Issue 1.0.0** | **T15 – Commands - Annexes** | **Page 282**

---

### T15.A3.3.2
When a Generation / Demand Entity provides Frequency Containment Reserve (FCR), in accordance with the Ancillary Services Provision Obligations, the relevant Commands must specifically refer to the provision of the Frequency Containment Reserve (FCR) service and/or the Frequency Restoration Reserve (FRR) service, when required. These Commands must have, for example, the following form: 

> "<\User 1>, <\Entity 1> at 80 MW and provision of upward Frequency Containment Reserve of 5 MW, Command at 21:00".

---

### T15.A3.4 Restart after Total Blackout

#### T15.A3.4.1
Commands for restarting after total or partial blackout of the system, in accordance with the Ancillary Services Provision Obligations, must have, for example, the following form: 

> "<\Producer 1>, <\Unit 1>, start process for Restart after Total Blackout, Command at 19:00".

---

### T15.A3.5 Commands for Emergency State

#### T15A.3.5.1
Commands for Emergency State must always have the prefix: **"This is an Emergency State Command"**. Subsequently, they may have a predetermined format, such as the following: 

> "This is an Emergency State Command. Reduce production of <\Entity 1> to “X” MW in “Y” minutes, Command at 20:00".

---

### T15.A3.6 Commands for Voltage Control

#### T15.A3.6.1
Commands for Voltage Control may have different wording, depending on the means and magnitude to be regulated, so that Transmission System Voltages are maintained within acceptable limits while simultaneously maintaining Reactive Power reserves under normal conditions as well as under fault conditions on the Electrical System. These Commands must use, as appropriate, the following expressions:

* **(a)** "Increase/ Decrease (generation/injection or absorption) of Reactive Power to 20 MVAr".
* **(b)** "Maximum Reactive Power generation/injection (MVAr)".
* **(c)** "Maximum Reactive Power absorption (MVAr)".
* **(d)** "Increase by one tap step the position of the step-up transformer of the Generation Unit, or move to tap x of the step-up transformer".
* **(e)** For Simultaneous Tap Changes of a number of Transformers: "Change by one (or two) tap steps the windings of the step-up transformer of the Generation Unit or interbus transformer to increase or decrease accordingly the Voltage on the Transmission System, starting immediately after the issuance of the Command".
* **(f)** "Set the voltage to 140 kV and then allow it to fluctuate according to Transmission System conditions".
* **(g)** "Maintain the voltage at 140 kV until further Command. Change transformer taps as required".

In matters related to Commands for Voltage Control, the generation/output of Reactive Power (MVAr) of a Generation/Demand Entity is the export to the Transmission System, whereas the absorption of Reactive Power (MVAr) is the consumption from the System.

<!-- PAGE 283 -->

...Transmission. It is emphasized that excitation control systems for operation under constant **Reactive Power** or constant **Power Factor** must always be deactivated, unless otherwise agreed with the **Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC)**.

\<End of Appendix to T15\>

# TRANSMISSION RULES

## T16 - ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS FOR RENEWABLE ENERGY SOURCE (RES) STATIONS

### Version 1.1.01.0.0

# T16 - ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS FOR RENEWABLE ENERGY SOURCES (RES) POWER PLANTS

## T16.1 INTRODUCTION

T16:
(a) Contains provisions that apply to Wind Parks and are included in T16.4.
(b) Contains provisions that apply to Photovoltaic Parks and Solar Thermal Power Plants and are included in T16.5.
(c) Contains the modeling requirements for the systems of Producers from Renewable Energy Sources. These requirements are included in T16.8.
(d) T16 complements, without replacing, the existing provisions of the Transmission Rules, unless explicitly stated otherwise.
(e) It is noted that the Dispatch of Producers from Renewable Energy Sources is regulated by the relevant provisions of the Electricity Market Rules.

## T16.2 PURPOSE

The main purpose of T16 is to define the technical requirements and conditions that must be complied with by RES and High-Efficiency Cogeneration (CHP) Producers, for the RES Units they represent, as specified in the individual paragraphs of T16, with the objective of ensuring the safe and reliable operation of the Electricity System.

## T16.3 SCOPE OF APPLICATION

The provisions of T16 apply to the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and/or the Distribution System Operator (where applicable), and to the following Users:
(a) Transmission System Owner
(b) Distribution System Owner
(c) RES and CHP Producers, for the RES Units they represent, as specified in the individual paragraphs of T16.

## T16.4 TECHNICAL REQUIREMENTS FOR WIND PARKS

### T16.4.1 INTRODUCTION

In T16.4, the term "Wind Park" has the meaning of the User System of the RES and CHP Producer.

In cases where the Wind Park forms part of a Storage Installation with Integrated RES or a RES Unit with Integrated Storage, the requirements of T16.4 apply exclusively to the Wind Park (and not to the entire Storage Installation or the RES Unit) and refer to the

<!-- PAGE 286 -->

...maximum output power of the Wind Farm, unless specified otherwise.

At the Connection Point of the Storage Facility or RES Unit to the Transmission System (or the Distribution System), the requirements regarding all components of each Storage Facility or RES Unit must be cumulatively satisfied. T16.4 applies only to RES and CHP Producers operating Wind Farms or applying for connection of Wind Farms to the Transmission System or the Medium Voltage Distribution System, for which the Connection Offer is issued by the TSOC, taking into account the following:

a) For Wind Farms that are connected or applying for connection to the Medium Voltage Distribution System and for which a Connection Offer is issued by the TSOC, in addition to T16.4, all Articles of the Distribution Code referring to Distributed Generators or RES Producers shall apply. In the event of a conflict between T16.4 and these articles, the provisions of T16.4 shall prevail.

b) For Wind Farms that are connected or applying for connection to the Medium or Low Voltage Distribution System and for which a Connection Offer is issued by the DSO, only the Articles of the Distribution Code shall apply, with the exceptions stated below:
i) All Wind Farms connected to or applying for connection to the Low or Medium Voltage Distribution System for which a Connection Offer is issued by the DSO must comply with T16.6 regarding data provision.
ii) All Wind Farms connected to or applying for connection to the Medium Voltage Distribution System for which a Connection Offer is issued by the DSO must comply with T16.8.2 regarding modeling requirements.

In T16.4, all references to "Active Power", "Reactive Power", and "Power Factor" refer to the corresponding quantities measured by the TSOC or the DSO at the Connection Point, unless specified otherwise.

All technical requirements described in T16.4 aim to ensure, to the maximum extent possible, the safe and reliable operation of the Electrical Power System; therefore, all Wind Farms to which T16.4 applies must comply with its provisions.

### T16.4.2 MANDATORY OPERATIONAL CAPABILITY RANGE OF WIND FARMS FOR VOLTAGE AND FREQUENCY

**T16.4.2.1** Regarding the Transmission System or Distribution System Voltage, with respect to the mandatory operational capability of the Wind Farm, the following shall apply:

<!-- PAGE 287 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T16 - ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS FOR RES PLANTS** | **Page 287**

---

### (a) Power System Operation within the normal Voltage range

For Voltage variations that fluctuate within the envisaged normal operation range specified in the Transmission Rules (T1.8.3.1) for the Transmission System and in the Distribution Rules for the Distribution System, the wind turbines of the Wind Park must remain connected, in accordance with the requirements of Diagram 16.1. Diagram 16.1 illustrates the minimum times that the wind turbines of a Wind Park must remain synchronized (when the Voltage is within the defined normal limits), as a function of the Power System Frequency.

> [Technical Diagram Architecture Note: Diagram 16.1 - Mandatory Operating Capability of Wind Parks (within the normal operation Voltage range of the Power System) as a function of Frequency.]
> * **Y-Axis [Voltage V (kV)]:** Upper limits (12 / 24 / 145 kV), Nominal (11 / 22 / 132 kV), Lower limits (10 / 20 / 119 kV).
> * **X-Axis [Frequency f (Hz)] Operating Bands:**
>   * 47.0 Hz – 47.5 Hz: Minimum required operation time = **10 sec**
>   * 47.5 Hz – 49.5 Hz: Minimum required operation time = **60 min**
>   * 49.5 Hz – 50.5 Hz: **Continuous Operation** (*Συνεχής Λειτουργία*)
>   * 50.5 Hz – 51.0 Hz: Minimum required operation time = **60 min**
>   * 51.0 Hz – 52.0 Hz: Defined operation profile curve

---

### (b) Power System Operation under disturbance conditions causing Voltage dips

During Power System disturbances that cause Voltage dips, the response of the Wind Park during these Voltage dips is governed by the technical requirements described in T16.4.5 "Fault Ride Through Capability".

---

### (c) System Operation under Overvoltage conditions

In the event that an Overvoltage occurs in the Transmission System or the Distribution System, the wind turbines of the Wind Park must remain connected to the Power System for a minimum duration specified in Table 16.1, providing the response prescribed in T16.4.5.3. 

It is clarified that, in cases where the activation of Fast Fault Current injection according to T16.4.5.3 under Overvoltage conditions leads to the disconnection of the Wind Park wind turbines in a time shorter than that specified in this paragraph, the Fast Fault Current injection under Overvoltage conditions must remain deactivated for the range of Overvoltages specified in Table 16.1, unless different instructions are given by TSOC.

---

Points (a), (b), and (c) above are summarized in Table 16.1.

<!-- PAGE 288 -->

**Table 16.1: Mandatory Operational Capability of the Wind Park in relation to System Voltage**

| Transmission System or Medium Voltage Distribution System Voltage at the Connection Point<br>(Per unit - p.u.) | Minimum time period for which the Wind Park wind turbines must remain connected to the System |
|---|---|
| $U < 0.90$ | According to T16.4.5.1 |
| $0.90 \le U \le 1.10$ | According to Diagram 16.1 |
| $1.10 < U \le 1.20$ | 300 s |

***

### T16.4.2.2
With respect to System Frequency, the wind turbines of a Wind Park connected to the Transmission System or the Distribution System must satisfy the following:

**(a)** Remain continuously synchronized for Power System Frequency within the normal operating frequency range as defined in T1.8.2.1.

**(b)** Remain synchronized to the Power System in the event of a System Frequency variation between 47.5 Hz and 52.0 Hz for a duration of at least 60 minutes.

**(c)** Remain synchronized to the Power System in the event of a System Frequency variation between 47.0 Hz and 47.5 Hz for a duration of at least 10 seconds.

**(d)** Remain synchronized to the Power System in the event of a Rate of Change of Frequency (RoCoF) less than or equal to 1.0 Hz per second, as measured within a rolling time window of 500 ms. It is clarified that this requirement applies solely to the capability of the Wind Park's wind turbines and does not imply a requirement for the installation of Rate of Change of Frequency protection or specific protection settings. Protection requirements are described in T16.4.8.

---

### T16.4.3 MANDATORY REACTIVE POWER GENERATION/ABSORPTION CAPABILITY (MINIMUM OPERATIONAL CAPABILITY RANGE OF WIND PARK)

#### T16.4.3.1
The minimum required capability of the Wind Park for Reactive Power generation/absorption is determined by the Voltage-Power Factor and Active-Reactive Power diagrams, as described below. Diagram 16.2a specifies the minimum limits within which the Power Factor and Reactive Power can vary as a function of Voltage at the Connection Point, and Diagrams 2b and 2c as a function of Active Power.

All quantities in Diagrams 16.2a, 16.2b, and 16.2c are measured at the Connection Point.

***

| Version 1.1.0 | T16 - ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS FOR RES STATIONS | Page 288 |
|---|---|---|

<!-- PAGE 289 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T16 - ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS FOR RES STATIONS** | **Page 289**

It is clarified that the specifications defined in Diagrams 16.2b and 16.2c presuppose the operation of all wind turbines of the Wind Farm. The capability to provide Reactive Power is reduced based on the number of wind turbines currently in operation.

Under low wind resource conditions, the capability to provide Reactive Power is a function of the Wind Farm's wind turbines that are in operation. Consequently, the minimum Reactive Power provision obligation, as defined in Diagrams 16.2b and 16.2c, ceases to apply when the Active Power production of the Wind Farm is below 12% of its rated capacity.

**T16.4.3.2** In Diagram 16.2a, the non-hatched area where the Power Factor is greater than 0.95 refers to operation of the Wind Farm without the need to reduce Active Power; that is, at all points within this region, the Wind Farm must be capable of producing its rated power. In the hatched area, where the Power Factor ranges between 0.835 and 0.95, the Active Power of the Wind Farm shall be restricted under conditions in accordance with the provisions of Diagrams 16.2b and 16.2c.

**T16.4.3.3** The Wind Farm must be capable of operating at a minimum within the area enclosed by the polygonal line of Diagram 16.2b, with a lower Power Factor limit of 0.835 (inductive or capacitive).

> [Technical Diagram Architecture Note: Diagram 16.2a - Minimum Mandatory Operating Region of the Wind Farm for Voltage and Power Factor]

**Diagram 16.2a:** Minimum mandatory operating region of the Wind Farm for Voltage and Power Factor (minimum operational capability range). Voltage and Power Factor are measured at the Connection Point.

>

> - **Horizontal Axis (X-axis):** Reactive Power (%) (Percentage of Nominal Active Power) [-50% to +50%]

>   - Positive range: Reactive power production

> - Power Factor 0.835 (outer capability limits)

> 
> **Axes:**

**Diagram 16.2c:** Minimum mandatory operating area of the Wind Park for Active Power and Power Factor (minimum capability range). Active Power and Power Factor are measured at the Connection Point.

---

**T16.4.3.4** The minimum operating capability range of the Wind Park, as defined in Diagrams 16.2a, 16.2b, and 16.2c, shall...

<!-- PAGE 291 -->

**Edition 1.1.0** | **T16 - ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS FOR RES STATIONS** | **Page 291**

---

...demonstrated through the technical study referred to in T16.8.3. For the purposes of this Article, the technical study must include modeling of all equipment of the Wind Farm (e.g., wind turbines, internal Wind Farm cabling, MV/HV step-up transformer, reactive power compensation systems, etc.), in accordance with the requirements of TSOC based on T16.8.2. In the event that the Wind Farm has the capability for a wider operating range than that specified in Diagrams 16.2a, 16.2b, and 16.2c, it must provide this capability to TSOC, and the operating point/range of the Wind Farm shall be determined in accordance with T16.4.4.

**T16.4.3.5** The operating capability range of the Wind Farm (at the Connection Point) shall form part of the Preliminary Project Design Data and, subsequently, the Committed Project Design Data, in accordance with the provisions of the Transmission Rules.

**T16.4.3.6** TSOC may specify additional Reactive Power requirements in the Connection Agreement, if deemed necessary during the process of issuing the Connection Offer and Terms.

### T16.4.4 REQUIREMENTS FOR VOLTAGE, REACTIVE POWER, AND POWER FACTOR REGULATION OF THE WIND FARM

**T16.4.4.1** Wind Farms must have the capability to regulate their Voltage, Reactive Power, and Power Factor at the Connection Point, as described below, and must have received approval from TSOC.

Regarding Wind Farms with a Connection Point at HV, it is clarified that the MV/HV Step-Up Transformer connecting the Wind Farm to the Transmission System must satisfy the requirements specified in the Transmission Rules for Transformers connected to the Transmission System, in accordance also with the requirements of the Connection Agreement.

**T16.4.4.2** The Wind Farm must be capable of receiving Voltage Control Instructions from TSOC for operation:

(a) with Voltage regulation  
(b) with Reactive Power regulation  
(c) with Power Factor regulation  
(d) with Reactive Power regulation in relation to the Voltage at the Connection Point according to the characteristic curve of Diagram 16.3.

The regulation of each quantity refers to the Connection Point.

The Instructions referred to in T16.4.4 may concern either the operation of the Wind Farm or overall the operation of the Electricity Storage Facility with integrated RES or the RES Unit with integrated Electricity Storage. During the connection process stage, the relevant requirement that must be met by the Electricity Storage Facility or the RES Unit will be defined.

<!-- PAGE 292 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T16 - ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS FOR RES POWER PLANTS** | **Page 292**

---

> [Technical Diagram Architecture Note: Diagram 16.3: Voltage – Reactive Power Characteristic for Voltage Regulation at the Connection Point]
> - **Y-Axis:** Voltage at the Connection Point $V \text{ (kV)}$
> - **Voltage Setpoint Range:** $95\% < V_{\text{set}} < 105\%$
> - **X-Axis:** Capacitive Reactive Power (MVAr) $\leftarrow 0 \rightarrow$ Inductive Reactive Power (MVAr)
> - **Left Operating Limit:** Maximum reactive power absorption at nominal operation
> - **Right Operating Limit:** Maximum reactive power production at nominal operation

---

With reference to Diagram 16.3, the following are stated:

(a) The characteristics of the curve in Diagram 16.3 (the slope of the characteristic and the Voltage value corresponding to zero Reactive Power injection at its measurement point) shall be determined by the **TSOC**, taking into account the characteristics and grid requirements at the Connection Point, and shall be communicated to the RES and CHP Producer during the preparation stage of the technical study referred to in T16.8.3 or following the execution of the Connection Agreement.

(b) The characteristics of the curve may be modified according to the requirements of the **TSOC**, and the RES and CHP Producer is obliged to comply. Any change to the Voltage – Reactive Power characteristic must be notified by the **TSOC** to the RES and CHP Producer prior to its implementation in accordance with the provisions of T8.1.8.

(c) The implementation of the above characteristic curve shall take place upon request of the **TSOC**, based on the prevailing requirements of the Power System.

**T16.4.4.3** The Automatic Voltage Regulator (AVR) shall, through continuous variation of the Reactive Power of the Wind Park, control the regulated quantity (Voltage, Reactive Power, Power Factor) and satisfy the limits for induced Voltage changes in accordance with T16.4.7.1.

**T16.4.4.4** The Automatic Voltage Regulator (AVR) shall be capable of operating under any of the following options:

(a) Agreement of a specific regulation setting or a regulation setpoint schedule.

(b) Determination of the operating setpoint via real-time Command dispatched from the National Control Center (NCC).

<!-- PAGE 293 -->

**Edition 1.1.0** | **T16 - ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS FOR RES STATIONS** | **Page 293**

---

(c) In the event of loss of communication with the NECC, the Automatic Voltage Regulator shall automatically switch to Voltage regulation mode in accordance with the Voltage - Reactive Power characteristic curve of Diagram 16.3.

**T16.4.4.5** The Voltage control point is the Connection Point.

**T16.4.4.6** The Wind Farm must have the capability for automatic operation:  
(a) at a local level  
(b) by receiving real-time Commands from the NECC.  

In case of inability to receive the setpoint value or implementation of automatic control operation at a local level, Voltage regulation at the Connection Point shall be performed based on the Voltage - Reactive Power Characteristic logic, as given in Diagram 16.3.

**T16.4.4.7** The target value of the controlled quantity must be implemented by the Wind Farm within 30 s after receiving the relevant signal from the NECC. The response speed of the control system must be such that, following a step change in Voltage at the Connection Point, or in the Power Factor setpoint, or in the Reactive Power setpoint, the Reactive Power output of the Wind Farm reaches 90% of its steady-state value in a time shorter than 1 s.

**T16.4.4.8** The response speed of the Voltage control system when operating according to the Characteristic of Diagram 16.3 shall be proportional to that required during Command execution from the NECC as described in T16.4.4.6.

**T16.4.4.9** When the System characteristics at the Connection Point do not allow effective Voltage regulation at that point, then upon approval by the TSOC, no Voltage Control Command imposition shall be provided, and Voltage support shall be carried out according to the Voltage – Reactive Power Characteristic logic of Diagram 16.3.

**T16.4.4.10** The operation of any other additional/auxiliary Voltage support means, such as the operation of a reactive power compensation system (STATCOM, SVC), automatic switching of capacitor banks, operation of the step-up transformer on-load tap-changer (OLTC) system, etc., must operate harmoniously together with the Automatic Voltage Regulator of the Wind Farm. The proper operation of the additional/auxiliary Voltage support means must be proven by the technical study submitted by the RES and CHP Producer for approval to the TSOC in accordance with T16.8.3.

**T16.4.4.11** For concluding a Wind Farm Connection Agreement with the TSOC, RES and CHP Producers must submit to the TSOC:  
(a) Certificates proving that the wind turbines of the Wind Farm possess the technical capabilities described in T16.4.4. These certificates must have been issued by

<!-- PAGE 294 -->

**Issue 1.1.0 T16 - ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS FOR RES STATIONS**  
*Page 294*

---

accredited bodies for this purpose, in accordance with the provisions of T16.4.11.2.

(b) Technical study in accordance with T16.8.3.

In any case, the RES and CHP Producer bears the final responsibility in the event of non-compliance with the specifications defined in T16.4.4. TSOC retains the right to perform Tests to verify the compatibility of the Wind Park with the requirements of T16.4.4. The cost of the tests shall be borne by the RES Producer.

It is clarified that for the purpose of verifying compliance with T16.4.4.5 and T16.4.4.6, appropriate Tests shall be carried out by TSOC, in consultation with the RES Producer, during the activation stage of the Wind Park and the Equipment Inspection and Commissioning stage.

### T16.4.5 FAULT RIDE-THROUGH (FRT) CAPABILITY

#### T16.4.5.1 Voltage Profile for Fault Ride-Through Capability of Wind Turbines

The wind turbine generators of the Wind Park must ensure fault ride-through (under-voltage ride-through) capability, in accordance with Diagram 16.4.

Symmetrical or asymmetrical Voltage dips due to faults or disturbances of the Power System must not lead to instability or disconnection of the Wind Park when the phase Voltage at the Connection Point remains in the unshaded area above the solid black line of Diagram 16.4.

> [Technical Diagram Architecture Note: Diagram 16.4 - Voltage Curve for Fault Ride-Through Capability of Wind Turbines]
> - **Y-Axis:** Phase Voltage at Connection Point (%) [Values: 0%, 15%, 90%, 100%]
> - **X-Axis:** Time from fault onset (ms) [Values: 0 ms, 250 ms, 2000 ms]
> - **FRT Profile Characteristics:**
>   - $t = 0 \text{ ms}$ to $t = 250 \text{ ms}$: Voltage withstand limit at $15\%$ of nominal phase voltage.
>   - $t = 250 \text{ ms}$ to $t = 2000 \text{ ms}$: Linear voltage recovery curve from $15\%$ to $90\%$ of nominal phase voltage.
>   - **Unshaded Area (Above solid black line):** Mandatory continuous operation / fault ride-through zone (no disconnection permitted).
>   - **Shaded Area (Below solid black line):** "Area where disconnection of wind turbines is permitted".

**Diagram 16.4: Voltage Curve for Fault Ride-Through Capability of Wind Turbines.**

Disconnection of wind turbines is permitted in all cases for Voltage falling within the shaded area of Diagram 16.4. The...

<!-- PAGE 295 -->

Automatic re-synchronisation of wind turbines that disconnect is not permitted.

### T16.4.5.2 Active Power Restoration after Voltage Disturbance

Wind Farms that do not disconnect from the Power System during Voltage dips / faults shall continue to produce Active Power in proportion to the retained Voltage level.

It is understood that the produced Active Power cannot at any time exceed the Available Input Power.

### T16.4.5.3 Provision of Fast Fault Current for Voltage Support during Voltage Disturbances

Wind Farms shall support the Transmission System Voltage during Voltage Disturbances by providing Fast Fault Current with priority over Active Power. The provision of Fast Fault Current may be ensured by voltage deviation measurements either at the Connection Point or at the terminals of each wind turbine. In any case, for the measurement point, the normal Voltage operating range of the Connection Point shall apply.

Wind Farms connected to the Transmission System prior to the approval of the present edition of the Transmission Rules shall provide Fast Fault Current only to the extent permitted by their technology, while ensuring that no reduction of the active current magnitude is caused. It is clarified that such Wind Farms may refrain from providing Fast Fault Current if this is necessary to ensure the active current magnitude.

The provision of Fast Fault Current shall initiate within 20 ms and reach 90% of the expected value within 100 ms from the onset of the Voltage Disturbance. The variation of reactive current magnitude for Voltage support, increasing reactive current injection under Undervoltage conditions and increasing reactive current absorption under Overvoltage conditions, shall be at least proportional to the Voltage deviation from the normal operating limits. That is, the ratio $k$ of the change in reactive current magnitude to the Voltage deviation shall be greater than or equal to 1. The TSOC may request the Producer to alter the setting of the ratio $k$, provided that the technology of the Wind Farm's wind turbines permits it.

The provision of Fast Fault Current shall not lead to a reduction in active current magnitude. Consequently, the maximum injection of reactive current magnitude shall be limited, depending on the respective Active Power production level.

### T16.4.5.4

For the conclusion of a Connection Agreement for Wind Farms with the TSOC, RES and CHP Producers are required to submit to the TSOC:

(a) Type or Measurement Certificates, in accordance with the requirements of

TSOC, proving that the wind turbines of the Wind Farm possess the technical capabilities described in T16.4.5. These certificates must be issued by bodies accredited for this purpose, in accordance with the provisions of T16.4.11.2.

(b) Technical study in accordance with T16.8.3.

In any case, the RES and High-Efficiency Cogeneration (CHP) Producer bears ultimate responsibility in the event of non-compliance with the specifications defined in the provisions of T16.4.5. The TSOC retains the right to perform compliance testing to verify the conformity of the Wind Farm with the requirements of T16.4.5. The cost of such tests shall be borne by the RES Producer.

### T16.4.6 ACTIVE POWER GENERATION CONTROL

#### T16.4.6.1 
Each Wind Farm, for the purposes of the smooth operation of the Power System, must possess real-time Active Power control capability over the power it generates, through the following modes:

(a) via an Active Power curtailment Instruction sent in real time from the National Energy Control Center (NECC), as described in T16.4.6.3,

(b) in accordance with the Frequency response system, as described in T16.4.6.4,

(c) by executing an Instruction issued by the TSOC within the framework of the Balancing Market operation. The reading and execution of said Instruction is the responsibility of the RES and CHP Producer or the Independent Aggregator Entity representing the Wind Farm in the Electricity Market.

The Instructions referred to in T16.4.6 may pertain either to the power output of the Wind Farm or to the total power output of the Electricity Storage Facility or the RES Unit. During the grid connection process stage, the relevant requirement to be satisfied by the Electricity Storage Facility or the RES Unit shall be specified.

For Overfrequency conditions only, the Active Power setpoint determined by the Frequency response system shall override and take execution priority over the Active Power value resulting from the Active Power curtailment Instruction dispatched by the NECC.

#### T16.4.6.2 Active Power Ramp Rate at the Connection Point

##### T16.4.6.2.1 
Wind Farms must be capable of controlling the Ramp Rate of their generated Active Power at the Connection Point. For this purpose, they must feature a control mechanism for adjusting the Active Power output Ramp Rate to comply with the requirements specified below.

##### T16.4.6.2.3 
The Active Power Ramp Rate limit of Wind Farms at the Connection Point is specified as follows, with

<!-- PAGE 297 -->

**Version 1.1.0 | T16 - ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS FOR RES STATIONS**  
**Page 297**

---

...based on Table 16.2 below, which shall be applied following relevant instructions from TSOC:

### Table 16.2: Rate of Change Limits

| Wind Farm Capacity "P" | Average Rate of Change (per minute) over a 10-minute period | Average Rate of Change (per minute) over a 1-minute period |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **8 MW < P ≤ 20 MW** | 7.5% of capacity | 15% of capacity |
| **20 MW < P** | 3.5% of capacity | 7% of capacity |

Compliance with the above limits, particularly in the case of a sudden drop in wind speed, may be achieved with the contribution of a Storage System. It is understood that the provisions of T14.4.1.6 apply for operation under restrictions due to Available Energy.

***

#### **T16.4.6.2.4**
The Rate of Change limits of generated Active Power at the Connection Point must apply to all operational stages of the Wind Farm, including Start-up, normal operation, generation curtailment operation, shutdown, and stopping. However, during the activation of the Frequency response scheme described in Paragraph T16.4.6.4.2, an exception shall be permitted to exceed the limits specified in T16.4.6.2.3.

***

### **T16.4.6.3 Maximum Power Output and Instruction for Active Power Curtailment**

#### **T16.4.6.3.1**
The Wind Farm must have the capability to curtail and control its generated Active Power at the Connection Point, in order not to exceed the setpoint value transmitted by the National Control Center (NCC).

The NCC will transmit, based on communication protocols specified by TSOC, real-time Instructions to the Wind Farm.

#### **T16.4.6.3.2**
The Wind Farm must execute the above Instruction within 60 seconds from signal reception, and send an acknowledgment signal to the NCC confirming signal reception and execution of the Instruction. Detailed instructions regarding the operational mode of the Instruction and relevant signals will be provided in due course by TSOC.

#### **T16.4.6.3.3**
The RES and CHP Producer must submit to TSOC relevant certificates and information regarding the capability and execution method/time of the above Instruction (for the wind turbine model proposed for installation), in accordance with the standards specified in T16.4.11.2 or other International/European standards acceptable to TSOC.

#### **T16.4.6.3.4**
For the purposes of verifying compliance with T16.4.6.3, appropriate Tests shall be conducted by TSOC, in consultation with the RES Producer, during the commissioning stage of the Wind Farm and the Equipment Inspection and Acceptance stage.

#### **T16.4.6.3.5**
During operation under curtailment of generated Active Power, the...

<!-- PAGE 298 -->

**Version 1.1.0 | T16 - ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS FOR RES STATIONS**  
**Page 298**

---

The Active Power Rate of Change of the Wind Park shall not exceed the limits specified in T16.4.6.2.3.

### T16.4.6.4 Control of Generated Active Power of the Wind Park based on System Frequency.

**T16.4.6.4.1** The Wind Park shall be equipped with equipment (Frequency response system) allowing the regulation of the generated Active Power based on System Frequency, in accordance with Diagram 16.5.

The shape of the curve presented in Diagram 16.5 as well as the values of the parameters for Active Power and Frequency are indicative. TSOC shall specify the shape of the curve (e.g., linear, stepped, or other) and the values of the parameters defining the various points of the curve according to the requirements of the Power System, pursuant to T16.4.6.4.2.4.

**T16.4.6.4.2** The Frequency response system shall be capable of implementing the Frequency - generated Active Power curve of Diagram 16.5.

Operation in sections AB and BC, indicating Under-Frequency conditions and normal operation conditions respectively, shall be activated upon execution of an Instruction dispatched by NECC. Operation in section CD, indicating an Over-Frequency condition, shall be activated automatically by the control system of the Wind Park without requiring intervention from NECC.

Frequency measurement accuracy shall be at least 10 mHz.

---

> [Technical Diagram Architecture Note: Diagram 16.5: Frequency - Generated Active Power Curve]
> * **X-axis**: Frequency (Hz) [Indicative markers: $f_A$, $f_B$, $f_C$, $f_\Delta$, $f_{\Sigma T}$ across range 48 Hz – 51 Hz]
> * **Y-axis**: Active Power of Wind Park (% of available capacity) [Scale: 0% to 120%]
> * **Operating Characteristic Segments**:
>   * **Segment AB & BC**: Under-Frequency and Normal Operation regions (Activated via instruction from NECC).
>   * **Segment CD ($\Gamma\Delta$)**: Over-Frequency response region (Triggered automatically by local Wind Park control system).
> * **Legend/Note**: The shape of the curve and parameter values for Active Power and Frequency shall be specified by TSOC pursuant to T16.4.6.4.2.4.

---

<!-- PAGE 299 -->

| Version 1.1.0 | T16 - ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS FOR RES STATIONS | Page 299 |
|---|---|---|

**T16.4.6.4.2.1** Under normal System Frequency variations (i.e., during operation of the Electrical System within the normal Frequency range as defined in T1.8.2.1), the wind turbines of the Wind Farm shall operate with an Active Power output determined by segment BC. Points B and C may define a deadband of the Frequency-Active Power control characteristic where the Wind Farm shall operate at a constant percentage of available power. TSOC may request the capability to regulate the generation of the Wind Farm within this operating range as well.

**T16.4.6.4.2.2** If the System Frequency decreases below point B, then the Frequency response system shall be activated in order to increase the generated Active Power of the Wind Farm in accordance with segment BA of the Frequency-Active Power characteristic.

**T16.4.6.4.2.3** If the System Frequency increases beyond point C, then the Frequency response system shall act automatically so as to reduce the Active Power of the Wind Farm, in accordance with segment CDEST of the Frequency-Active Power characteristic.

**T16.4.6.4.2.4** As shown in Diagram 16.5, the Active Power generated by the Wind Farm shall be a function of the System Frequency and the current available Active Power of the Wind Farm. The shape of the curve and the parameter values for Active Power and Frequency shall be specified by TSOC during the signing stage of the Connection Agreement or, if this is not feasible, prior to or during the energisation stage of the Wind Farm. The shape of the curve and the values of the parameters may be modified according to the prevailing needs of the System, in which case the Wind Farm must comply. Any modification of the above parameters shall be communicated by TSOC to the RES and CHP Producer at least 2 weeks prior to its implementation.

**T16.4.6.4.3** For the conclusion of a Connection Agreement for Wind Farms with TSOC, RES and CHP Producers shall submit to TSOC:

(a) Certificates proving that the wind turbines of the Wind Farm possess the technical capabilities described in T16.4.6.4. These certificates must be issued for this purpose by accredited bodies, in accordance with the provisions of T16.4.11.2.

(b) A technical study in accordance with T16.8.3.

In any case, the RES and CHP Producer bears final responsibility in the event of non-compliance with the specifications defined in the provisions of T16.4.5. TSOC retains the right to carry out Tests to verify the compliance of the Wind Farm with the requirements of T16.4.6.4. The cost of the Tests shall be borne by the RES Producer.

**T16.4.7 IMPACT OF THE WIND FARM ON THE TRANSMISSION SYSTEM AND THE DISTRIBUTION SYSTEM**

**T16.4.7.1 Static Voltage Increase**

<!-- PAGE 300 -->

**Edition 1.1.0** | **T16 - ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS FOR RES STATIONS** | **Page 300**

---

The static (steady state) Voltage rise at the Connection Point or deeper in the Grid due to the connection/operation of the Wind Farm shall not exceed 2%. Under no circumstances shall the Voltage at the Connection Point or deeper in the grid exceed the Voltage limits for normal operating conditions. For Wind Farms connected to the Medium Voltage System, the corresponding relevant requirements of the Distribution Rules apply.

### T16.4.7.2 Short Circuit Level

The connection of the Wind Farm is expected to increase the short circuit levels of the grid at the Grid Substation and/or deeper in the grid. If after studies it is proven that due to the Wind Farm the fault levels in the grid have increased beyond the desired levels or beyond the short-circuit breaking capacity of the switching equipment, then the RES and CHP Producer, in full consultation with TSOC, must proceed with taking appropriate measures to limit fault levels. It is noted that short circuit levels for the Transmission System and for the Distribution System are specified in the Transmission Rules (T1.8.6) and the Distribution Rules, respectively.

### T16.4.7.3 Power Quality

Regarding the Power Quality of the wind turbines, the RES and CHP Producer shall ensure compliance with the EN 61400-21 standard as well as the EN 50160 standard and other relevant standards specified in the Transmission Rules and the Distribution Rules.

Rapid Voltage Changes at the Connection Point that may be caused by the connection or disconnection of a wind turbine shall not cause a violation of the limits specified in EN-61000-3-7.

Voltage Flicker at the Connection Point is specified in the Distribution Rules. Harmonic currents injected by the Wind Farm into the grid shall not cause Individual Harmonic Voltages at the Connection Point exceeding 1.5%, for harmonic orders from 1 to 50. The Total Harmonic Voltage Distortion at the Connection Point is set at 2%.

To monitor Power Quality at the Connection Point, the RES and CHP Producer is required to install a Power Quality recording system in accordance with the requirements of TSOC and the terms of the Connection Agreement.

### T16.4.7.4 Load Remote Control System

In the Transmission System and the Distribution System, a Ripple Control / Load Remote Control System (STIFOR) operates at 283.3 Hz. Its maximum Voltage amplitude is affected by the operation of capacitors and/or generators.

<!-- PAGE 301 -->

If, following an investigation, it is proven that the compensation system or other systems of the RES and CHP Producer (if installed) attenuate the signal of this system to unacceptable levels as defined by the TSOC, then the RES and CHP Producer shall be required to cover the cost of installing special equipment specified by the TSOC.

### T16.4.8 PROTECTION

**T16.4.8.1** The protection of the Wind Farm is the responsibility of the RES and CHP Producer and shall be implemented in accordance with the requirements of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) or the Distribution System Operator (DSO), as applicable. It is recommended that the RES and CHP Producer take precautions against disturbances occurring on the Transmission System and the Distribution System and install appropriate protection in accordance with T1.10.5.

**T16.4.8.2** In addition to any protection system of the Wind Farm installed by the RES Producer, the RES and CHP Producer must install protection systems such that the Wind Farm disconnects under abnormal grid operation conditions leading to a violation of Voltage or Frequency limits and/or Loss of Mains (LOM) and islanded operation of the Wind Farm. Clause T1.10.11.2 is relevant.

In cases of connections to the Transmission System or Medium Voltage Distribution System, a minimum protection system acting on the circuit breaker of the RES and CHP Producer shall be installed, comprising Overvoltage/Undervoltage, Overfrequency/Underfrequency, Overcurrent, Earth Fault, and Rate of Change of Frequency (RoCoF) relays.

**T16.4.8.3** Islanded operation of the Wind Farm—namely, the Wind Farm supplying an isolated portion of the Distribution System or Transmission System—is currently not permitted, and the RES and CHP Producer shall install appropriate protection to prevent island formation, taking into account the relevant provisions of the Distribution Code. The Wind Farm must disconnect automatically from the grid in cases of a fault on the Medium Voltage or High Voltage busbars of the Grid Substation or on the Connection Grid.

**T16.4.8.4** The settings of all protection relays (both in the Transmission System and Distribution System as well as in the User System) shall be configured such that their operation is compatible with the operational principles and practices of the existing protection system in the Transmission System and Distribution System, and must not be altered without the agreement of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) or the Distribution System Operator (DSO), as applicable. The settings of the RES and CHP Producer's relays at the Connection Point may be revised at any time in the future by the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) or the Distribution System Operator (DSO), taking into account the relevant provisions of the Distribution Code.

---

### T16.4.9 EARTHING ARRANGEMENTS AND DESIGN OF RES PRODUCER INSTALLATIONS

The Earthing of the RES and CHP Producer installation is the responsibility of the Producer and shall be designed in such a way as to comply with the relevant international specifications and the guidance of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC), pursuant to T1.7.2.1, for connections to the Transmission System, or the guidance of the Distribution System Operator, based also on the relevant provisions of the Distribution Code, for connections to the Distribution System.

The design of the Installations and Machinery of the RES and CHP Producer must satisfy the minimum specifications regarding insulation levels and safety clearances specified in T1.7.2.2.

### T16.4.10 METERING ARRANGEMENTS AND DATA TRANSMISSION

**T16.4.10.1** All provisions of T13 shall apply. Additionally, the RES and CHP Producer shall ensure that signals in digital format indicating wind speed and wind direction—as measured by anemometers or other specialized metering devices of the wind turbines—are transmitted to the metering location (or the Remote Terminal Unit [RTU] room), in accordance with the requirements of the Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC).

### T16.4.11 IMPORTANT TECHNICAL SPECIFICATIONS APPLICABLE TO INSTALLATIONS AND MACHINERY OF THE RES PRODUCER

**T16.4.11.1** Regarding technical specifications for the Installations and Machinery proposed to be installed by a RES and CHP Producer operating Wind Parks, T1.6 shall apply.

**T16.4.11.2** In addition to T16.4.7 above, regarding Wind Parks applying for connection to the Transmission System or the Distribution System, the RES and CHP Producer shall prove to the TSOC that all applicable International and/or European standards for the proposed Machinery and equipment are met, and shall provide the TSOC with all necessary data and information requested. Specifically, for wind turbines connected to the Transmission System or the Distribution System, the standards of the International Electrotechnical Commission (IEC) EN 61400 series must be met, as amended and applicable from time to time, and specifically standards EN 61400-1, EN 61400-21, and EN 61400-12-1 (as well as EN 61400-12-2 and EN 61400-12-3 upon publication), as amended and applicable from time to time. The relevant Type Certificates, Measurement Certificates, Certificates of Conformity, or other Certificates submitted by the RES and CHP Producer to the TSOC as proof of compliance with the aforementioned standards must be issued by an internationally recognized laboratory/body accredited by an approved accreditation body/organization for the specific scope of certification issued.

<!-- PAGE 303 -->

**Version 1.1.0** | **T16 - ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS FOR RES STATIONS** | **Page 303**

---

### T16.4.12 COMMUNICATION OF WIND FARM WITH THE NATIONAL ENERGY CONTROL CENTRE (NECC)

To achieve real-time monitoring and control of the Wind Farm by the NECC, signal exchange (indications, measurements, Commands, etc.) is required between the Grid Substation, the Entry Substation, and the NECC, in accordance with the provisions of T8. For the transmission of signals, the required infrastructure shall be installed, which shall include, among others, telecommunication equipment (power line carrier, optical fibers, leased lines, etc.), appropriate communication protocols with the NECC, Remote Terminal Units (RTUs), etc.

The required communication infrastructure as well as the local Telecontrol and Energy Management Systems (SCADA) of the Entry Substation and/or Grid Substation and the Wind Farm must support real-time information exchange with the NECC in accordance with the provisions of T8.2.5. The availability of all the above infrastructure elements and equipment must be extremely high and ensured through redundant systems and dual telecommunication routings (between the NECC and the Entry Substation and/or Grid Substation, and between the Entry Substation and/or Grid S/S and the Wind Farm), according to the instructions of the TSOC.

The required communication infrastructure as well as the local monitoring and control systems of the Wind Farm, as well as of the Entry Substation and/or the Grid Substation, shall be specified in the Connection Agreement to be executed between the TSOC/DSO and the RES Producer. The Connection Agreement shall also include an indicative list of the required signals to be exchanged in real time with the NECC. The final signal list shall be compiled by the TSOC at the initial project implementation stage.

Wind Farms are obligated to comply fully with the requirements of the TSOC, as these will be specified in the relevant Connection Agreement.

---

### T16.5 TECHNICAL REQUIREMENTS FOR PHOTOVOLTAIC PARKS AND SOLAR THERMAL POWER PLANTS

#### T16.5.1 INTRODUCTION

In T16.5, the terms "Photovoltaic Park" and "Solar Thermal Power Plant" have the meaning of the User System of the RES and CHP Producer concerning these specific Generating Stations.

T16.5 applies only to RES and CHP Producers who operate Photovoltaic Parks and Solar Thermal Power Plants or are applying for connection of Photovoltaic Parks or Solar Thermal Power Plants to the Transmission System or the Medium Voltage Distribution System, for which the Connection Offer is issued by the TSOC, taking into consideration the following:

<!-- PAGE 304 -->

| Version 1.1.0 | T16 - ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS FOR RES STATIONS | Page 304 |
| :--- | :--- | ---: |

***

(a) For Photovoltaic Parks and Solar Thermal Power Plants connected to or requesting connection to the Medium Voltage Distribution System and for which a Connection Offer is issued by the TSOC, all Articles of the Distribution Code referring to Distributed Generators or RES Generators apply, in addition to T16.5. In case of conflict between T16.5 and these articles, the provisions of T16.5 shall prevail.

(b) For Photovoltaic Parks and Solar Thermal Power Plants connected to or requesting connection to the Medium Voltage or Low Voltage Distribution System and for which a Connection Offer is issued by the DSO, only the Articles of the Distribution Code apply, with the exceptions mentioned below:

* **i)** All Photovoltaic Parks and Solar Thermal Power Plants connected to or requesting connection to the Low or Medium Voltage Distribution System and for which a Connection Offer is issued by the DSO, are required to apply T16.6 regarding data provision.
* **ii)** All Photovoltaic Parks and Solar Thermal Power Plants connected to or requesting connection to the Medium Voltage Distribution System and for which a Connection Offer is issued by the DSO, are required to apply T16.8.2 regarding modeling requirements.

All technical requirements described in T16.5 aim to ensure, to the maximum achievable extent, the safe and reliable operation of the Electrical System, and therefore all Photovoltaic Parks and Solar Thermal Power Plants to which T16.5 applies must comply with its provisions.

**T16.5.2** For Photovoltaic Parks and Solar Thermal Power Plants to which T16.5 applies, i.e., a Connection Offer is issued by the TSOC, all provisions of T16.4 shall apply. The TSOC may determine, during the design stage of the User Development Project and in accordance with the specific needs and particularities of the Electrical System at any given time, which of the requirements of T16.4 may not apply to the Photovoltaic Park or Solar Thermal Power Plant.

### T16.6 DATA PROVISION AND COLLECTION FOR RES POWER PLANTS

**T16.6.1** T16.6 applies only to RES Power Plants that are not directly connected to the Telecontrol and Energy Management System (EMS/SCADA) of the TSOC, in accordance with the provisions of T8.

**T16.6.2** The RES and CHP Producer must take all necessary measures and perform all required actions, in accordance with the guidelines of the DSO and the TSOC, so that the DSO is able to collect on its own servers, in real-time and by geographical area, aggregated generation data from RES Power Plants that are not directly connected to the EMS/SCADA, or to be able to perform an estimation of these data.

<!-- PAGE 305 -->

**Version 1.1.0 | T16 - ADDITIONAL PROVISIONS FOR RES POWER PLANTS**  
*Page 305*

---

The DSO shall provide every necessary facilitation to ensure that the TSOC has access to these data in real time, for the purposes of monitoring the operation of the Electricity System.

### T16.7 INSTRUCTIONS TO RENEWABLE ENERGY SOURCES POWER PLANTS

**T16.7.1** T16.7 applies to all Renewable Energy Sources Power Plants that are connected to the Transmission System or the Distribution System.

**T16.7.2** The issuance of Instructions to RES and CHP Producers is regulated by the relevant provisions of the Electricity Market Rules and the applicable Chapters of the Transmission and Distribution Rules.

**T16.7.3** The Transmission System Operator of Cyprus (TSOC) and/or the Distribution System Operator (DSO) have the right to curtail without restriction and at any time the active power injected into the Transmission System or the Distribution System generated by Renewable Energy Sources, if they deem that it is required by the operational conditions of the Electricity System, in order to ensure the safe and reliable operation of the Electricity System.

---

### T16.8 REQUIREMENTS FOR MODELING AND CONDUCT OF STUDIES

#### T16.8.1 INTRODUCTION

T16.8.2 applies to all RES Power Plants that are connected or are applying for connection to the Transmission System or the Medium Voltage Distribution System, for which a Connection Offer is issued either by the TSOC or by the DSO.

T16.8.3 applies to all RES Power Plants for which a Connection Offer is issued by the TSOC.

#### T16.8.2 Requirements for Modeling

Regarding requirements for modeling, the RES and CHP Producer shall satisfy T2.8.

#### T16.8.3 Requirements for the Conduct of Studies

Regarding the conduct of studies, the RES and CHP Producer shall conduct and submit for approval to the TSOC a technical study in accordance with the provisions specified in T2.4, aiming to demonstrate the compliance of the RES and CHP Producer with the requirements of the Transmission Rules and the Distribution Rules, as well as the level of impact of the RES Plant operation on the operation of the Electricity System.

The conduct of the study, and subsequently its evaluation and approval by the TSOC, shall take place during the design phase of the Development Project.

<!-- PAGE 306 -->

User, and prior to the signing of the Connection Agreement, so that any measures needed to satisfy the technical requirements of T16 and the remaining articles of the Transmission Rules are taken into account in a timely manner by all involved parties.

<End of T16>
